### Eligible API shapes and current protections

Only POST paths classified by `classifyTokenProxyPath` enter compression: suffix `/v1/messages`, `/chat/completions`, or `/responses`. Other paths remain passthrough. The route table maps `anthropic`, `openai`, and `chatgpt` to their existing upstreams; source inspection here does not certify live provider compatibility.

| API                | Candidate strings                                                                                                                | Delayed-mode protection                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Anthropic Messages | `messages[].content[]` blocks with `type: tool_result`, using string `content` or recognized text parts                          | Skip the last user message and result/text-part cache-control breakpoints.                                                                                     |
| Chat Completions   | `messages[]` entries with `role: tool`, using string `content` or recognized text parts                                          | Only results before the last assistant message; skip message/text-part cache-control breakpoints.                                                              |
| Responses          | `input[]` entries with `type: function_call_output` or `custom_tool_call_output`, using string `output` or recognized text parts | Only outputs before the last model item; assistant, reasoning and types ending in `_call` identify model items. Skip item/text-part cache-control breakpoints. |

`collectText` recognizes `text`, `input_text`, and `output_text` parts. Non-text parts are left untouched, while neighboring recognized text parts can still be candidates; this is not proof of complete multimodal understanding. Early mode lifts freshness and cache-control exclusions but still selects only tool-result text. Non-JSON bodies, unsupported shapes and undecodable selected strings produce no edits. System/user/assistant prose, tool declarations and linkage fields are not replacement targets.

### Frozen-cache and transport invariants

- `resolveBlock` skips strings below 1,000 estimated tokens before hashing. Larger strings are queued by SHA-256 of their decoded text's UTF-8 bytes. The first cached `kept` or `compressed` verdict is reused without re-running compressors. The queue serializes decisions within the process; `CompressionCache.write` itself is not a cross-process first-writer lock.
- `decideBlock` requires a reduction of at least 10% in string length with the recovery marker included. It uses existing heuristics, then the optional `token-proxy` task for output still at least 8,000 estimated tokens. `summarizeToolResult` caps input at 24,000 estimated tokens, chunks it, and abandons a partial failed summary. No model task was invoked during inspection.
- Compressed entries live under `proxy-cache/<first-two-hash-characters>/` as original text, replacement text and a version-1 JSON verdict. Texts are written first and the record last through the existing atomic writers; only then may the replacement be used. Kept entries store only the verdict. Memory eviction does not remove disk records; there is no disk pruning path.
- Cache read/write filesystem failures leave the block unchanged. Unexpected compressor errors reach the server's fallback, which forwards the original body. Invalid records, different record versions and missing replacement files currently read as absent, so future work must preserve compatible version-1 records and legacy compressor IDs. Missing original text can still yield a compressed verdict; recovery reports that original as unavailable.
- The request buffer and decoded output are capped at 64 MiB. Oversized uploads switch to streaming passthrough. Decoding supports gzip, deflate, Brotli and zstd when the runtime provides it, with strict UTF-8 handling. Unchanged requests keep their received bytes and encoding; changed bodies receive corrected length/encoding headers. Responses and upstream error bodies are streamed unchanged; mid-stream upstream failure closes the client response.
- Failure/recovery eligibility guards do not exist yet. Explicit tool failure can therefore reach `resolveBlock` today. The next safeguards must run before cache lookup without changing stored verdicts; this inventory does not claim those safeguards are implemented.

## 01.3: Failure and original-recovery vetoes

Execution date: 2026-11-06. Status: complete for the scoped implementation and static checks. Selected the first unchecked task, 01.3, in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-01.md`; 01.1 and 01.2 were checked. Confirmed `git branch --show-current` = `acappella` and `git rev-parse HEAD` = `cc40d97423155e2b6f33ebf9e2874489b9bd7c77` before edits. The two tracked dirty files were the existing 01.2 changes in `proxy/compress.ts` and its regression owner; the 181 pre-existing untracked files were inventoried by SHA-256. No staged changes existed.

Re-read the execution contract, analysis, ledger and matching repository/utility/test guidance. Reused the existing descriptor linkage, `eligibleSpans`, `memberOf`, `stringValue`, `getBasename`, cache/options fixtures and cross-API format table. The existing private custom-args splitters do not reject incomplete quotes or shell operators, so they cannot establish a literal recovery invocation. The agent-error registry classifies provider/process errors, not tool-result protocol status, and was not repurposed as a prose failure detector.

- `src/main/local-models/proxy/compress.ts`: `compressRequestBody` now checks eligible descriptors before hashing, cache reads/writes, heuristics or model summarization. Explicit `failureStatus = failed` vetoes every selected text part, even without a linked call. A recognized original-recovery call supplies the other veto. Failed results take precedence when both apply. Vetoes never replace or overwrite a frozen verdict. The exported `findToolResultSpans` selection contract is unchanged.
- `literalCommandTokens` reads one literal command without execution or expansion; `isOriginalRecovery` requires a uniquely linked, named structured shell call. It recognizes `Bash`/`bash`, `shell`, `shell_command` and `exec_command` (also the `functions.` namespace), string commands and string-only argv, plus one `sh`/`bash`/`zsh` `-c`/`-lc` wrapper. The CLI must be `maestro-cli` (including `.exe`/`.cmd`) or `node`/`node.exe` invoking a `maestro-cli.js` basename, followed by exact `models proxy block`, a 64-digit hexadecimal hash and a standalone `--original` option. Quoted/escaped paths, `--json`, and literal numeric or colon-range options are recognized. Range flag names are not prescribed here; actual range validation remains phase 03.
- Fresh-result and cache-control selection still comes from the existing mode-specific descriptor walker. Optional request-local `stats.eligibilityVetoes` counts failed/recovery text spans only after those gates; `candidates` excludes vetoed spans. These counters travel with the existing compression stats, but persistent aggregation/UI work remains phase 06. No archive, dependency, permission, setting, public bridge or new error-pattern bank was added.
- `src/__tests__/main/local-models/proxy/compress.test.ts`: authored eight cross-API regression families for successful-then-failed identical content, a mixed successful/failed request, unlinked multimodal failed results with an unusable cache, unknown/unsupported failure status, original recovery in both modes, exact command recognition versus mentions/compound syntax, missing/ambiguous linkage and freshness/cache-control/failure-priority behavior. Cache spies assert that vetoes do not read/write; frozen verdicts are compared through a reloaded cache; later successful copies must retain their prior bytes. A raw body with whitespace and an integer beyond 2^53 must remain identical. These tests were not executed.

Exceeding a scan/evidence bound keeps the original plain text. `compressPlainText` requires a shorter result including all omission notes; the unchanged `decideBlock` still requires at least 10% reduction including the recovery marker. The implementation change is one production file, 72 inserted/six removed lines. Existing compressor IDs, immutable verdicts and write-before-use remain unchanged.

Assertions name both omission regions and repeat counts. `src/__tests__/main/local-models/proxy/compress.test.ts`: added a boundary fixture where line reduction alone clears 10% but the recovery marker prevents acceptance; the exact request must be retained and a kept verdict stored. These tests were not executed.

- `src/main/local-models/proxy/compressors.ts`: `compressCommandOutput` prepares content-only candidates with `git-status`, `git-log` or `search` labels. Scans stop at 262,144 UTF-16 code units / 10,000 lines; C0/C1 terminal controls, NUL records and carriage returns return no candidate. - `compressSearchRecords` groups filename prefixes and preserves exact numeric/text suffixes, explicitly counting output records rather than match occurrences. It retains a sample from every file and all small groups, diagnostic/identifier evidence and suffixes ambiguous with columns or colon-bearing filenames; every sampled gap has a count. Diagnostic prefixes resembling filenames veto this reducer. `sampleCommandLines` is shared with Git-message sampling.

`src/__tests__/main/local-models/proxy/compressors.test.ts` contains independent fixtures for status classes/renames/spaced and Unicode paths, exact commit/merge hashes and diagnostics, custom Git layouts, line/column/numeric-snippet ambiguity, per-file counts, unique middle-file hits, omission-region counts, NUL/control data, mixed output, evidence/scan limits, marker overhead and deterministic calls. ## 03.1: Bounded original line recovery

Execution date: 2026-11-06. Status: complete for the scoped implementation, authored regressions and static checks. Selected the first unchecked task, 03.1, in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-03.md`; phase 02 was checked. Before edits, `git branch --show-current` returned `acappella` and `git rev-parse HEAD` returned `cc40d97423155e2b6f33ebf9e2874489b9bd7c77`. Captured the eight pre-existing dirty tracked files, scoped source/test contents, starting patch and SHA-256 inventory of 181 untracked files under ignored `.maestro/scratch/token-optimization-03-1-20261106/`. Nothing was staged.

Read the execution contract, analysis, ledger, repository guidance, IPC-PATTERNS, utility/storage/test guides and canonical token-proxy entry. Reused `isContentHash`, the existing cache root/entry path, original file, verdict reader and `readTokenProxyBlock`. The existing JSONL reader drops blank lines and normalizes line boundaries, so it is unsuitable for exact original excerpts. No new archive, dependency, filesystem discovery or retention policy was introduced.

- `src/shared/local-models/token-proxy.ts`: added `TokenProxyOriginalLineRange`, `validateTokenProxyOriginalLineRange`, range result/provenance types and optional `TokenProxyBlockDetail.originalRange`. Bounds require only own `startLine`/`endLine` properties, positive safe integers, inclusive and ascending, at most 1,000 lines. The output cap is 262,144 UTF-8 bytes. LF defines a line; CRLF and lone CR remain exact; a trailing LF adds no empty line.
- `src/main/local-models/proxy/compression-cache.ts`: overloaded the existing `readOriginal(hash)` with a typed range argument, preserving full-read behavior. Ranges read that same `.original.txt`, scan at most 64 MiB in 64 KiB buffers, assemble UTF-8 safely across buffer boundaries and close the handle in `finally`. Output/scan limits throw instead of truncating a line; other read failures propagate. Missing originals remain null. Recovery does not write originals or verdicts.
- `src/main/local-models/proxy/service.ts`: `readTokenProxyBlock(hash, range?)` validates before cache access. Full-block callers keep their result shape. Range results carry the excerpt, archive hash/source, requested/returned lines, `complete` for the requested interval and `hasMore` for subsequent original bytes. Missing verdicts return null; missing originals yield `original: null` and `originalRange: null`. Replacement text and token totals still describe the full frozen block.
- Regression artifacts updated, never executed: `compression-cache.test.ts` covers exact line endings/Unicode/blank/lone-CR text, reload/verdict/original preservation, EOF/empty/absent lines, buffer boundaries, skipped oversized lines, malformed/inherited/extra bounds, exact line/byte caps, scan limits, missing/kept originals, invalid hashes and failed reads with closure. `service.test.ts` covers delegation/provenance, missing-original versus missing/kept-verdict behavior, validation before lookup, failure propagation and full-block compatibility. Existing fixtures and filesystem mock seams were reused.

- `src/shared/local-models/token-proxy.ts`: an optional `/agent/<id>/launch/<64 lowercase hex characters>` prefix carries an opaque binding selector. `isTokenProxyLaunchBinding` requires exactly 64 literal characters, including rejecting trailing line terminators. `resolveTokenProxyTarget` strips the prefix without normalizing provider path/query bytes; parsing grants no capability. The existing gateway builder accepts an explicit optional token, refuses cross-agent rebinding and leaves legacy three-argument behavior intact. Legacy targets retain their previous shape.
- `src/main/local-models/proxy/service.ts`: `registerTokenProxyRecoveryLaunch` is a main-process-only preparation seam with no production caller. It requires an already running proxy, known provider base route, same archive data-directory namespace and bounded supplied local evidence. It rejects remote/unknown kinds, other gateways, credential-bearing/non-HTTP URLs, queries/fragments and already bound/cross-agent routes. A maximum of 256 active records retains copied, frozen evidence with 2,048-character path bounds; capacity exhaustion refuses registration without evicting other launches. Tokens are random, process-local and unpublished in service logs/state. Explicit release is idempotent; stop/configuration replacement revoke all records, configuration generations prevent queued registration revival, and observed archive-namespace mismatch permanently revokes that record. Lookup requires matching token, agent and provider route and returns an independent evidence copy. Neither stored SSH settings nor agent identity supplies recovery evidence.
- `src/main/local-models/proxy/server.ts`: `recoveryFor` resolves host evidence only for a bound message request on an explicitly selected compression mode, after body buffering and before calling the compressor. Unknown/revoked/mismatched bindings supply no `recovery`; body/header/query assertions cannot supply it. Resolver failure uses the existing exact-original forwarding path. Verified evidence reaches the prepared compressor through its existing optional input. Provider paths/query strings, per-request modes, upstream response streaming and background-routing behavior retain their existing owners. Successful request logs omit the binding prefix and capability contents.
- Regression artifacts updated, never executed: `shared/local-models-token-proxy.test.ts` covers strict tokens, malformed/unscoped prefixes, raw path/query preservation, legacy shapes and single same-agent binding across all routes. `server.test.ts` covers both compression modes and all provider routes, unchanged request/error-response bytes, absent/client-asserted/unknown capabilities, passthrough/non-message exclusions, resolver failure and revocation during a partial upload. `service.test.ts` covers distinct opaque tokens, evidence-copy isolation and compressor forwarding, legacy/current-settings exclusion, cross-agent/cross-route/unknown/stale bindings, unavailable listeners, rejected namespaces, all base routes, capacity/release behavior, stop/restart/configuration invalidation, queued registration and archive-namespace changes.

## 03.4b3b: Unchanged protocol and remaining proof candidates

Execution date: 2026-11-06. **Incomplete under the task's explicit acceptance rule: actual tool-environment proof remains unavailable.** Selected the first unchecked task, 03.4b3b; dependent 03.4b3c also remains unchecked. Branch/HEAD is `acappella` / `cc40d97423155e2b6f33ebf9e2874489b9bd7c77`, with empty staging. No production code or regression fixtures changed.

After reading the generation help, `codex --version` returned `codex-cli 0.167.0` and `codex app-server generate-json-schema --experimental --out .maestro/scratch/token-optimization-03-4b3b-proof-audit-20261106T214014/schema` exited 0. All 470 generated JSON schemas are byte-identical to the prior recheck, with none added or removed. This generates descriptions only; no provider, listener or tool execution was started. Additional inspected candidates do not close the dependency:

- `EnvironmentStatusResponse` explicitly includes local environments in `ready`; connection status does not establish tool sandbox or archive visibility. `EnvironmentConnectionNotification` carries only thread/environment IDs.
- `CommandExecutionRequestApprovalParams` can identify an environment and requested extra permissions. `ThreadItem` command-execution records expose command, cwd, output/status and optional process information, without the effective environment or archive/bridge access proof. Approval or a successful unrelated command cannot authorize recovery.
- `AttestationGenerateResponse` describes an opaque **client** attestation token, without a tool-environment proof contract. The canonical `src/shared/maestro-lib/codex/server-requests.ts` returns method-not-found for this request; it supplies no recovery attestation.

## 03.4b3b and 03.4b3c: superseded by model-side retrieval

Execution date: 2026-11-11. Tasks 03.4b3b (proof of the actual launch's tool execution environment) and 03.4b3c (emitting an executable shell recovery hint in the compression marker) are superseded by the `2026-11-11-Token-Proxy-Retrieval` playbook (R02.1 to R02.7, branch `feat/token-proxy-retrieval`). They are not to be resumed.

Why: the shell hint could only be emitted once the proxy knew how the actual provider launch runs a shell command (effective shell, a reachable `maestro-cli`, archive visibility), and the sections above show that proof was never complete. The replacement gives the model a tool it calls by name, `retrieve_compressed_original`, served by the per-launch MCP bridge Maestro already injects (`maestro-cli mcp serve`) and answered through `readTokenProxyBlock()`. A tool call needs no proof of the launch's shell. The proxy learns that a launch has the tool from the host-issued launch binding (`registerTokenProxyRecoveryLaunch()` with `{ kind: 'mcp-retrieve-tool' }`), never from the request body, and only then lets `compress-early` use the lossy `full` profile. The marker text is unchanged, so no stored verdict changes bytes, and the shell-command hint stays unemitted.

Design, decisions and the one real-model result (R02.6): "Retrieval: the model asks for an original" in `docs/architecture/local-models.md`. User docs: "The Maestro proxy" in `docs/local-models.md`.

Read the execution contract, analysis, this ledger, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/agent-guides/SHARED-UTILS.md` and the canonical-utilities index. Reused `findToolResultDescriptors`/`ToolResultDescriptor` (the only input path), `memberOf`/`stringValue`/`isNullSpan`, `contentHash`, `parseCompressionMarker`, `normalizeWorktreePath`/`isPathAtOrUnderRoot`, `isAbsolutePath`, `TokenProxyOriginalLineRange` (the inclusive 1-based range type; its 1,000-line validator does not fit a file read and is not used) and `TokenProxyRequestKind`. The existing `diff` dependency is not needed here: pair eligibility is identity, range and hash equality, and the delta computation belongs to 04.2.

- `src/main/local-models/proxy/read-opportunities.ts` (new): `readEventsFromDescriptors()` turns the read-tool results of `findToolResultDescriptors()` into `ReadEvent`s (path, `offset`, `limit`, decoded text, context, epoch); `analyzeReadOpportunities()` relates them into a `ReadOpportunityReport` of `first` / `repeat` (`identical` | `changed`, both `contentHash()` values kept) / `unsupported` findings with counts, hashes and sizes and no text. `READ_OPPORTUNITY_LIMITS` (256 reads, 1,048,576-character literal per result, 8,388,608 decoded characters, plus a 4,096-character path bound) is explicit and lowerable per call. A pair needs the same read tool, canonical absolute path, `offset`/`limit` as sent, context, epoch and printed first line number; the nearest earlier match is the baseline. Unsupported reasons: `ambiguous-tool-id`, `incremental-history` (`previous_response_id`, `conversation`), `managed-context` (`context_management`), `over-budget`, `failed-read`, `unsupported-parameters`, `unresolved-path`, `outside-workspace`, `non-text-content`, `too-large`, `compressed-text`, `binary-looking`, `unknown-numbering`, `partial-range`, `reset-between` (a `compaction` item), `other-context`. It reads no file, opens no cache, keeps nothing between calls and edits no request.
- `src/shared/fileKinds.ts`: `isBinaryContent()` moved here from the renderer's `filePreviewUtils.ts` (a main-process module cannot import renderer code); the function body is unchanged and the 8,192-character window is now a named constant. `filePreviewUtils.ts` and `FilePreview.tsx` import it from the shared file. `src/shared/toolActivityLabel.ts`: `normalizeToolName()` is exported (no behavior change) so the analyzer recognizes read tools by the provider-spelling-insensitive key the activity feed already uses.
- `CLAUDE.md` index and `docs/agent-guides/CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md`: three matching entries (`isBinaryContent`, `normalizeToolName`, the read analyzer).

Bounds: per-result, read count, total characters, other tools not counted. Identity and history: shared ids in all three APIs, `previous_response_id`, `conversation`, null continuation fields, `context_management`. Resets and contexts: `compaction` item in Messages and Responses, a baseline reached across it refused, reads after it related, a subagent context and a separate subagent request, a Task result not treated as a read. Contract: deep-frozen inputs, deterministic output, `getCompressionCache`/`createCompressionCache` never called, no file text in a report, counts by status and reason. `fileKinds.test.ts` gained the nine `isBinaryContent` cases (moved from the renderer test, plus the late-NUL, C1/DEL and sample-window cases); `toolActivityLabel.test.ts` gained two `normalizeToolName` cases.

### Unresolved limits

- The read dialect list is deliberately small: tools named `read` / `read_file` with one path key (`file_path`, `filePath`, `path`, `absolute_path`) plus `offset` and `limit`. Shell reads (`cat`, `sed -n`), Copilot `view` and Antigravity `view_file` are not analyzed. Only the arrow/tab `cat -n` numbering is recognized; any other numbered format reads as plain text unless it mixes numbered and unnumbered lines (then `unknown-numbering`).
- Path identity is string identity after `normalizeWorktreePath`: symlinks, case folding and drive-letter case are not resolved, which can only miss a pair. Relative paths are refused. The caller must supply workspace roots; an empty list analyzes nothing.
- Resets are detected only from `compaction` items. A client that rewrites its own history (for example replacing old results with a placeholder) shows up as `unknown-numbering` or a plain-text `changed` pair, not as a reset.
- The proxy cannot tell a subagent or tab from its parent, so the descriptor path supplies one context per request. The `context` field exists for a caller that can prove more.
- No production code calls the analyzer yet; exposure through the report and inspection model is 04.4, and previews are 04.2. Nothing here measures realized savings or confirms what a model retained. Exercised with synthetic bodies only; no provider workload was run.

## 04.2: Delta and identical-output previews

Execution date: 2026-11-11. Status: complete, observation only. Selected the first unchecked task in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-04.md`. Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`, HEAD `560ecc325a`, tracked tree clean before edits; `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` untouched. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin` (the default shell has `v26.12.1`).

Read the execution contract, the analysis (T08, T09, T11 and "Immutable cache and identity design"), this ledger, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/agent-guides/SHARED-UTILS.md` and the canonical-utilities index. Reused: the analyzer's `repeat` findings and `ReadVersion` (they identify the source and target versions and carry both hashes), the events' text, `contentHash`, `MIN_SAVING_RATIO` (the compressor's own "at least 10% smaller, marker included" bar), `estimateTokenCount`, `findToolResultDescriptors`/`ToolResultDescriptor`/`stringValue` (for delivery evidence), and the existing `diff` dependency (`structuredPatch`, `formatPatch`, `OMIT_HEADERS`). Looked at and not reused: `countLineChanges()` bounds its diff with a wall-clock `timeout` (not deterministic) and returns counts, not hunks; `buildSyntheticGitDiff()` and `<TextDiff>` are renderer code with no work bound; the CLI's `createPatch` call has neither a bound nor a position shift.

- `src/main/local-models/proxy/read-previews.ts` (new, 327 code lines): `buildReadPreviews({ report, events, deliveries, limits? })` returns a `ReadPreviewReport` (`mode: 'preview'`, `note`, `complete`, the effective `limits`, `previews`, `counts`). Every `repeat` finding gets exactly one entry, in report order: a `preview` (`kind` `unchanged` | `delta`, the baseline and target `ReadVersion`s with their source hashes, `baselineBasis: 'same-request-delivered-whole'`, `targetDelivery`, `potential`, `changes`, `text`) or `unsupported` with a reason. `potential` is `{ kind: 'potential-reduction', realized: false, basis: 'text-as-sent', tokenCountKind: 'character-estimate', fullChars/Tokens, previewChars/Tokens, reducibleChars/Tokens }`; no field in the report is named as a saving. `resultDeliveries(sent, forwarded)` derives `whole` | `replaced` per tool result from the descriptors of the request as sent and as forwarded, and gives a result it cannot read on both sides no entry. `READ_PREVIEW_LIMITS` and `READ_PREVIEW_NOTE` are exported.
- Preview text. Identical: `[maestro-preview unchanged-read baseline=<call id> sha256=<12 hex> lines=N]`. Changed: `[maestro-preview read-delta baseline=<call id> sha256=<12> target-sha256=<12> positions=file|text lines=+A/-R]` and unified hunks (`formatPatch` with `OMIT_HEADERS`, so `\ No newline at end of file` appears where it belongs). A call id is printed only when it matches `^[A-Za-z0-9_.:-]{1,64}$`. The baseline is named by its tool call id and a hash prefix, both already in the model's context, so it needs no marker of its own; every marker, hunk header and hunk line is in the estimate.
- Bounds, all explicit and lowerable per call: 64 pairs per request, 262,144 characters per pair, 2,097,152 characters in total, 5,000 lines, 256 changed lines (jsdiff's `maxEditLength`, a count of added plus removed lines, so a changed line costs 2), 3 context lines. Sizes are checked against the finding before any text is hashed or diffed. The diff is bounded by `maxEditLength` and never by `timeout`, so a loaded machine cannot change a result.
- Reasons a pair has no preview: `over-budget`, `source-missing`, `source-mismatch` (length, hash, relation or first printed line number disagrees with the finding), `baseline-replaced`, `baseline-unproven`, `baseline-previewed`, `too-large`, `too-many-lines`, `diff-too-large`, `no-line-changes` (the reads differ only in how numbers are printed), `insufficient-reduction`.
- A baseline counts as visible on one basis only: it sits in this request (the analyzer's proof: same context, no reset, no incremental or managed history) and the caller proved the model is shown its exact text. A baseline with no delivery entry is `baseline-unproven`; one shortened on the way is `baseline-replaced`. Age is not read anywhere. A baseline that is itself the target of a preview earlier in the report is `baseline-previewed`: replacing it would leave the model without its full text, so the pair's figure would overstate. The totals in `counts.potentialChars/Tokens` are therefore additive.
- Numbered reads are diffed without their printed numbers, so an insertion above does not turn every later line into a change; their hunks carry the file's own line numbers (the printed first line number shifts the positions) and `positions=file`. A plain read prints no numbers, so its hunks count lines of the returned text and say `positions=text`; `offset` is not trusted as numbering.
- `src/main/local-models/proxy/read-opportunities.ts`: exports `withoutLineNumbers()`, which reuses the analyzer's own `NUMBER_PREFIX` (no second numbering pattern).
- `src/__tests__/helpers/proxyReadRequests.ts` (new): the request-body builders (`body`, `read`, `numbered`, `event`, `deepFreeze` and the fixture constants) moved unchanged out of `read-opportunities.test.ts`, which now imports them, so 04.2 and the next two tasks share one copy.
- `CLAUDE.md` index and `docs/agent-guides/CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md`: a new entry for the preview builder; the analyzer's index line and entry now name `withoutLineNumbers()` and point at the previews.

Regression cases (executed): `src/__tests__/main/local-models/proxy/read-previews.test.ts`, 62 cases. Identical re-reads in all three APIs (exact marker text, both versions and hashes kept, the basis string, a short re-read refused, the 10% bar at its edge). Deltas: a small edit in a numbered read (exact text), the estimate arithmetic, a plain read, an offset read, an insertion above (one added line, not a rewrite), zero context lines with an offset, tab numbering, CRLF, merged and separate hunks, first and last line, a missing final newline in both directions (a change, never "unchanged"), a number-padding-only change, huge diffs (over the edit budget; inside it but not smaller; the 1/2 edit-line boundary). Visibility: replaced, unproven, no age inference (adjacent and 30 results apart), end to end through `resultDeliveries()`, a shortened re-read reported through `targetDelivery`, the chain rule for changed and identical reads, a later pair still allowed after a refused one. `resultDeliveries()`: identical bodies, one result changed, unalignable results, a multi-part result, all three APIs. Integrity: missing text, same-length and different-length text, a lying relation, a skewed printed number, an unsafe call id, analyzer findings that are not pairs, the analyzer's `complete: false` passed on. Bounds: pairs, total characters, per-pair characters and lines (no diff computed past them), the echoed limits, context lines, one entry per pair in order. Labels: report and per-figure labelling, no saving-like key anywhere, totals equal the built previews only, file text only in `text`. Contract: deterministic, a clock jumping an hour per call changes nothing and no `timeout` is passed, deep-frozen inputs, the cache accessors never called, nothing kept between calls. `read-opportunities.test.ts` gained 6 `withoutLineNumbers` cases.

The tool has no diagnostics operation, so type safety rests on `npm run lint`.

### Unresolved limits

- No production code calls the builder yet. Exposure through the report and inspection model is 04.4, which must also supply `deliveries` (`resultDeliveries()` over the request as sent and as forwarded is the intended source). With no entry for a result, a pair is refused as `baseline-unproven`; nothing defaults to "visible".
- Every figure is measured against the re-read as the agent sent it. Where the proxy also shortens that re-read the two reductions overlap, `targetDelivery` says so, and a preview figure must not be added to realized compression savings.
- Only the first re-read of a chain (read, edit, read, edit, read) is previewed; later ones are `baseline-previewed`. This undercounts and never overstates.
- "Visible" means in this request and delivered whole. What a provider does with its own context after that is outside what the proxy sees; the analyzer already refuses `previous_response_id`, `conversation`, `context_management` and compaction items.
- The preview text holds lines of the file. 04.4 decides whether it leaves the process and should redact it (`redactSecrets()`) if it is persisted or sent.
- Hunks of a plain read count lines of the returned text, not file lines. Token figures are 4-characters-per-token estimates that err low (`TOKEN_PROXY_ESTIMATE_NOTE`).
- This is the first runtime `require('diff')` in the Electron main process. It is a production dependency, but no packaged build was made here; the final phase owns the build.
- Exercised with synthetic bodies only; no provider workload was run, and nothing here measures what a model retained or what a replacement would cost in quality.

## Supervisor repair: provider targets and blanket proxy defaults, 2026-11-11

`applyDefaultAgentTokenProxy()` in `src/renderer/services/agentGateway.ts` treated every stored `runOn` object as an explicit source override. A valid `{ type: 'provider' }` therefore bypassed the global proxy default. Reused `storedRunOnNamesSource()` from `src/shared/local-models/run-on.ts`: explicit provider targets now inherit the default, while source and malformed targets, explicit proxy modes (including `off`), gateways and ineligible agents retain their previous protection. No live settings were changed.

Executed `npm test -- src/__tests__/renderer/services/agentGateway.test.ts` with Node 24. The three new provider-target cases failed before the fix; the complete file passed all 65 tests afterward, including new explicit-off and malformed-source cases. `npx tsc -p tsconfig.lint.json`, targeted ESLint, Prettier and `npm run docs:verify` passed. LSP definition/references resolved the canonical source-target validator, and both production files returned no syntactic or semantic diagnostics; the test file's inferred LSP project lacked the existing `window.maestro` ambient declaration, so no clean test-file LSP claim is made. Logs: `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/supervisor-agent-default-*`. Final phase still owns full-suite and integration validation.

## Supervisor repair: structured Responses shell failures, 2026-11-11

`responsesToolResults()` in `src/main/local-models/proxy/compress.ts` now recognizes failure evidence in an output string shaped as `{ output: string, metadata: { exit_code: integer } }`. `hasFailedShellEnvelope()` reuses the span scanner and canonical `describeToolActivityStatus()`; it requires unique output, metadata and exit-code fields and a safe integer. A nonzero code vetoes compression before cache lookup; it never changes a frozen verdict, and zero does not promote unknown data to success. Top-level `is_error: true` remains protective. Other protocols, malformed or ambiguous envelopes and plain-text exit claims keep their previous classification.

The targeted red run reproduced five failures before the fix. Executed the complete compressor, loopback-server and read-opportunity test files afterward: **317 tests passed in 3 files**. Added 20 regression cases covering both compression profiles, pre-existing cross-protocol cached verdicts, unchanged large-number API bytes, negative/positive codes, conflicting flags, duplicate keys, wrong types and malformed text. Targeted ESLint, main-process TypeScript and Prettier passed. LSP definition/references resolved the shared status classifier; six syntactic/semantic diagnostics requests across production, tests and classifier returned no diagnostics. Logs: `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/supervisor-exit-guard-*`. These checks close the two concrete supervisor risks identified for phase 08.1; final review and full-suite validation remain required.

## 04.3: Code-structure preview

Execution date: 2026-11-11. Status: complete, observation only. Selected the first unchecked task in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-04.md`. Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`; HEAD was `9703cbf52a` at the start and advanced to `38c184b9ff` during the run through two supervisor commits. Neither touched a file of this task except this ledger and `CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md`, which were re-read before editing; the mid-run edit to `compress.ts` that `git status` showed was the supervisor's, was committed by them, and is not part of this task. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin` (the default shell has `v26.12.1`).

Read the execution contract, the analysis (T10, the packaging paragraph and "Immutable cache and identity design"), this ledger, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/agent-guides/SHARED-UTILS.md` and the canonical-utilities index. **Parser inventory first** (log `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/04.3-parser-inventory.log`): the app has no language-server client and no tree-sitter; the only code grammars are the Lezer packs behind `@codemirror/lang-*`, imported by four renderer files for highlighting; `typescript` is a dev dependency (`^6.0.3`); Document Graph parses Markdown links. So the parser is Lezer, which is already a production dependency tree, and no new package was added. Reused: `ReadVersion`, `ReadFinding`, `withoutLineNumbers` (04.1), `potentialReduction` and `MARKER_HASH_CHARS` (04.2, exported for this), `MIN_SAVING_RATIO`, `contentHash`, `getFileExtension`, `yieldToEventLoop`. Looked at and not reused: the renderer's `getLanguageFromFilename()` and `languageLoader.ts` (renderer code; they answer which highlighter, not which grammar), and `countLines()` (renderer only, and a different count).

- `src/main/local-models/proxy/code-structure.ts` (new, 495 code lines by the count 04.2 used: no blanks, no comments): `buildCodeStructurePreviews({ report, events, deliveries?, limits? })` returns a `CodeStructureReport` (`mode: 'preview'`, `note`, `complete`, `limits`, `previews`, `counts`) with exactly one entry per read the analyzer accepted as one clean text: a baseline, a repeat, or a read it only could not relate to an earlier one (`partial-range`, `reset-between`, `other-context`). An entry is a `preview` (`read` with the source hash, `language`, `parser: { name, dialect }`, `confidence: 'parsed-without-errors'`, `delivery`, `potential`, `outline` counts, `text`) or `unsupported` with a reason. `codeLanguageOf()`, `CODE_STRUCTURE_LIMITS`, `CODE_STRUCTURE_NOTE` and `PARSE_SLICE_STEPS` are exported. The file stays whole in the request and nothing is edited, opened or kept.
- Languages, by extension only: TypeScript (`.ts .mts .cts`), TSX, JavaScript (`.js .mjs .cjs .jsx`, parsed with the JSX dialect) and Python, through `@lezer/javascript` and `@lezer/python` imported on first use. Everything else, Markdown, policy files and data included, is `unsupported-language`. Each language is a small profile of node names (imports, wrappers such as `export` and decorators, nodes with a body, classes, signatures shown as written, bindings), not new control flow.
- Outline text: `[maestro-preview code-structure language=… sha256=<12 hex> lines=N bodies-omitted=B statements-omitted=S]`, then imports and re-exports as written; each function, method and function-valued constant as its signature with `{ /* N lines omitted */ }` (Python: `: ...  # N lines omitted`); overload signatures as written; classes with their members one level down; interfaces, aliases and enums as written; a constant on one line as written and a value over several lines replaced by a notice; CommonJS `module.exports` and `exports.x` statements; Python assignments, decorators and nested classes (summarized). Comments, doc comments and every other statement are left out and counted in the marker. Entries use the indentation the source gave them. A declaration over `maxEntryChars` (2,000) is cut at a line break with a notice that says how many characters were left out.
- Refusals: `partial-range` (a call with `offset` or `limit`, or printed numbers not starting at 1), `too-large`, `parse-error`, `too-complex`, `nothing-to-outline`, `insufficient-reduction` (the compressor's own `MIN_SAVING_RATIO` bar, marker included), `over-budget`, `source-missing`, `source-mismatch` (length or hash differs from the finding), `unsupported-language`. Gates run cheapest first, and everything before the parse is decided without loading a grammar. A text is parsed once per language however many reads show it.
- Uncertainty rule: the grammar runs in **strict mode** and the finished tree is **scanned for error nodes**; one is `parse-error`. Both are needed: strict mode alone accepted a 240 KB expression chain whose tree held 184 error nodes (Lezer forces a reduction there). A non-`SyntaxError` from the grammar is rethrown, not relabeled.
- CPU: the parse is driven through Lezer's public `startParse`/`advance` and gives the event loop a turn every 1,024 steps and after each distinct source, because the proxy forwards in this same process. Measured with Node 24 (logs `04.3-parse-bench*.log`): 256 KiB of punctuation took **16,341 ms** with error recovery and **0.1 ms** in strict mode; an unterminated template literal 999 ms to 0.0 ms; 50,000 nested parentheses 635 ms to 2.9 ms; real repo files of 31 to 44 KB parse in 4.5 to 6.3 ms; a valid 247 KB single-line object 131 ms. A step covered at least one character in every run (240,007 steps for 240,006 characters), and the longest 1,024-step slice was 1.9 ms on four repo files, 5.7 ms on 10,000 small functions and 16.2 ms on the 60,000-term chain (the worst input found). Loading the grammars costs 11.2 ms (`@lezer/javascript`) and 2.4 ms (`@lezer/python`) and about 4.6 MB of resident memory, once, on first use. Bounds, all lowerable per call: 32 sources, 262,144 characters per source, 1,048,576 characters in total, 300,000 tree nodes, 2,000 characters per shown declaration. There is no line bound: a parse costs by characters and nodes, and one was removed to keep the module under the size bound.
- `src/main/local-models/proxy/read-previews.ts`: `potentialReduction()` and `MARKER_HASH_CHARS` are now exported (LSP `findReferences` showed they were file-local, so nothing else changed). `package.json` and `package-lock.json` (+4 lines each): `@lezer/common ^1.5.2`, `@lezer/javascript ^1.5.4`, `@lezer/lr ^1.4.10` and `@lezer/python ^1.1.18` are now direct production dependencies at the versions already installed and locked; no `node_modules` entry changed. `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/agent-guides/CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md`: a new index line and entry, and `potentialReduction()` added to the previews entry.
- Fixtures: `src/__tests__/helpers/codeStructureFixtures.ts` holds the TypeScript, TSX, JavaScript, CommonJS and Python sources written to reach every shape the outline handles (04.4 reuses them).

Regression cases (executed): `src/__tests__/main/local-models/proxy/code-structure.test.ts`, 128 cases. Exact reviewed golden outlines for all five fixtures with their counts; numbered and plain reads giving the same outline, measured against the numbered text; CRLF equal to LF; a byte-order mark; every import, re-export, function, constant, class, decorator, interface, enum, namespace and `declare global` form; CommonJS statements including the near misses `exports.call()` and `exportsFoo = 1`; indentation from the source and the tab fallback; a wrapped signature with an inline comment; both cut forms with the exact omitted count; Python decorators, async, attributes, valid-Python placeholders. Language table (12 supported and 8 unsupported names, prototype-named extensions, 20 documents and data files with real code as text, Markdown in a `.ts` file). `partial-range` (offset, limit, offset 0, both, numbers from 120), a whole read after a range read and after a compaction both outlined, a re-read outlined, reads ruled out by the analyzer (NUL bytes, compression marker, failed call) given no entry, and all three provider APIs through the descriptor path. Trust: eight TypeScript gaps and six Python faults give `parse-error`, the 60,000-term chain (error nodes under strict mode), 50,000-deep nesting, and 256 KiB of punctuation in under 3 s; nothing-to-outline cases; the saving bar swept across its edge with an outline of constant size. Bounds: each limit at its edge, refusals before any parse (no turn of the loop), one parse per text and language, the same text under two extensions, incomplete analyzer, the frozen defaults, a source of 8,000 lines. Hashes: missing, longer and same-length wrong text. Event loop: turns grow with size, one per source, results identical under a delayed scheduler. Failing grammar: a `SyntaxError` is `parse-error`, any other error surfaces. Lazy loading: a fresh module loads no grammar until a source of its language is parsed, and Python's only for `.py`. Figures: exact `potentialReduction` arithmetic, labels, no key named as a saving, additive totals, `delivery` per read, file text only in `text`. Contract: deep-frozen inputs, deterministic output, the cache accessors never called, `server.ts` and `compress.ts` do not import the module, the module imports no file system, process, network or dev-only module, and every package it imports is a production dependency in `package.json`.

### Unresolved limits

- No production code calls the builder yet. Exposure through the report and inspection model is 04.4, which must also supply `deliveries` (`resultDeliveries()`); a read with no entry is reported `unproven`. This task adds no UI action, so there is no CLI parity row; 04.4 owns it.
- A construct the grammar does not know is `parse-error`, never a wrong outline. Measured gaps: a decorator before `export`, `import x = require()`, `declare module 'x'`, a class index signature, an anonymous `export default class extends X`, an unterminated template literal, `@a.b[0](1)` in Python. Strict Python also rejects an empty file or one of comments only, which is labeled `parse-error` though valid. The grammars tolerate a few faults: an unterminated single-quoted Python string ends at its line, and Python 2 `print` parses, so such a file is outlined as the grammar saw it.
- Text appended to a Read result that is not file content (a reminder block, say) makes the text a parse error. The tool's own truncation of a long file is invisible, so a cut read is outlined as far as it goes; the note says so.
- The figure is measured against the file as sent, is an alternative to a `read-previews.ts` figure for the same result, and overlaps any compression the proxy applies (`delivery` says which). It must not be added to either, and an outline cannot replace a file whose bodies are needed. Token counts are 4-characters-per-token estimates.
- The outline text holds lines of the file, and a one-line constant is shown as written, a secret-looking one included. 04.4 decides whether it leaves the process and should apply `redactSecrets()` if it is persisted or sent.
- Python class attributes and module assignments are shown like TypeScript constants; unannotated attributes of a class are therefore shown too. Class members are shown one level down only (a Python nested class is summarized); TypeScript object-literal methods and everything inside a body are not.
- The thresholds come from synthetic inputs and the repository's own sources on one machine. No packaged build was made (the final phase owns it), and no provider workload ran. Nothing measures what a model does with an outline.

## 04.4: Read opportunities in the report

Execution date: 2026-11-11 to 2026-11-12. Status: complete, observation only. Selected the first unchecked task in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-04.md`. Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`, HEAD `14a7bfb8f2` at the start; tracked tree clean before edits, `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` untouched. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin` (the default shell has `v26.12.1`). The supervising session edited this worktree while the task ran (the "Supervisor review during 04.4" section above: the note constant, the observer header, a server comment and one shared contract case). Those edits were re-read before each of mine and belong to this commit.

Read the execution contract, the analysis (T09 to T11, "Immutable cache and identity design"), this ledger, `CLAUDE.md` with its canonical-utilities index, and the task lists of phases 05 to 08 to see which later task owns what: stats rows and migrations are 06.1 to 06.5, the Settings panel is 07.3 and the dashboard view is 07.4, so none of them is built here. Reused, unchanged: `findToolResultDescriptors`, `readEventsFromDescriptors`, `analyzeReadOpportunities`, `buildReadPreviews`, `resultDeliveries`, `buildCodeStructurePreviews` and their limits, `MARKER_HASH_CHARS`, `redactSecrets`, `yieldToEventLoop`, the CLI formatter's `plural`, `formatCount` and `formatRelativeTime`, and the shared request builders of the 04.2 tests. Not reused: `recoveryReadCounters` (it counts archive reads; the fixed-size-tally idea is the same, the shape is not), the stats database (phase 06), `structuredClone` (not in the ES2020 lib two of the four TypeScript configurations use).

**The overhead decision.** The task allows computing previews only on an explicit report request "from existing captured data" if observing each request costs too much. Nothing could be computed that way: the proxy keeps no request text (a log entry never holds a body, and the cache holds only compressed blocks keyed by hash, with no path, range or call). So the question was whether a pass is cheap enough to run per request. Measured with Node 24 on synthetic Messages bodies (scratch benchmark, log `04.4-bench.log`, median of five): a whole pass cost 2.6 to 4.0 ms for 203 KB (52 results), 10.3 to 10.5 ms for 1.3 MB (202), 37.6 to 38.2 ms for 6.0 MB (502) and 51.7 to 53.8 ms for 13.4 MB (122, over the 8 MiB bound, so skipped in the product); parsing the body is under 1 ms of that, and the history scan (descriptors, decode, hash) is almost all of it. Previews and outlines of the newest results cost 0 to 5.5 ms. Request bodies rebuilt from two real Claude Code transcripts of this project (17 and 15 requests ending in a Read result, bodies up to 1.6 MB, log `04.4-real.log`) took a median of 3.4 and 1.0 ms and a p95 of 24.7 and 8.4 ms. That is bounded, so observation runs per request, deferred and single-flight, and the report states its coverage.

- `src/main/local-models/proxy/opportunity-observer.ts` (new): `observeRequest(request, roots, observedAt, limits?)` runs the three phase 04 steps over one request and returns a `RequestObservation` (counts and previews) or the reason it was not examined (`too-large`, `no-workspace`, `unreadable`). `createOpportunityLedger()` holds fixed-size, saturating tallies (reads, re-read previews and outlines apart, each with `unsupportedByReason`) and the 25 newest previews, and hands out a JSON copy. `createOpportunityObserver({ rootsFor, onFailure?, now?, limits? })` examines one request at a time: `observe()` returns at once, starts the work on a later turn, counts a request that arrives meanwhile as `busy`, counts a thrown pass as `failed` and reports it through `onFailure`. `OPPORTUNITY_OBSERVER_LIMITS` (8 MiB body, 25 recent previews, 8,192 characters shown per preview).
- **Only the newest results are counted** (`newestResultsStart()`): the final Messages message, the trailing `tool` messages, or the trailing `function_call_output` / `custom_tool_call_output` items. Every request re-sends its history, so counting every read would count one read once for each later request; a request that ends in a new prompt adds nothing. The older reads stay the baselines. The analysis window (`windowStart()`) is the newest reads that fit the analyzer's 256-read and 8 MiB bounds, because the analyzer spends its bounds from the oldest read and would otherwise leave the newest reads `over-budget` in a long history. Most requests end in a shell result or an edit and cost one parse and a descriptor pass, with no hashing.
- **No identity is invented.** The report lists `tab`, `native-session`, `subagent` and `window` as unavailable (`TOKEN_PROXY_UNAVAILABLE_IDENTITY`) with a sentence saying why, nothing is remembered between requests, and an entry carries only the `agentId` the gateway URL named. A baseline counts as visible only through `resultDeliveries()` over the request as sent and as forwarded, so a baseline the proxy shortened is `baseline-replaced` and one that cannot be lined up is `baseline-unproven`.
- **Previews leave the process redacted and cut.** `redactSecrets()` runs over a window four times the shown size, then the text is cut at a line break to 8,192 characters; `textChars`, `cut` and `redacted` say what happened. The raw text is never kept.
- `src/main/local-models/proxy/server.ts`: `TokenProxyObservedRequest` and the `observe` option. The hook is offered only a request the proxy read and compressed or left whole (not passthrough, not a path without a message list, not a body it could not decode, not a compressor failure, not a background-routed request), is assigned last in the `try` so a failure is never offered, and runs from `setImmediate` after `forward(...).end(body)`. It is not awaited and no byte, header, log field or status depends on it.
- `src/main/local-models/proxy/service.ts`: one observer for the process, `workspaceRoots()` (the stored record's `projectRoot` and `cwd`), `observe` wired into the server, `onFailure` to the log and Sentry (`tokenProxy:observeReadOpportunities`), `readOpportunities` in `getTokenProxyReport()` beside `usage`, and `resetTokenProxyForTests()` waits for the pass in flight and resets it.
- `src/shared/local-models/token-proxy.ts`: the contract (`TokenProxyReadOpportunities`, `TokenProxyOpportunity`, the three tally types, `TokenProxyObservationSkip`, `TOKEN_PROXY_UNAVAILABLE_IDENTITY`), `TOKEN_PROXY_OPPORTUNITY_NOTE`, the label tables and `describeOpportunityReasons()`. `TokenProxyReport.readOpportunities` is optional, because a CLI may talk to an older desktop. `TokenProxyPotentialReduction` moved here from `read-previews.ts`, which now aliases it (`PotentialReduction`), so the report and the builders share one definition. `read-opportunities.ts` exports `isReadToolName()` and `countFindings()`, both already there.
- `src/cli/output/formatter.ts`: `formatTokenProxyOpportunities()`, printed by `models proxy status` after "Recent compressions" and apart from the realized totals; `--json` carries the whole block. `src/cli/index.ts` and `docs/cli-reference.md`: the status description. `CLAUDE.md`, `CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md`, `docs/local-models.md`, `docs/architecture/local-models.md`, `CLI-PLAYBOOKS.md` and `CLI-UI-PARITY.md` describe it. No new UI action exists, so no new parity row; the existing status row names the field.
- `src/__tests__/helpers/proxyReadRequests.ts`: `Item` may be a list of steps the model called in one turn (their results arrive together), for all three APIs. Existing cases build the same bodies as before.

Regression cases (executed). `opportunity-observer.test.ts`, 48 cases: an unchanged and a changed re-read at the end of the history in all three APIs (exact kinds, hashes, basis, delivery, change counts); a read counted once while the next request carries it in its history; every result of a parallel newest turn; a request ending in a new prompt, and one ending in a shell result, add nothing; a 300-read history still examines the read the request adds; the three skip reasons; unsupported reasons by name (`partial-range`, `outside-workspace`, `failed-read`, `incremental-history`); a baseline the proxy shortened, a forwarded body that cannot be lined up, a shortened re-read (`delivery: replaced`), and the forwarded body lined up with the window in a long history; an outline of a TypeScript fixture, and an outline and a re-read preview kept apart; redaction of a key-shaped constant (the token is assembled at run time), a cut at a line break with `textChars`; a request past a work bound marked incomplete; yields (3 for a pass, 0 for nothing to do); determinism, frozen input, the cache accessors never called, and no key named as a saving; the ledger (empty state and constants, sums with reasons, skips, the recent list order and bound, saturation, copies, reset); the observer (nothing done in the caller's turn, one pass at a time, a failure reported and survived). `opportunity-report.test.ts`, 5 cases through the product's own path (the service starts a real server, the real compressor and observer run, a loopback provider answers): the provider receives the request byte for byte, the working directories come from the stored record, a passthrough agent is never examined, an agent with no working directory is counted, what was noticed sits beside the usage and the request recorder's entry holds no opportunity field, and reset forgets it. `server.test.ts` +7: the hook is offered the request as sent and as forwarded; same bytes, headers, answer and log with and without it, even from a hook that never returns; it runs after the compressor; it is never offered an unread request (passthrough, a path without messages, an unknown route, two unreadable bodies, a failed compressor); with the real observer, a re-read is reported and the request is untouched, a read is counted once across two requests, and a baseline the proxy already shortened leaves no opportunity while the log shows the realized reduction. `service.test.ts` +5: wiring, roots from the record, failure to the log and Sentry, the report field beside the usage, reset order. `read-opportunities.test.ts` +4, `models-proxy.test.ts` +6 (human output apart from the totals, nothing for an older desktop or an unset proxy, the whole block in JSON, skips and incomplete lines), `local-models-token-proxy.test.ts` +4.

The first mutation sweep (42 breaks) left 7 survivors. One was a redundant branch (the Messages role check; removed from the code), one was a malformed mutation of mine, equivalent by construction (dropped), four were real gaps in my tests and each got a case (the forwarded body lined up with the window, the line-break cut, the incomplete flag, the yields), and one is the equivalent survivor above.

LSP was exposed as a deferred tool and used: `findReferences` for `PotentialReduction` (7 references in `read-previews.ts` and `code-structure.ts`, all resolving through the alias), `countFindings` (4, in the analyzer and the observer) and the `observe` option (10, in the server, the service and the server tests). Its `documentSymbol` view of the new module was one edit behind (it listed a constant I had already renamed), so no diagnostics claim rests on it; type safety rests on `npm run lint`. The editor diagnostics shown while editing `models-proxy.test.ts` (`provider` missing on the older `RUNNING` fixture) predate this task and are not part of `npm run lint`.

Real-shape check (counts only, no file text recorded): in the 32 rebuilt requests every Read result was recognized: 26 first reads and 6 `partial-range` (ranged calls), 3 source files outlined (2 in one transcript for 4,502 estimated tokens of potential, 1 in the other for 4,517), and no identical or changed re-read at all. Claude Code on this machine mostly reads once and in ranges, so the feature reports what is there and no saving is implied.

### Unresolved limits

- The report is process-lifetime and in memory. Stats rows, a migration and aggregation are 06.1 to 06.5; the Settings panel is 07.3 and the dashboard view 07.4. Until then the data is visible through `maestro-cli models proxy status` (human and `--json`) and the `localModels:proxy:report` channel, which the renderer already receives.
- Attribution is by the newest results of each examined request. A client that resends a request is observed twice, a request skipped as busy loses its newest results, and the first request after a restart counts only its own newest results. Reads older than the newest 256 reads or 8 MiB are not examined, so a re-read whose baseline is that old shows as `first`.
- Working directories are the stored record's `projectRoot` and `cwd`. An agent's extra directories are not roots, so a read there is `outside-workspace`.
- Only requests the proxy reads are examined (a compress mode); passthrough bodies are streamed unread and an agent on an SSH remote cannot reach the proxy. The Responses `previous_response_id` and `conversation` shapes are refused by name, and Claude Code's Read in ranges is `partial-range`.
- Every figure is a 4-characters-per-token estimate against the text as the agent sent it. A re-read preview and an outline can describe the same result and are kept apart; where the proxy also shortened that result, `delivery` says `replaced` and the figure overlaps the realized reduction. Nothing measures what a model does with a delta or an outline.
- Size. The change adds 623 non-blank, non-comment production lines in 8 production files (the new module is 398), over the roughly 500 lines and 1 to 3 files this playbook asks of a task. 127 are type and label declarations in the shared contract and about 35 are zero initializers; the parts were written and tested together and are not split. Timings are from synthetic bodies and one machine. No packaged build was made (the final phase owns it) and no provider workload ran.

## Supervisor review during 05.1: verification false positives, resolved (first three by 05.1, the rest by 05.1b)

An executed pure probe of the in-progress `buildContextDigest()` found three false verified observations. A completed Bash call with exit code 0 and (a) `rg 'npm test' checks.log`, output `Yesterday npm test: 12 passed`; (b) `cat 'npm test results.log'`, output `12 passed`; or (c) `printf '%s %s\\n' 12 'passed'; echo 'npm test'`, output `12 passed` followed by `npm test`, is currently put in `verifiedObservations`. These read or print text and never execute a check. The substring `CHECK_COMMAND` match and `command.includes(pass)` do not prove a check invocation. The probe bundled the pure module with existing esbuild, executed no shell payload and changed no production file. Evidence: `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/supervisor-digest-verification-probe.log`.

Resolve these with executed regression cases before accepting 05.1 or 05.2. Reuse existing command parsing where available; do not patch only the three example strings or promote arbitrary quoted runner names, file names, comments or compound-command output into verification. If invocation or result provenance is uncertain, report an observation or unknown instead of a verified result. All other task acceptance, including original user constraints and explicit unknowns, still applies.

A second executed probe found command-identity loss: a failed `npm test -- -t 'alpha  beta'` followed by a passing `npm test -- -t 'alpha beta'` produces no unresolved failure. `recordRun()` collapses whitespace inside the quoted argument, treating two different test selections as one command. Preserve semantically distinct command arguments instead of applying global whitespace replacement; do not let one selection's success clear another selection's failure. Evidence: `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/supervisor-digest-command-identity-probe.log`. No shell command from either probe was executed.

A third pure probe found user-constraint fidelity loss. User messages `Only modify src/Foo.ts` and `Only modify src/foo.ts` with the paths in inline-code spans are deduplicated into the first constraint; a later unrelated request means the second is not retained elsewhere. `Never open /tmp/a  b` with the path in inline code is changed to `/tmp/a b`, with no unknown or truncation flag. The probe retained only two constraints and no unknowns. Keep case-sensitive identifiers and literal whitespace intact when deduplicating and displaying user constraints. Evidence: `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/supervisor-digest-constraint-probe.log`. These are executable regression requirements for 05.1, not optional later polish.

The command-identity issue also affects argv arrays: failed `['npm', 'test', '--', '-t', 'alpha beta']` followed by successful `['npm', 'test', '--', '-t', 'alpha', 'beta']` loses the first failure because `describeToolCommand()` joins both into the same display string. Display text is not an invocation identity. Preserve original argument boundaries when deriving evidence and grouping runs; do not reinterpret a space inside an argv item as an argument separator. Reproduced in `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/supervisor-digest-argv-identity-probe.log`, with no subprocess execution.

Include supplied execution context in that identity too: two `exec_command` calls with identical `cmd: 'npm test'` but `workdir: '/repo/one'` (failed) and `workdir: '/repo/two'` (passed) currently erase the first package's failure. Reproduced in `supervisor-digest-workdir-identity-probe.log` in the same scratch folder. Preserve meaningful tool-input differences such as argv boundaries, working directory and environment instead of grouping by display text; missing execution context stays uncertain. Use the existing `canonicalJson()` when serializing invocation keys, as required by the canonical-utilities index, rather than introducing another `JSON.stringify` equality key.

After the first strict-command fix, one verification counterexample remains: `npx --package vitest echo 'All tests passed'` with a clean exit and that printed line is still verified. `vitest` is an option value here; the executable is `echo`. Do not treat the first non-dash token as the executable when preceding options can consume values. Ambiguous wrappers may stay unknown. The positive control `npx vitest run` still verifies. Reproduced by the pure module in `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/supervisor-digest-npx-provenance-probe.log`; no npx command was executed by the probe.

The consolidated independent probe is now executable: with Node 24 run `node .maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/digest-review-probes.cjs`. It bundles the current source and runs only synthetic inputs, including positive controls and same-invocation retry. The latest run exited 1: 9 of 12 cases passed; remaining failures are npx option-value provenance, argv boundaries and working-directory identity. Output: `supervisor-digest-review-current.log` in that scratch folder. Resolve these before 05.1 acceptance and retain meaningful cases in the tracked Vitest suite.

## 05.1: Evidence digest of a conversation

Execution date: 2026-11-12. Status: complete, preview only. Selected the first unchecked task in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-05.md`. Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`; HEAD `39795335cb` at the start, `079ff4a0c4` at commit time (a merge of the supervisor's Computer History digest work: 14 files, none one I touched). Tracked tree clean before edits; `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` untouched. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin`. The supervising session appended "Supervisor review during 05.1" above while the task ran. It reported three executed probes against my in-progress code. All three were real defects; the fixes and their regression cases are below, and that section is kept as written.

Read the execution contract, the analysis (T13, T14 and the Continuity row), this ledger, `CLAUDE.md` with its canonical-utilities index, and `CLAUDE-PATTERNS.md` (the lifecycle sections and §15 error handling; no lifecycle path changed, because the digest starts no turn, registers nothing and swallows no error). Symbol search found no existing digest owner. `parseSynopsis()` and `isFailedSynopsisResponse()` read synopsis evidence, `activeContextRecords()` and the native-records projections are provider-translation records (05.2 uses them where identity is proven), `getContextCompactCommand()` maps providers to compaction prompts, and `goalHandoff.ts` is the Goal-Driven iteration note. So the digest is a new pure module composed from those owners, not a second synopsis or checkpoint store.

Reused unchanged: `forEachMarkdownLine`, `redactSecrets`, `truncateAtWordBoundary`, `getBasename`, `stripAnsiCodes`, `MAX_PERSISTED_TOOL_OUTPUT_CHARS`, `describeToolActivityStatus`, `parseSynopsis`, `isFailedSynopsisResponse`. Extended in their canonical owner: `toolActivityLabel.ts`. Moved: the token proxy's private `literalCommandTokens()`.

- `src/shared/contextDigest.ts` (new, pure): `buildContextDigest({ entries, synopses?, agentId?, maxChars? })` returns the objective (first user request with real wording) and the latest request, the user's constraint sentences, pending requirements (an unanswered request, then the open steps of the agent's latest task list), the next action, verified observations, unresolved failures, optional synopsis excerpts, `unknowns` with reasons, `omitted` counts, `references` (`user #12`, `tool #31`, `synopsis h-9`) and a `preview` string. Same entries give the same digest; the input is never changed.
- **Rules kept.** Objective, constraints and requests come only from `user` entries (fenced code, `>` quotes and questions skipped) and the agent's own task list; assistant and tool text appears only as a quoted `detail` (one line, backticks removed, secrets redacted) that the preview labels as data. A result is verified only when the call finished, the command is read as ONE invocation of a check runner and its output has a passing summary line. Exit status alone is listed as an unknown. A clean exit beside failure wording is a conflict, a command that failed after passing keeps both, a failure the identical command later passed is dropped, a first and a different latest request are both shown, wording that may take back an earlier instruction is noted without removing anything, and items from before the last completed compaction are marked. The budget (8,000 characters of items by default) spends on constraints, then pending work, failures, verified results, excerpts; what it leaves out is counted. There is no daemon, hook, timer or checkpoint file.
- `src/shared/literalCommand.ts` (new): `literalCommandTokens()` moved verbatim from `compress.ts`, plus `readLiteralCommand()` (`words` after leading assignments, and a `key` where equal keys mean the same arguments with quoting resolved, so spacing inside a quoted argument matters and spacing between arguments does not). It reads past a leading `cd <literal path> &&` only, because that prints nothing and runs the rest only if it worked; the directory is part of the key. Every other pipe, `;`, `||`, redirect, subshell, comment, glob, expansion, line break or unclosed quote returns null.
- `src/shared/toolActivityLabel.ts`: `describeToolCommand()` (the whole untruncated shell command of a shell call), `describeToolTaskList()` (the steps of a TodoWrite or update_plan call) and `describesFileEdit()`, all answered from the verb `describeToolActivity()` chose, so they cannot disagree with the feed. `describeToolActivity()` now takes its input split and its command from two small helpers and its verbs from constants, with no change to any label (all 45 existing tests pass). **A latent crash was fixed on the way:** `todoSummary()` threw a `TypeError` on a `null` step in a task list, which the new task-list reader would have inherited for every tool entry. It now skips non-object steps (regression case added).
- `src/renderer/services/contextSummarizer.ts`: `buildContinuityDigest(logs, options?)` on `ContextSummarizationService`, a pure pass-through that starts no turn and calls no IPC (tested). `src/main/local-models/proxy/compress.ts` imports the moved function instead of defining it.
- Docs: index lines and full entries in `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/agent-guides/CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md` (digest, literal command reader, and the three new `toolActivityLabel` helpers), and the service line in `docs/agent-guides/RENDERER-SERVICES.md`.

### Supervisor findings, resolved with executed regression cases

1. **False verified observations.** `CHECK_COMMAND` matched a runner name anywhere in the command line and `command.includes(pass)` proved nothing about the invocation. Now the command is read by `readLiteralCommand()` and is verified only as a single invocation of a runner (`isCheckInvocation()`: a bare runner, `npx`/`bunx`/`pnpx` plus a runner, `npm`/`pnpm`/`yarn`/`bun` plus a test, lint, build, typecheck, check or validate script, `python -m` plus a runner, and `go`/`cargo`/`dotnet`/`swift`/`mvn`/`gradle`/`make` plus a check subcommand). `rg 'npm test' checks.log`, `cat 'npm test results.log'`, `echo "npm test: 12 passed"` and `grep -n vitest package.json` are no check and leave no trace. `printf ... ; echo 'npm test'`, `npx vitest run && echo ok`, a pipe, a redirect, a comment, an expansion and `cd app; ...` mention a check without being one invocation, so they are listed under unknowns as unverified and never as results. The old regex survives only as `MENTIONS_CHECK`, used to flag such a command, never to verify. Cases: 4 not-a-check rows, 7 chained rows and 11 literal-invocation rows (`it.each`), not the three example strings.
2. **Command identity.** `recordRun()` no longer collapses whitespace. The key is `readLiteralCommand().key` (arguments with quoting resolved, assignments and `cd` directories included), or the exact trimmed text when the command cannot be read. A failed `npm test -- -t 'alpha  beta'` followed by a passing `npm test -- -t 'alpha beta'` now keeps the failure; selections differing by case stay apart; the same command in two directories stays apart; `npm   test` and `npm test` are one command. The command is also shown as it ran, with its spacing.
3. **Constraint fidelity.** Constraints are deduplicated by exact text (`Foo.ts` and `foo.ts` are two rules), spacing inside a sentence is kept (`/tmp/a  b`, tabs), a sentence is no longer split inside an inline code span or after `e.g.`/`i.e.`/`vs.`/`cf.`, and a line break in an excerpt shows as ⏎ instead of silently becoming a space. The Constraints heading now says a rule worded without constraint wording is not found. Only a secret is replaced, by its placeholder.

LSP was exposed as a deferred tool and used: `findReferences` on `literalCommandTokens` (23 references in the shared module, `compress.ts` and the tests), `buildContextDigest` (the summarizer adapter and the tests) and `describeToolCommand` (the digest).
