### Unresolved limits

- **Preview only, no surface yet.** Nothing in production calls `buildContinuityDigest()`; 05.2 owns the explicit summarize/continue preview, its activity and synopsis gates, cross-tab rejection, cleared or compacted history, native active/archived projections where identity is proven, and the `maestro-cli` path that `CLI-UI-PARITY.md` requires for a new UI action. Until then the digest is never injected, sent or stored.
- **Sentences, not meaning.** The constraint and retraction cues find sentences to show; a rule worded without a cue ("use tabs") is not found and the heading says so. The objective is the first message with three words, not a judged goal.
- **What can be verified is narrow on purpose.** Only a literal single invocation of a recognized runner can be verified. `npm test 2>&1 | tail -5` and other chained or piped runs are unknowns even though they often passed; the one chaining accepted is a leading `cd <literal path> &&` (it prints nothing and gates the rest, so provenance is certain; this goes beyond a strict reading of the review and is tested both ways). A `package.json` script named `test` that only echoes a pass line would verify, because the script's content is not visible.
- **Stored output is short.** Tool output is kept at 4,000 characters with the head, so a runner's summary at the end of a long output is often missing; the run then stays exit-only and the unknown says the output reached its cap.
- **Failures.** Only shell calls and agent errors (with no reply after them) are read as failures; a failed edit or read is not. Identity is by exact arguments, so `tsc -p a` failing and `npx tsc --noEmit -p a` passing stays unresolved. (An argv-array command was compared by its space-joined text here; 05.1b compares its elements, with the directory and variables a call names.) File edits since a result count tool edits only; a file changed by `sed -i` is not counted.
- **Redaction** replaces a secret with its placeholder, visibly, and sets no separate flag.
- **Size.** `contextDigest.ts` has 615 non-blank, non-comment lines (about 100 are type and interface declarations), `literalCommand.ts` 70 (47 are `literalCommandTokens` moved verbatim; `compress.ts` lost 45), `toolActivityLabel.ts` +72 and -14, `contextSummarizer.ts` +11: five production files and about 710 net lines, over the roughly 500 lines and 1 to 3 files this playbook asks of a task. The supervisor's review added about 60 of them (the command reader, the identity key, the fidelity fixes). Not split: the parts were written and tested together, and the parser move was reuse. `classifyRun()` and the run history are the extraction point if 05.4 needs the same call evidence.
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them).

## 05.1b: Digest review repairs (command identity and wrapper provenance)

Execution date: 2026-11-12. Status: complete, preview only. Selected the first unchecked task in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-05.md`. Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`, HEAD `507cf80b61` at the start; tracked tree clean before edits, `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` untouched. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin`. I read the latest supervisor review above (all five findings, not only the first three) and the consolidated probe; the baseline run reproduced 9 of 12 (failing: npx option-value provenance, argv boundaries, working-directory identity).

**Cause, one level down.** All three failures were one defect: the digest decided "same invocation" from a display string. `describeToolCommand()` joined an argv array with spaces and dropped `workdir`, so `readLiteralCommand()` saw `npm test -- -t alpha beta` for both argv shapes and the same `npm test` for both directories. The wrapper bug was separate: `isCheckInvocation()` took the first non-dash word after `npx` as the program, so the value of `--package` counted as an executable.

- `src/shared/literalCommand.ts`: the key is now `canonicalJson` of `{ argv, cd?, set? }` (the old key was a `JSON.stringify` of an array). Leading assignments are their own `set` member, so the program `CI=1` of an argv array is not the assignment `CI=1` of a command line. New `readCommandCall({ text, argv?, cwd?, env? })` returns `{ key, words }` for any call: an argv array is read as it is (each element is one argument, no shell syntax, no assignments), and is the same invocation as the equivalent command line; `cwd` and `env` are part of the key; an unreadable call is keyed by its exact text and the same context, in a separate `text` member that cannot collide with a readable key; an absent directory or variable set is not equal to a present one. `CommandCall` and `CommandReading` are the new types. `readLiteralCommand()` is unchanged in behavior.
- `src/shared/toolActivityLabel.ts`: `describeToolCommand()` now returns the call, `{ text, argv?, cwd?, env? }`, instead of a joined string. `text` quotes (with the shared `shellEscape()`) any argv element that is not plain, so `['a', 'b c']` and `['a', 'b', 'c']` read differently; `argv` is the array as sent; `cwd` reads `workdir`, `workDir`, `cwd`, `Cwd`, `directory`, `working_directory` and `workingDirectory`; `env` reads `env` or `environment` and keeps string, number and boolean values as strings. A list that is not an argv array (empty, a non-string element, a blank program, a hole) names no command and is never re-read as a line. `isArgv()` is shared with the label path (`Array.from` fills holes, so a sparse array is not an argv array). No label changed: `describeToolActivity()` still joins argv with plain spaces.
- `src/shared/contextDigest.ts`: `classifyRun()` takes the call and keys the run with `readCommandCall()`. `readInvocation()` replaces `isCheckInvocation()` and answers `check`, `other` or `unknown`. For `npx`, `bunx` and `pnpx` only value-less switches (`-y`, `--yes`, `--no`, `--no-install`, `-q`, `--quiet`, `--silent`, `--ignore-scripts`, `--offline`, `--prefer-offline`, `--prefer-online`) may come before the program; any other option may take the next word as its value, so the program is `unknown`. An `unknown` wrapper that mentions a check is listed under unknowns (the note now says "or behind a wrapper whose program cannot be named"); one that mentions none leaves no trace, as before. The preview shows the directory and the NAMES of added variables beside a run (`[in /repo/one, tool #1]`, `with env CI`), never a value, and the unknown list names the directory too, so two runs of one command can be told apart.
- Library surface: `describeToolCommand` is re-exported by `export *` from `maestro-lib`, and its return type changed, so `MAESTRO_LIB_VERSION` is `0.7.0` (the policy in `version.ts` asks for a bump in the same change; 05.1 added three exports without one, so this covers both) and the version line in `docs/maestro-lib.md` follows. The library builds with the new signature (see checks).
- Docs: `CLAUDE.md` index line and the three `docs/agent-guides/CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md` entries (command reader, `describeToolCommand`, digest) describe the new key, the call shape and the wrapper rule; the digest entry's "argv compared by its space-joined text" limit is gone.

### Regression cases added (all executed, see above)

- `literalCommand.test.ts` (16): the key is `canonicalJson` of `argv`, `cd` and `set`; `readCommandCall` for a line, an argv array (boundaries, equivalence with the line, display text never part of identity, no shell syntax read, `NAME=value` is a program not an assignment, empty array), the directory and variables in the key for a line, an argv array and an unreadable line, absent versus present, blank directory, variable order, empty variable set, a `cd` prefix kept apart from a call directory, and an unreadable call keyed by exact text that cannot take a readable key even when it spells that key's JSON.
- `toolActivityLabel.test.ts` (18): the call shape for every spelling, argv quoting (space, quote, `$`, glob, `;`, empty), the label left as the plain join, five non-argv lists (including a sparse one) that name no command, seven directory spellings, environment values (string, number, boolean kept; null, object, list, function, bigint and symbol skipped), and a call that names nothing has no `cwd` or `env` member.
- `contextDigest.test.ts` (40): npx forms that verify (`-y`, several switches, `bunx`); eight wrapper forms with an option in front (`--package` with a space and with `=`, `-p`, `--call`, `--workspace`, a switch then `--package`, `bunx`, `pnpx`) that are never verified and are listed as unknown with the wrapper reason; a real check behind an option is unknown, not guessed; a wrapper that mentions no check leaves no trace; a non-runner program is not verified whatever it prints; argv forms of both; an argv array is the one program it names; argument boundaries of argv arrays (both orders, identical arrays, array versus line, quoted-space line versus split array); the directory (two directories, same directory, five spellings, named versus unnamed in both orders, call directory versus `cd`, argv array, an unreadable command); variables (different, same in any order, a different value, names shown and no value, secret-shaped or plain); the directory shown beside the command and in the unknown list; directory text cleaned of backticks and line breaks; no note for a call that names nothing.

### Unresolved limits

- **A standalone `cd` in a persistent shell is not tracked.** Claude Code keeps its shell directory between calls, so `npm test` after a bare `cd packages/a` runs elsewhere with no directory in its input. The digest reads only a directory the call itself names (a `cd x &&` prefix, or `workdir`/`cwd`), so two such calls are one invocation. Tracking it would need a model of the shell session; recorded, not built.
- **A later clean run of a command that is not a check does not clear that command's earlier failure.** Only checks are recorded when they finish clean, so a failed `./deploy.sh` followed by a clean `./deploy.sh` still lists the failure (executed in the extra probe: `unresolvedFailures=1`). This is the safe direction (an extra failure, never an erased one) and is outside the three named defects, so I left it; the section title says "no later identical run recorded", which is then loose.
- **Shell wrappers are not unwrapped.** `bash -lc 'npm test'` (the shape Codex's `command_execution` uses) is a program `bash`, so it is neither verified nor listed. The proxy's original-recovery check already unwraps one literal `-c`/`-lc` layer; the digest does not share that code.
- **The npx whitelist is conservative on purpose.** `npx --package=vitest vitest run` and `npx --prefix app vitest run` are real checks that are listed as unknown, because an option other than a plain switch may take the next word. `vitest@latest` as a program is not a runner name and leaves no trace.
- **`readLiteralCommand()` has no production caller now** (the digest uses `readCommandCall()`, which shares its internal reader). It is the documented line-only reader in the canonical-utilities index and has 26 test references, so it is kept, not deleted. `isCheckInvocation()` was replaced by `readInvocation()` in the same change.
- **Variable identity is by exact value.** `env: { CI: 1 }` and `{ CI: '1' }` are the same (numbers and booleans are stringified); `CI=1 npm test` as a line and `env: { CI: '1' }` are kept apart (assignments and an environment object are different members), which can only leave a failure listed.
- **Size.** Three production files, about 146 added non-blank, non-comment lines (`contextDigest.ts` 53, `literalCommand.ts` 44, `toolActivityLabel.ts` 49) plus the one-line version bump; inside the roughly 500 lines and 1 to 3 files this playbook asks of a task.
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them). The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

## 05.2: Explicit continuity preview of one tab (desktop operation and CLI; in-app surface split to 05.2b)

Execution date: 2026-11-12. Status: complete for the scope below, preview only. The in-app surface (menu item, palette entry, modal) is the new checkbox 05.2b. Selected the first unchecked task in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-05.md`. Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`; HEAD `9227c59e3f` at the start; tracked tree clean before edits; `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` untouched. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin`. The supervising session edited this checkout while the task ran: it refined the 05.2b checkbox I added (a mock-layout step before the UI work) and changed this ledger's Status line. Both were re-read and kept.

Read the execution contract, the analysis (T13, T14, the Continuity row), this ledger (05.1 and 05.1b included), `CLAUDE.md`, the CLI/UI parity, canonical-utilities and renderer-services guides, and the code of `useSummarizeAndContinue`, `contextSummarizer`, `tabContextOps` (shared and renderer), the WebSocket context handlers and their preload and callback hops, the History read, `providerSwap` (`NativeSessionBinding`, `matchesNativeSessionBinding`) and `native-records` / `context-window`.

**What the task's words became.**

- _"The existing explicit summarize/continue preview"_ does not exist: Compact runs at once, and Merge Into and Send to Agent show only a token preview. The preview is therefore its own explicit action, `useSummarizeAndContinue().previewContinuity(sessionId, tabId)`, reached remotely by `maestro-cli tab continuity`.
- _"Current activity and synopsis gates"_: the digest's own (a call that never ended is unknown; `isFailedSynopsisResponse`, `parseSynopsis` and nothing-to-report keep a bad synopsis out), the tab's busy state, and the History gates below.
- _"Canonical prompt delivery"_: nothing is delivered. The digest is shown, never sent. `src/__tests__/shared/continuityReachability.test.ts` lists exactly which production files may import the digest or the preview or name the entry points, and names 19 forwarding, compaction, system-prompt, session-start and Cue paths that must stay free of them. A new caller fails the test until someone reads why it wants the digest.
- _"Native active/archived projections only where identity is proven"_: **not used, by decision (D3).**
- _"Do not auto-compact, rewrite native transcripts, restore an old task into another tab, change the system prompt silently or start provider turns"_: the preview reads the tab and one History page. It writes no log entry, creates no tab, changes no selection, raises no toast, spawns nothing (tested through the store, the process mock and the toast mock).

**Split.** The in-app surface alone is 12 or more files (overlay menu and `TabBar` props, palette, `AppModals`, `modalStore`, priorities). The playbook asks for 1 to 3 files per checkbox and says to split first, so 05.2b was added as an explicit unchecked checkbox before any code. The parity guide records the gap in "Continuity preview: remaining gap". A person cannot click to the preview until 05.2b lands; an agent or a person can run `maestro-cli tab continuity`.

**Decisions.**

- **D1. Its own message, never a flag.** `continuity_preview` / `continuity_preview_result` (`TabContextOp` `'continuity'`). A desktop that predates it ignores a field it does not know, so a preview asked for with a flag on `summarize_context` would start a real compaction (a provider turn) there. A test pins that `summarize_context` with `preview: true` still parses as a compaction request, so no flag can be mistaken for the preview. An older desktop answers the new message with an `echo` the CLI does not wait on; the CLI times out at 30 s plus a margin and says nothing was changed (it does not say "may still be running", which is true of a compaction only).
- **D2. The gates live in a pure module.** `src/shared/continuityPreview.ts`, `planContinuityPreview()`. The digest trusts its caller that the entries are one conversation; this is the caller's side of that promise. Gates: the tab read must be the tab and agent asked about; a consult tab and a document chat are refused; the digest is built from the tab's own entries only; a tab with no entries gets an empty digest that says so and History is **not** read to fill it in. A History synopsis is admitted only as an interactive (`USER`) turn that did not fail, owned by this agent, in the provider session the tab is bound to, on the tab's provider, and tied to the tab by `matchesNativeSessionBinding` (account, host and directory, not a session id alone) or, only where an owner record is missing on one side, by the same tab id. A bound owner that differs is a refusal, never a fallback. A synopsis older than the first entry the tab holds is refused (cleared or trimmed away); one older than the last completed compaction is shown and marked. Every refusal is counted by reason and named in a notice.
- **D3. Native projections are not used.** `extractNativeConversation()` needs the provider transcript bytes (`Buffer`, `node:path`), so only the main process or the headless host can compute a projection; the renderer has no IPC that returns one with its owner record, and adding a main-process transcript read for one line of a preview is not warranted (the main-process heap-out-of-memory fix in #1862 traced to full transcript reads). No identity-proven projection reaches the preview, so none is used. The preview says the provider's window is unknown ("a compaction the provider did by itself is not visible in Maestro's transcript") and claims no active or archived count; a test asserts that for a bound tab, an unbound tab and an empty tab.
- **D4. Untrusted text is made inert.** New `stripControlCharacters()` in `stringUtils.ts` (C0 except tab and line feed, DEL, C1, the bidirectional overrides and isolates; CR reads as a line break). The digest now cleans every string it shows (`safeText`) **before** redaction, so a control character cannot hide inside a secret. A synopsis id from a History file other tools can write is cleaned before it becomes a source label. The receiver cleans the wire preview again (`normalizeTabContextOpResult`) and so does the CLI before it prints, because the sender may be another build. Excerpts are model text: the preview is plain text and 05.2b must show it in a `<pre>`, never as Markdown.
- **D5. The stored window.** Maestro stores the newest 100 entries of a tab (`MAX_PERSISTED_SESSION_LOGS`), so a restored tab can hold only the tail of its conversation and the "objective" is then the first request still held. Found by running the planner over this machine's own stores (below). The preview now says so from 100 entries up, and a synopsis of the missing early work is refused as older than the first entry held, the same way as one from a cleared tab. The transcript window is measured from the oldest timestamp, not the first array element, because a merged or forked tab need not be in order.

**Files.**

- `src/shared/continuityPreview.ts` (new, pure; 280 non-blank, non-comment lines): `planContinuityPreview()`, `continuityWire()`, the input and result types, `SynopsisRefusal`.
- `src/shared/contextDigest.ts`: control characters stripped in `safeText()`; a synopsis id cleaned in its label; an optional `beforeCompaction` flag on a synopsis, shown as "before the last compaction".
- `src/shared/stringUtils.ts`: `stripControlCharacters()`.
- `src/shared/tabContextOps.ts`: the `'continuity'` op, `TabContinuityPreview`, `normalizeContinuity()` (bounded, cleaned, malformed shapes dropped), `namesSessionId()`, `MAX_CONTINUITY_TEXT_CHARS`.
- Main: `handleContinuityPreview` and its `case`, the `remote:continuityPreview` channel in `contextOpsCallbacks.ts`, `onRemoteContinuityPreview` and its reply sender in the preload, typings in `global.d.ts`.
- Renderer: `previewContinuity()` and `readContinuityHistory()` on `contextSummarizationService` (the first pure, the second the one expected-failure catch, around the IPC call only); `previewContinuity()` on `useSummarizeAndContinue` (resolves the tab, reads History, then reads the transcript **again** so it is at least as new as History, refuses if the tab closed meanwhile); the `continuity` branch of `runRemoteTabContextOp()`; the subscription in `useRemoteIntegration`; App passes the function to the remote listener.
- CLI: `tabContinuity()` in `tab.ts`, the `tab continuity [tab-id]` registration and its example (`--json` adds counts, notices and unknowns beside the text).
- Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (two index lines), `CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md` (two full entries), `RENDERER-SERVICES.md`, `CLI-UI-PARITY.md` ("Continuity preview: remaining gap"), `cli-reference.md`, `cli.md`.
- Reused unchanged: `buildContextDigest`, `matchesNativeSessionBinding`, `MAX_PERSISTED_SESSION_LOGS`, `buildSharedHistoryContext`, `window.maestro.history.getAllPaginated` (the History panel's read), `selectSessionById`, `resolveTabProvider`, `isAgentDormant`, `normalizeTabContextOpResult`, `withMaestroClient`, `redactSecrets`.

LSP was exposed as a deferred tool and used. `findReferences` on `previewContinuity` of the service returned 5 references in 3 files (service, hook, tests), as expected. ### Regression cases added (all executed, see above)

- **Cross-tab rejection.** A tab that is not the one asked for (tab and agent); a consult tab; a document chat; an id with control characters in a refusal; a tab of another agent, an unknown agent and a closed tab (the hook reads no History for them); one History matrix, one row per reason (Auto Run, Cue, consult and relay entries, a failed turn, no owner, another agent, no session, another session, another provider, neither tab nor owner, another tab), each asserting the refused text appears nowhere in the output; bound owners that differ even when the tab id matches; a bound entry against an unbound tab falling back to the tab id, and not when the tab differs; a matching owner record accepted although the tab id moved; every entry refused for a tab with no provider session; a rule sentence from another tab never becoming a constraint; a duplicate counted once; counts by reason in one notice; the hook preferring the tab asked for over the active one.
- **Cleared and compacted history.** An empty tab reads no History at all, however well it matches, and says so; a cleared tab keeps synopses written after the clear and drops those before it (also at the hook, through the store); equality at the boundary; no time gate when entries carry no time; the oldest timestamp, not the first element; the 100-entry warning at 99, 100 and 180; a synopsis marked before a completed compaction and not after it; a pending compaction does not mark and is reported unknown; a running turn is flagged; History unreadable and History read in part.
- **Untrusted excerpt provenance.** An instruction-like synopsis appears only once, under the "written by a model, not verified" heading, and never as a constraint, pending item or next action; each excerpt names its History entry in the sources; tool output and assistant text never become constraints; escapes, OSC titles, CR, NUL, BEL and bidirectional overrides removed from user text, tool commands, errors, synopsis text and synopsis ids (digest level and preview level), and a control character hidden inside a secret does not stop its redaction; a provider error is dropped by the provider's own bank (Claude and Codex differ); the wire normalizer re-cleans, bounds and drops malformed previews (16 cases); the CLI re-cleans what it prints.
- **No automatic injection.** The reachability test (26 cases); the summarization prompt carries no continuity text and `summarizeContext` reads no History; the preview calls no turn, no process, no toast, writes nothing to the store, leaves the selected agent and tab, and makes exactly one IPC call.

### Unresolved limits

- **No in-app surface yet (05.2b).** A person cannot click to the preview. The reachable paths are `maestro-cli tab continuity` and the hook function 05.2b will call. Resolved by 05.2b (below).
- **No native projection (D3).** The preview cannot tell what the provider still holds. A compaction the provider did on its own is invisible to it, and the preview says so instead of guessing.
- **Provider switches.** A tab that changed provider keeps the older provider's synopses out (they belong to another session); matching a parked slot was not built.
- **The stored window (D5).** From 100 entries up the preview warns; below that it cannot know a tab was never trimmed. Refusing a synopsis as "older than the transcript" treats a trimmed tab like a cleared one, which hides legitimate early synopses (counted and named, never silent). Maestro cannot tell the two apart from the tab alone.
- **Legacy History.** An entry with neither a tab id nor an owner record is unproven; 8,734 were kept out on this machine, so older conversations show fewer excerpts than they have.
- **History window.** The newest 300 interactive entries of the agent are read; a busy agent can push a quiet tab's synopses out (the preview says it read a window).
- **Mixed versions.** A CLI newer than the desktop gets no answer and times out with a message that says nothing was changed. A desktop newer than the CLI is unaffected.
- **The digest's own limits** from 05.1 and 05.1b are unchanged (what can be verified is narrow on purpose; wrappers are not unwrapped; a standalone `cd` is not tracked).
- **Size.** 16 tracked production files changed (+387, -32 lines, about a third of it comments) plus one new module (280 non-blank, non-comment lines), over the 1 to 3 files and about 500 lines the playbook asks of a task. The logic is three files (planner, service, hook); the rest is the wire the CLI needs (12 files of mechanical plumbing that every existing context operation also has). Splitting the UI out (05.2b) is what keeps it from being about 30. Tests add 635 tracked lines and three new files (1,183 lines).
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them). The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

## Supervisor repair: immediate proxy-setting persistence failures, 2026-11-12

Reproduced three failures in `agentGateway.test.ts`: a rejected `sessions.setMany` still returned success for compression and off, and a failed blanket-default write produced no warning. `flushAgentToStore` now accepts an optional required-write mode; its default remains best effort for existing callers. `setAgentTokenProxyMode` requires a confirmed main-process write, including an unchanged selection so a retry after failure actually writes again. The renderer retains the intended setting for its existing debounced persistence; a failure is no longer reported as applied. `applyDefaultAgentTokenProxy` uses its existing warning path. This does not change live settings.

Checks: red regression run 3 failed / 66 passed; repaired service and remote/group writer run 143 passed in 3 files (`supervisor-persistence-green.log`); existing group/default/creation/delegation/System One caller run 175 passed in 5 files (`supervisor-persistence-callers.log`). `npm run lint` passed all four configurations; targeted ESLint, Prettier and `git diff --check` passed. Fresh TypeScript LSP resolved `flushAgentToStore` and six references; production syntactic and semantic diagnostics were empty (`supervisor-persistence-lsp.log`). The test file is outside the production tsconfigs; its inferred-project LSP diagnostics include existing ambient-window and legacy fixture issues, so no clean test-file LSP claim is made.

## 05.2b: In-app Context: Preview Continuity (tab menu, tiled pane menu, palette, read-only modal)

Execution date: 2026-11-12. Status: complete, preview only. A read-only window onto the 05.2 preview; no savings are credited. Selected the first unchecked task in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-05.md`. Images analyzed: 0 supplied (neither the task nor the document references any); I rasterized and looked at the two SVG mocks I drew (2 views). Branch `acappella`; HEAD `3dd8825623` at the start; tracked tree clean before edits; `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` untouched. Read the execution contract, this ledger (05.2 included), `CLAUDE.md`, the UI-patterns "Every Surface Needs Three Ways In" section, `CLI-UI-PARITY.md`, and the code of `Modal`, the tab overlay menu chain, the tiled pane menu, the palette builders, `modalStore`, and the Snooze Tab and Model & Effort modals (the closest precedents).

**Mock layouts (drawn before any renderer change).** Two SVG options in the existing modal style (Dracula tokens, the real `Modal` anatomy: header with a dim subject and X, content, footer with Copy and Close), data marked illustrative in a banner. Both rasterized and checked: nothing clipped.

- A, the text only: `/Users/pedram/Projects/Maestro-WorkTrees/acappella/.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/05.2b-mock-A-text-only.svg` (render: `.../05.2b-render/05.2b-mock-A-text-only.png`).
- B, a count strip above the text: `/Users/pedram/Projects/Maestro-WorkTrees/acappella/.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/05.2b-mock-B-count-strip.svg` (render: `.../05.2b-render/05.2b-mock-B-count-strip.png`).

**Recommended and implemented: A.** The preview text already holds the notices, the refused-by-reason sentence ("N History entries were not used, by reason (...)") and the "Unknown:" list, and `maestro-cli tab continuity` prints exactly that text. B draws the same numbers a second time from the structured fields, so the window could one day disagree with the CLI; A cannot. (`.maestro/` is ignored, so the mocks are not in the commit.)

**Decisions.**

- **D1. The Snooze Tab / Model & Effort precedent, not the Merge Into / Send to Agent chain.** Those two switch to the tab and then open a modal on the _active_ tab, through App-level handlers and `AppModals` props (about 12 more files). The preview carries `{ sessionId, tabId }` in the modal store (`openModal('continuityPreview', ...)`), so it previews the tab it was opened for, selects nothing, and needs no App, `useMainPanelProps` or `AppModals` prop. The menu opener lives in `MainPanel` (like `handleOpenSnooze`), the palette opens the store directly (like `snoozeTab`), and `AppUtilityModals` renders it from the store with only the theme.
- **D2. One function, one normalizer.** The modal calls `useSummarizeAndContinue().previewContinuity` itself (the hook has no effects and keeps its state in stores) and reads the answer through `normalizeContinuity()`, now exported from `src/shared/tabContextOps.ts`: the normalizer `maestro-cli tab continuity` already reaches through `normalizeTabContextOpResult`. An integration test asserts the modal's text equals the text the CLI path prints.
- **D3. Plain text, nothing kept, two controls.** The text is one string in a `<pre>` (no element is created from it). It lives in the modal's own state and goes with the modal: nothing in a store, a log, a tab or the queue. The only buttons are Copy (the shared clipboard helper and the standard "Copied" flash) and Close, plus the header X, Escape and a backdrop click.
- **D4. Errors.** A refusal is a result and is shown as plain text (control characters removed). A rejection is a bug: it goes to `captureException()` and the modal shows a generic failure, so it never waits on "Reading the tab..." forever.
- **D5. No hotkey, no `UI_SURFACES` entry.** Compact, Merge Into and Send to Agent are the sibling tab-scoped Context actions and have neither; `UI_SURFACES` feeds `maestro-cli open <surface>` and `open_modal`, which cannot name a tab (the CLI path is `tab continuity`). The three-ways-in rule is met by the tab menu, the tiled pane menu and the palette. `paletteShortcutCoverage.test.ts` fails on a shortcut with no palette entry, not the reverse, and stays green. `docs/keyboard-shortcuts.md` is unchanged.
- **D6. Gates.** The tab menu and the palette need one entry to read (the same as Merge Into) and borrow no compaction or capability gate: the preview asks no provider anything. The tiled pane menu is ungated like its siblings. MainPanel documents that menu as mirroring the chip menu, so it gets the item too.
- **D7. The 05.2 reachability guard did its job.** It failed on two new files that name `previewContinuity`. The modal is a legitimate explicit caller and was added to the list on purpose. The palette file matched only through my command id, so the id was renamed `continuityPreview` (the modal's id) rather than loosening the guard. The guard gained four cases: the modal may be opened only from `MainPanel.tsx` and the palette builder; the store's modal data carries a tab and never a preview; the modal has no send, compact, merge, spawn, toast or session-write symbol; and no Markdown renderer or raw HTML.
- **D8. Priority 871.** `CONTINUITY_PREVIEW`, just below `MODEL_EFFORT` (872): another per-tab dialog, with nothing opening on top of it. It has a `resizeKey` (`modal-continuity-preview`), `closeOnBackdropClick`, and no `select-none` (a reading surface, so the `<pre>` is selectable).

### Regression cases added (all executed, see above)

- **Modal, with the hook mocked (26).** Asks once for the opened tab; loading, then exactly the text; the tab name in the header; the notices, refused counts and Unknown list in the text; no second ask on re-render. Plain text: hostile Markdown, HTML, an image, a script and a code fence create no element and run nothing; control characters (escapes, bidirectional override, CR, BEL) are removed; a refusal reason is plain text. Refusal, no reason, an answer with no preview, a malformed preview dropped whole, and a rejection reported to Sentry with a failure shown. A late answer dropped after close and after a move to another tab. Copy copies exactly the text, flashes only on success, and is disabled while loading or refused. Escape, the X, the footer Close and the backdrop each close once. Only Copy, Close and the X exist (no send, continue, compact or merge); no editable field; the session store is untouched.
- **Modal, real hook, planner and History read (11).** The text equals what `maestro-cli tab continuity` prints for the same tab; user constraints come before excerpts; the refused-by-reason counts (another tab, a failed turn) are shown and the refused text is not; the Unknown list and the provider-window notice; a cleared tab is not refilled from History; a consult tab is refused as plain text; the opened tab, not the active one; an instruction inside an excerpt stays under the model-written heading; and what it leaves alone: no turn, no compaction, no toast, no spawn or write, one History read, the same sessions array, the selected agent and tab, the draft and the queue unchanged, and nothing kept after unmount.
- **Menu (7), palette (9), host (4), wiring (7), guard (4).** Menu: shown with one entry, hidden without the handler or without entries, no compaction gate, adjacent to Compact and before Merge Into and Send to Agent, calls the handler with the tab id only and closes, previews the tab the menu was opened on and selects nothing. Palette: offered for an AI tab with an entry and no hotkey, right after Compact, no borrowed gate, not on file, terminal or browser tabs, not without entries or an agent, closes the palette first and opens for the active tab, stores the tab and nothing else. Host: nothing until opened, opens with the theme and the target, closes through the store, one modal that remounts for a second tab. Wiring (source level, as the unread-props test beside it): both `<TabBar>` sites, the pane actions and their dependency list, the opener selects nothing, the pane type and item and its place before Merge Into and in the divider rule. Guard: D7.

### Unresolved limits

- **Not drawn in a running desktop app.** The surface is proven through the component, store, host and wiring tests with the real hook and planner, and the SVG mock; layout fidelity rests on reusing `Modal` and `ModalFooter`. The final phase owns the packaged build.
- **No hotkey and no `maestro-cli open` entry, by decision (D5).** The keyboard path is the palette; the script path is `tab continuity`.
- **The tiled pane menu is not gated on entries** (like its siblings), so an empty pane opens a preview that says the tab has no transcript entries.
- **Dev builds read twice.** The renderer mounts `React.StrictMode`, which runs the effect twice in development: one extra, read-only History read whose answer is dropped. Production builds read once.
- **The 05.2 limits are unchanged** (no native projection of the provider's window, the 100-entry stored window, legacy History without an owner, the newest 300 History entries). The preview text is bounded at 64,000 characters by the normalizer, with a visible cut line.
- **Size.** 12 production files against the 1 to 3 the playbook asks of a task. The split was made in 05.2 to keep the wiring apart from the logic, and D1 kept it at 12 instead of about 24. Tests add three new files (835 lines) and 405 lines in four existing ones.
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them). The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

## Supervisor review during 05.3: configured text is not observed delivery

The current draft rebuilds the Maestro system prompt from current settings in `agent-footprint.ts`, and passes saved New Session/Nudge strings. `collectContextFootprint()` puts these into its loaded group; `formatContextFootprint()` then prints KNOWN LOADED. There is no bound delivery or current-provider-window evidence. The isolated synthetic probe `footprint-delivery-review.cjs` uses remote=true (no filesystem reads), a freshly assembled prompt, a first-message setting on a resumed tab, and a nudge changed after launch. It exits 1 and reports claimsKnownLoaded=true with actualDeliveryEvidence=false (`supervisor-footprint-delivery-red.log`). No private text or provider call is used.

Task 05.3b is inserted before integration to correct the JSON/human evidence contract, preserve unknown delivery/window and contribution frequency, and add executed regressions. Run the same probe after the repair; do not treat current settings or a rebuild as proof of prior delivery. If the current 05.3 worker already fixes this finding, 05.3b should verify that result without duplicating implementation.

Supervisor follow-up during 05.3: the worker corrected the draft before closure. Fresh independent execution of `footprint-delivery-review.cjs` now exits 0 (`supervisor-footprint-delivery-verified.log`). Source review confirms a `configured.contributions` group, `basis=configured-now`, explicit delivery/window records defaulting to unknown, and no combined configured-window total. The CLI heading no longer claims known loaded. Task 05.3b can verify the already-made repair and its tracked regression coverage; it must not recreate it.

## 05.3: Context footprint audit (read only, on demand)

Execution date: 2026-11-12. Status: complete, observation only. The audit credits no saving and measures nothing a provider loaded. Selected the first unchecked task in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-05.md`. Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`; HEAD `efe1ddc60f` at the start; tracked tree clean before edits; `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` untouched. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin` (the shell default is v26.12.1). The supervising session reviewed the draft while the task ran and appended two paragraphs to this ledger and the 05.3b checkbox to the playbook (below). Both were re-read and kept.

Read the execution contract, the analysis (T18 and the Existing foundation table), this ledger (05.2 and 05.2b included), `CLAUDE.md`, `CLI-PLAYBOOKS.md` (adding a verb), `CLI-UI-PARITY.md`, `CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md`, and the code of `memory-manager`, `memory-location`, `translation/resources`, `translation/memory`, the Cue bundle `cue-bundle-claude-assets` (`scrubMcpConfig`), `claude:getSkills` and `readClaudeSkillDescriptions`, `discoverCodexSkills`, the CLI and shared system prompt builders, `list-sessions` and the verb registration.

**What the task's words became.**

- _"Actually available loaded-component or inventory evidence"_: the inventory is real (a `stat` of named files). **Loaded-component evidence does not exist in Maestro's stores**: a provider's `init` event lists command names, Maestro keeps no per-component record, and nothing records what a provider put in a prompt. The types carry a slot for a record bound to a launch or session (`boundTo`); the verb supplies none, so every activation, delivery and window is `unknown`.
- _"Reusing existing discovery"_: instruction names and project skill roots (`INSTRUCTION_FILE_NAMES`, `PROJECT_SKILL_ROOTS`, exported from `translation/resources.ts` where `collectTranslationResources` already used them); `.mcp.json` server names (`scrubMcpConfig`, whose secret scrubbing means a credential is never in play); the memory store (`memoryLayoutFor`, `listMemoryEntries`, and `resolveProviderMemoryLayout`, extracted from the Memory Viewer's resolver so the viewer and the verb find the same store, worktrees included); the account dir (`resolveAgentAccountDir`, through a CLI wrapper `list-sessions` now shares); the Maestro prompt (`prepareMaestroSystemPromptCli`); the token rule (`estimateTokenCount`, with a byte form beside it). **Not reused, with the reason:** `collectTranslationResources` (opens whole files under a translation identity), `collectClaudeAssets` (reads and redacts whole files into an archive), `discoverCodexSkills` (starts a Codex process), `claude:getSkills` and `readClaudeSkillDescriptions` (closures inside IPC handlers, reading file bodies), and the Codex MCP parser (embedded in `handle-spawn`). The audit therefore holds a fourth, `stat`-only skill scan; consolidating the other three is a separate cleanup.
- _"Report file-size/token estimates separately from known loaded prompt contributions; missing activation is unknown"_: see D5. There are no known loaded contributions, so the section is **Maestro-added text, as configured now**.
- _"On demand, no recurring scanner"_: one verb, run when asked. A guard test fails on a timer, watcher, schedule, module-level cache or background spawn.
- _"Do not scan unrelated project/private scopes or edit configs"_: the collector reads a fixed list (below) through a port typed as `Pick<TranslationFs, 'stat' | 'readdir' | 'readFile' | 'realpath'>`, and a guard test fails on any name that writes, removes, moves or runs.

**Decisions.**

- **D1. A CLI verb, no UI.** `maestro-cli agent footprint [agent-id] [--json]`. The audit is agent-scoped (project, provider, account), it reads local files, and it needs no running app, as `delegation graph` does. Without an argument it uses the agent it runs under (`MAESTRO_CALLER_AGENT_ID`). Phase 07.4 owns a Models view; the parity guide records the verb as a read-only agent surface with no UI counterpart yet.
- **D2. A pure core, a bounded collector, and resolvers the caller supplies.** `auditContextFootprint()` is a function of its input. `collectContextFootprint()` reads only: the project root (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, `.mcp.json`, one folder per skill under four project skill roots), the account dir the caller resolved (the same instruction names and `skills/`), and the memory store the caller resolved. Only `.mcp.json` is opened. Limits: 500 skill folders per root, 500 memory entries, a 1 MiB `.mcp.json`; each cut is a note. A relative root and an SSH agent read nothing. `EACCES` and `EPERM` become a note; any other failure is rethrown.
- **D3. Candidates are places to look.** The largest inventory components of 1,000 estimated tokens or more, at most ten, ranked by size then id, each with path, finder, basis `file-size` and activation. An MCP server has no size (its tool definitions are not in its config) and so is never a candidate. Configured text is never a candidate. The threshold and the limit are heuristics, stated in the report.
- **D4. Nothing is a saving, in both directions.** `accounting.countsAsSavings` is the literal `false`. `contextFootprintReachability.test.ts` fails when a counter (the proxy folder, `stats/proxy-requests`, `stats/model-usage`, the token-proxy and model-usage shared types, `models-proxy`, the Usage Dashboard) imports the audit, when the audit imports one or names one of their symbols, and when the shared source speaks of savings other than to deny them.
- **D5. The supervisor's delivery-evidence finding, fixed inside 05.3.** The draft printed "KNOWN LOADED: text Maestro delivers, measured" for a system prompt rebuilt now and for saved New Session and Nudge settings, with one total across them. A rebuild or a setting is not a record of what any launch carried: the setting may have changed since, a resumed session does not get the first-message text again, and Maestro keeps no record of what it sent. The contract is now: `configured.contributions`, each with `basis: 'configured-now'`, its own one-occurrence size, its frequency (`each-spawn`, `first-message`, `every-message`), and `delivery` and `window` records that are `unknown` unless a caller supplies a record with `boundTo` (the launch or session it is about). There is **no total** over the additions and no `loaded` key. The report says "delivery unknown window unknown" on every line and that the additions "are not added together". The same `boundTo` rule governs an inventory component's `activation`. The supervisor's probe `footprint-delivery-review.cjs` went from exit 1 (`claimsKnownLoaded: true`) on the draft to exit 0 (`claimsKnownLoaded: false`) on the repair.
- **D6. An empty memory section is never silent.** A store that does not exist adds a note naming the path it looked at (`memory-missing`); a provider with no store, or no account dir, adds `memory-not-read`. Found while smoke testing: a project inside another repository maps to that repository's main checkout, exactly as the Memory Viewer does.
- **D7. The audit does not ask the desktop.** The Maestro prompt is built with a reader that answers "no answer", so the System One section, whose presence depends on desktop state, is left out and the report says so (`maestro-prompt-partial`). The measured prompt is the one `maestro-cli send` would build, not necessarily the one the desktop builds.

**Files (production, 12; about 800 non-comment lines added, 40 removed).**

- New: `src/shared/contextFootprint.ts` (309 lines): types, `auditContextFootprint()`, `formatContextFootprint()`. `src/main/context-footprint.ts` (314): `collectContextFootprint()`, `FootprintFs`, `FOOTPRINT_LIMITS`. `src/cli/commands/agent-footprint.ts` (81): the verb. `src/cli/services/agent-account-dir.ts` (23): `resolveCliAgentAccountDir()`.
- Reuse extractions and wiring: `src/shared/formatters.ts` (`estimateTokensFromBytes`, +3), `src/shared/maestro-lib/translation/resources.ts` (`INSTRUCTION_FILE_NAMES`, `PROJECT_SKILL_ROOTS`, +9 -2), `src/main/memory-manager.ts` (`resolveProviderMemoryLayout`, +25), `src/main/memory-location.ts` (calls it, +8 -14), `src/cli/services/git-utils.ts` (`getGitCommonDir`, +12), `src/cli/commands/list-sessions.ts` (calls the shared wrapper, +3 -24), `src/cli/index.ts` (+8), `src/cli/command-examples.ts` (+4).
- Docs: `CLAUDE.md` (three new index lines, two extended), `CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md` (the matching full entries), `CLI-UI-PARITY.md` (one row; the 05.2b row reworded, see Checks), `cli.md` (a section), `cli-reference.md` (one hand-edited entry; the file is generated and regeneration destroys edits), `context-management.md` (a short section).

LSP was exposed as a deferred tool and used: `findReferences` on `resolveProviderMemoryLayout` (5 references in 3 files: the definition, `memory-location`, the verb), on `INSTRUCTION_FILE_NAMES` (5 in 2 files: `resources.ts` and the collector) and on `resolveCliAgentAccountDir` (9 in 4 files: the definition, `list-sessions`, the verb, the verb's test). **A failure that was not mine.** `parity-doc.test.ts` failed once: the committed 05.2b row ("Context: Preview Continuity") said "has no `--background`", which the guard reads as a flag the row's command must declare. It failed at HEAD, was outside the 05.2b related-test selection, and sits in a document this task edits, so I reworded it to "takes no background flag" (no other change to that row).

### Regression cases added (all executed, see above)

- **Estimates stay estimates (core, 39).** A size becomes tokens at four bytes a token; configured text from its length; the inventory total exists and the configured section has none; configured text is kept as a size only; a component with a bound record stays in the inventory; unknown activation is never recorded as "not loaded"; the standing notes count only the components without a record and drop away when every one has one.
- **Configured text is not delivery (core, collector and verb).** A prompt assembled just now (delivery and window unknown, no "delivered" in the line); a nudge saved after a launch (sized as it is now, with the note that the launch does not hold it); a first-message setting (named "first message of a new session", with the note that a resumed session does not get it again); the three additions never added, in the data or the report; a record bound to a launch shown on its own line and nowhere else; the "delivery unknown" note dropped when every addition has a record; no `known...` key, no window total, no `loaded` key; the supervisor probe's exact input (a remote agent, a rebuilt prompt, a first-message text, a changed nudge) at the collector and the verb.
- **Candidates and rollups (core).** Threshold at and one token below; ranking, id tie-break and the omitted count; provenance, basis and activation on each; an unmeasured MCP server is never a candidate; configured text is never one; kind order, sort order, determinism under reordering, frozen input, shared state, ids.
- **Scope and read-only (collector, 31).** The fixed list of reads, proven by a recording port against a tree that holds a parent-folder instruction file, a subfolder one, `CLAUDE.local.md`, the account's `settings.json` and `.claude.json`, and another project's memory; the only file opened is `.mcp.json`; the whole fixture identical before and after (files, digests, mtimes); no timer, nothing kept between runs; an SSH agent and a relative root read nothing; `EACCES` becomes a note and `EIO` is rethrown; the three limits and their notes; a link and its target counted once with the alias kept; dot folders and non-skill folders skipped; `.mcp.json` malformed, oversized, and carrying a credential that never reaches the report; memory index apart from entries, a Codex nested store as account memory, a missing store named by path, a refused provider named by reason.
- **The verb (17).** Resolution by argument, by caller env and by neither; an unknown or ambiguous agent; JSON and text; the project root over the working directory; a worktree agent's store found under its main checkout; no memory for a provider with no account dir; an SSH agent; the prompt built without asking the desktop; a builder failure exits without a partial report.
- **Reachability and read-only guard (17).** Who may import the rules and the collector; the callers allow-list; no counter imports the audit and the audit imports none and names none of their symbols; no write, remove, move or run name; the port is a `Pick` of read methods; the rules import only `formatters`; no connection to the desktop; no timer, watcher or schedule; no module-level cache; no Cue, playbook or settings entry runs it; no "known loaded" and no window total anywhere in the three files.
- **Memory resolver and estimator (memory-manager 5, formatters 3).** Default key, worktree key by the main checkout, `autoMemoryDirectory`, a per-account store never asking git, a refused provider with the viewer's own message; the byte estimator against the text estimator, zero and impossible sizes, and non-ASCII leaning high.

### Unresolved limits

- **No loaded-component evidence is wired.** Every activation, delivery and window is `unknown`. A real source would be a record bound to a launch or session (a provider's `init` list kept per session, or a launch record). Maestro keeps neither today, and adding a tracker is outside this task.
- **MCP is project `.mcp.json` only.** User-level servers and other providers' MCP config are not read, and no server has a size: a provider loads tool definitions the config does not contain. The report says both.
- **Skills are sized by `SKILL.md`.** Which part a provider keeps in context (a listing, the body on use) is provider behavior Maestro has not verified, so the audit claims neither; all four project skill roots are listed for every provider, whichever one reads them.
- **Not audited, and stated in every report:** instruction files in parent folders or subfolders, `CLAUDE.local.md`, hook output, the provider's own prompt and tool definitions.
- **The measured Maestro prompt is `maestro-cli send`'s, without System One** (D7).
- **Token figures are bytes over four.** They lean high for non-ASCII text. The candidate threshold (1,000 tokens, ten items) is a heuristic.
- **Symbolic links are followed for size only**, one level deep; nothing is enumerated through them.
- **Three other skill scanners remain** (`claude:getSkills`, `readClaudeSkillDescriptions`, the Cue bundle walker). They differ in what they read and where; consolidating them is a separate cleanup.
- **No UI.** Phase 07.4 owns a view; until then the verb is the only way in.
- **Size.** 12 production files and about 800 non-comment lines added, against the 1 to 3 files and under 500 lines the playbook asks. The logic is three files (core 309, collector 314, verb 81); the rest is four reuse extractions that stop a duplicate of the Memory Viewer's resolver and of `list-sessions`' account lookup (about 65 lines), the wiring, and the new wrapper. I did not split finished code. Tests add four new files (2,009 lines) and 101 lines in two existing ones.
- **05.3b** (the supervisor's checkbox for the delivery-evidence finding) needs only verification: run the probe (exit 0 now), and read the tests listed under "Configured text is not delivery". I did not mark it.
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them). The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

## Supervisor review during 05.4: configured routing must not claim unsupported dispatch

Independent synthetic `routing-review.cjs` on the current draft exits 1: `describeContinuationRouting({toolType: 'codex', backgroundRequests: {target: {type: 'source', endpointId: 'synthetic-unavailable', model: 'synthetic'}}})` returns backgroundRequests.status=routed even though canonical `backgroundRequestsSupport('codex')` refuses it (`supervisor-routing-red.log`). The draft checks whether a configured target exists before honoring the support result. Correct this within 05.4, with a tracked regression and the independent probe passing. Invalid or stale settings must not bypass the canonical provider capability gate.

Also keep the scope of routing statements precise: this helper reads current settings, not a dry-run launch or runtime delivery record. It does not receive the agent's gateway/proxy state, endpoint enabled/capability state, or a launch result, so a configured target alone cannot establish that a side request WILL be sent there. Prefer configured-target wording and explicit unverified availability where effective dispatch is not established. Do not build a parallel router or perform provider/network probes for this report. Resolve the finding before checking 05.4 complete; if the worker already corrects it during its own review, record that evidence rather than duplicating work.

Supervisor 05.4 repetition finding: `signals-review.cjs` proves a false duplicate-read observation in the current draft. Three identical `Read` calls returning `" x"`, `"x"`, `"x "` produce one repeated-result signal, although the stored outputs differ. `readTool()` trims output before comparison. The exact-identical positive control and distinct-content negative control pass; the whitespace-change case fails (`supervisor-signals-red.log`, exit 1). Preserve actual stored output for exact result identity, keeping display sanitization separate; whitespace can be meaningful in source files. Add tracked regressions and require the same three-case independent probe to pass before closing 05.4. This is observation accuracy, not permission to alter tool output or forwarding.

## 05.4: Loop, churn and continuity readiness signals (observation only)

Execution date: 2026-11-12. Status: complete, observation only. Nothing here blocks a call, changes a prompt, switches a model, sends anything or keeps a cache warm, and no number in it is a score, a probability or a saving. Selected the first unchecked task in `TOKEN-OPTIMIZATION-05.md`. Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`; HEAD `c55233ca1d` at the start; tracked tree clean before edits; `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` untouched. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin`. The supervising session appended two review findings and a follow-up to this ledger while the task ran (see "Supervisor findings"); both findings were real and are repaired with tracked cases, and both of its independent probes exit 0.

Read the execution contract, the analysis (T12, T15, T16, T17, T19, T22), this ledger (05.1 to 05.3b and the supervisor's notes), `CLAUDE.md` with its canonical-utilities index, the `CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md` entries for the digest, the preview, tool labels and control characters, the `CLI-UI-PARITY.md` row, and the code of `contextDigest`, `continuityPreview`, `tabContextOps`, `toolActivityLabel`, `literalCommand`, `modelTiers`, `local-models/run-on`, `local-models/background-requests`, the renderer's context gauge (`contextUsage`, `useContextWindow`, the Tab Switcher), `useSummarizeAndContinue`, `contextSummarizer` and the CLI `tab continuity` verb.

**What the task's words became.**

- _"Existing parsed event evidence"_: the tab's transcript tool entries (`toolState`: status, exit code, input, stored output), the same events the digest and the activity feed read. Not the proxy: it sees requests, not tabs, and the ledger (04.4) lists tab, native session and window identity as unavailable there. Not message text: message length and wording are never compared (a test feeds eight assistant messages of one length and gets nothing).
- _"Exact repeated call/failure evidence"_: a call is the same call by identity, never by display text. A shell call is keyed by `readCommandCall()` (argv boundaries, directory and added variables count, spacing between arguments does not, so the 05.1b rules apply unchanged); any other tool by its normalized name and `canonicalJson(input)`. A failure is `describeToolActivityStatus() === 'failed'` (a non-zero exit code counts even when the provider says completed). The text of a result is compared exactly as stored.
- _"Intervening edits and compaction resets; polling and test-edit-retest are legitimate"_: see D2.
- _"Advice as report data only"_: sentences in the report, one per open reading. No hook, no gate, no prompt text, no timer.
- _"Reuse existing Run on/tier/background-route facts rather than a parallel router"_: `describeContinuationRouting()` reads each setting through its owner (D4).
- _"Continuity readiness signals"_: six readings (D3).
- _"Missing telemetry"_ and _"no invented accuracy score"_: a reading with no evidence is `unknown`, never `clear`; `accounting.countsAsSavings` is the literal `false`; a reachability test fails if a savings counter imports the signals.

**Decisions.**

- **D1. The producer is the explicit continuity preview, and nothing else.** `planContinuityPreview()` appends the signals to the preview text (so the in-app modal and `maestro-cli tab continuity` show them with no new wiring) and `continuityWire()` carries them as `signals` (strings and counts) for `--json`. No new verb, no UI, no stats row, no daemon, no scanner: the signals are computed when a person asks for the preview, from the tab's transcript, the tab's usage record and the stored settings. Phases 06 and 07 consume the pure `ContextSignals` type; until then there is no other reader. `continuityReachability.test.ts` pins that only the planner imports the signals, that no forwarding, compaction, system-prompt, session-start or Cue path does, and that no savings counter or dashboard does.
- **D2. A run is counted only while nothing could have changed the outcome, and the rules lean toward silence.** `repeated-failure` is the same call failing 3 or more times in a row (`SIGNAL_LIMITS.minRepeats`, a stated heuristic); `repeated-result` is the same file read returning the same stored text that many times. A run is ENDED, and counted up to that point, by: a finished file edit, a new user message, a completed compaction (a pending one does not), a success of the same call, a deliberate wait (a `sleep` call), or any step whose effect on the files cannot be shown to be none: a shell command that is not provably read-only (a short list of print-only programs and git subcommands is the exception), a subagent, an MCP tool, a tool Maestro has no name for. So test, edit, retest is never a run; neither is a retry the agent corrected or a retry after a wait; a bare `cd` or an `npm install` between attempts ends the run (this also means the 05.1b limit about a standalone `cd` does not bite here). Reads, searches, task-list updates and commands that only print do not end one. Polling is not repetition: a call to a live-state command (`git status`, `curl`, `kubectl get`, `gh run view`, `docker ps`, `ps`, `tail -f` and the like, matched at the START of a command so a word inside another command's arguments is not one) or to a live-read tool (a background command's output, a web fetch or search) is never counted, and a read whose text changed starts over. The list is short and stated in every report; other polling can look like a repeat. The price of these rules is recall: alternating failing commands (A, B, A, B), repeated reads through `cat` and loops through an MCP tool are not reported. Precision first, because a signal that cries wolf teaches people to ignore the one that matters.
- **D3. Six readiness readings, each `clear`, `open`, `info` or `unknown`.** `activity` (tab state, calls with no end, whether the newest request has a reply), `repetition` (ongoing runs), `unverified-edits` (finished edits after the newest passing check, using the digest's strict meaning of a passing check), `open-failures` (the digest's count, listed and budget-omitted), `since-compaction` and `pressure` (the provider's own context fill, read with the gauge's function). The digest's findings arrive as plain facts (`SignalDigestFacts`), so the signals do not import the digest and the reachability list is unchanged. When the digest's size budget cut every passing check it found, `unverified-edits` is `unknown` instead of "no check" (found while reading `applyBudget`; a planner case with `maxChars: 1` pins it).
- **D4. Routing is a reading of settings, not of a launch.** `describeContinuationRouting()` runs each owner's capability gate BEFORE believing a stored value: `agentRunOnSupport()` then `parseRunOnTarget()` for Run on, `agentTierRows()` for tiers (the tier owner's own `resolveTierTarget()` per tier), `backgroundRequestsSupport()` then `parseBackgroundRequests()` for background requests. Every statement says "configured", and `notChecked` (in the data and in the text) says what is not an input: a source's enabled and capability state, the token proxy's state for the agent, where earlier turns ran. There is no router, no model choice and no probe. The model and effort are the tab's own setting first, then the agent's, then the provider's default, each saying which.
- **D5. Extractions instead of copies.** `safeExcerpt()` (`src/shared/safeExcerpt.ts`) is the digest's private `safeText()` moved verbatim, now shared with the signals. `describeToolKind()` joins `describesFileEdit()` in `toolActivityLabel.ts`, answered from the same verb the feed chose (the verbs became constants; no label changed, all existing label tests pass). `readTabContextUsage()` (`renderer/utils/contextUsage.ts`) is the Tab Switcher's private gauge reading, extracted; the Tab Switcher now calls it, so the switcher and the preview cannot disagree. `describeToolKind` is a new export of a module `maestro-lib` re-exports, so `MAESTRO_LIB_VERSION` is `0.8.0` (the policy asks for a minor bump) and `docs/maestro-lib.md` follows.
- **D6. Display and comparison are separate.** What is shown of a call goes through `safeExcerpt()` (controls and bidirectional overrides removed, secrets redacted, backticks removed, cut at a word). What is compared is the stored text exactly as stored, and the identity key. Nothing shown is ever compared, and nothing compared is ever shown.

### Supervisor findings, resolved with executed regression cases

1. **Routing claimed dispatch an unsupported provider cannot do.** `routing-review.cjs` (Codex plus a stored background source target) exited 1 with `status: routed`: the draft checked for a stored target before the provider gate. Now the gate is first, for background requests and for Run on (a stored source on a harness with no Run on recipe is `unsupported`, with the owner's reason), a malformed value on an unsupported provider reads `unsupported` too, `routed` became `configured`, and the lines say "configured for" and "configured to go to", never "sent to". `notChecked` carries the unverified availability the supervisor asked for. Red: `supervisor-routing-red.log`. Green: exit 0, `reportedBackgroundStatus: "unsupported"` (`05.4-routing-review-green.log`).
2. **A false duplicate read from trimmed output.** `signals-review.cjs` (`" x"`, `"x"`, `"x "`) exited 1: `readTool()` ran `stripAnsiCodes(stored).trim()` before comparing. Now the stored text is compared exactly (whitespace, case, line endings and colour codes are all part of it; the failure-text flag follows the same rule, so a colour-code difference reads `varies`). Red: `supervisor-signals-red.log`. Green: exit 0, all three controls (`05.4-signals-review-green.log`). The earlier test that asserted colour codes are ignored was wrong under this rule and was replaced.
