### Regression cases added (all executed, see above)

- **Genuine repeated failure (core).** Three identical failing shell calls are one run with the entries it rests on (labels, ids, ordinals); thresholds at 1, 2, 3, 4 and 9; a long run keeps its count and cites the first three and last three entries; a non-zero exit code is a failure even when the provider says completed; reads, searches and print-only commands between attempts do not end it; the failure text is `identical`, `varies` or `not-stored`, exactly as stored; a cut output is said to be cut; a call of the same invocation with no end is noted.
- **Exact identity.** Different arguments, directory, added variables, argv boundaries and tool input are different calls (two runs of three, never one of six); spacing between arguments and key order in an input are not differences; message length and wording are never compared.
- **What ends a run (and what does not).** Test, edit, retest; a finished edit that ends a long run and is named; a failed edit that does not; a user message; a completed compaction versus a pending one; a success of the same call; a corrected retry; seven kinds of step that may change files (an install, a checkout, `sed -i`, a pipe, an MCP tool, a subagent, an unknown tool); a call with no input that may change files; a `sleep` (three spellings) and a word that only contains it; two runs of one call separated by an edit.
- **Polling.** Twenty live-state commands (including chained, assigned, `sudo` and `until` forms) failing five times each are never a loop and are counted as live-state calls; three live-read tools; four commands whose live-state word is only inside another command's arguments are still loops; a live check between attempts does not end a run; a poll whose answer changes is not a repeated read; the report says live calls were left out and states the limit.
- **Repeated reads and exact text.** Three identical reads; a changed text, file, range, edit or user message starts over; an unstored text is never guessed equal; shell `cat` and `ls` are not reads; a cut text is said to be cut. The supervisor's three cases plus seven more kinds of difference (a trailing newline, a line ending, a tab for spaces, indentation, letter case, a colour code, blank texts) are no repeat; a run restarts at the first differing text and counts the run that follows.
- **Missing telemetry.** Calls with no `metadata`, no `toolState`, a null state or no input are unidentified, counted and listed under unknowns, and make `repetition` unknown; calls with no end are neither failure nor success; hostile shapes (a circular input, a function, a bigint, an argv array with a number, a bare string, a non-string output, an empty tool name) neither throw nor invent a repeat; an empty transcript is unknown; no usage record, a NaN or a negative window reads `pressure` unknown and says that is not low use; no digest makes checks and failures unknown; a digest that cut every passing check makes `unverified-edits` unknown, and a known newest check wins over the flag.
- **Readiness and advice.** Each reading in each state (activity open and clear and unknown, edits with and without a check and after one, a check that is not in the transcript, failures, the position since a compaction including pending after done and done after pending, the pressure sentence exactly); advice only for open readings and ongoing runs, none for an ended run or a clear transcript, and the repeated-read sentence names the read once.
- **Contract.** No key or text named score, probability, confidence or saving; `countsAsSavings` false; deterministic, deep-frozen input unchanged, nothing kept between calls; the newest runs listed and the rest counted (12 runs, 10 listed, `maxObservations` 1); the threshold taken from the limits; a command with an escape, a backtick, a bidirectional override and a key-shaped token shown inert and redacted (built from `String.fromCharCode`, because the tool layer decodes typed `\u` escapes into literal characters); no em dash or en dash; the text and the wire form for repetition, readiness, advice, ended-by and the standing limits.
- **Routing.** Defaults, the tab-then-agent-then-default order for model and effort, Run on for a source (named by its saved name), a malformed value, a stored source on a harness with no recipe, an explicit provider target needing none; the built-in tier table not applied to an agent on a source (compared with the tier owner's own answer); an agent override, a harness default and the layer each came from; an unusable tier keeping the owner's reason; five provider and value combinations (valid, stale and malformed) on providers the proxy cannot route for, all `unsupported`, including the supervisor's exact case; "configured" wording with no "sent" or "will"; `notChecked` in the data and as the last line.
- **Planner, wire and surfaces.** The signals follow the digest text and leave it unchanged; a repeated failure of the tab with its entries; open failures and the last passing check taken from the digest it built; a running turn; usage and routing facts used and absent; History is not a source of calls; a refusal carries no signals; no control character from a tool call; the wire survives the receiver's normalizer unchanged; the normalizer cleans, bounds (40 items, 1,000 characters), drops malformed items without repairing them, treats `ongoing` as true only when exactly true, fills missing lists, and returns no signals for a non-object. The real hook path (service, planner, session and settings stores): a repeated failure in text and on the wire, the context fill from a tab usage record, an unknown fill without one, routing from stored settings with the tab model first, and no setting, tab or agent changed. CLI `--json` carries `signals` cleaned again on this side; the text path prints the text only.
- **Reuse extractions.** `safeExcerpt` (controls, line breaks, secrets hidden behind a control character, backticks for data only, the word cut, the scan bound); `describeToolKind` (22 tool spellings, agreement with `describesFileEdit`, `sed -i` stays a shell call); `readTabContextUsage` (the gauge's numbers, the occupancy snapshot of issue #1782, five reasons for `null`, agreement with the Tab Switcher).

### Unresolved limits

- **One producer, no storage.** The signals exist only inside an explicit preview of one tab. Nothing is stored, aggregated or charted; phase 06 may carry `ContextSignals` as optional continuity data and phase 07.4 may draw it. They have no other reader today.
- **Recall is deliberately low** (D2): alternating failing commands, repeats through shell `cat`, MCP tools and subagents, and any repeat separated by a step that may have changed files are not reported. A repeat that follows a change made outside the transcript (a person, another tool) is reported as a repeat, and the report says external changes are not seen.
- **Live-state recognition is a short list** matched at the start of a command. Other polling (a script that reads a status file, a health check through an unlisted program) can look like a repeat, and the report says so. A command that begins with a listed word but is not polling (`ping` of a host that never answers, `ss` used for something else) is left out, never flagged.
- **The stored window.** A tab holds at most its newest 100 entries across a restart, and stored output is capped at 4,000 characters, so a repeat that spans the cut is invisible and identical heads of long texts are not proof (said when it applies). A provider-side compaction is invisible; the first sentence of the standing limits says a repeat after one is not a loop.
- **Readiness is as good as its inputs.** `unverified-edits` counts edit-tool edits only and uses the digest's narrow meaning of a passing check; `open-failures` is the digest's count (shell failures and unanswered errors, not failed edits or reads); `pressure` is the provider's latest reading, not Maestro's count, and says so.
- **Routing says what is configured, not what will happen.** No source state, no proxy state, no launch result (D4). The harness gate for Run on and the provider gate for background requests are the owners', so a future change to either owner changes this report with them.
- **Wire compatibility.** `signals` is optional on the wire. A CLI newer than the desktop prints the text it gets and no `signals`; a desktop newer than the CLI adds a field the old CLI's normalizer drops.
- **Size.** Three new production files (`contextSignals.ts` and `continuationRouting.ts` and `safeExcerpt.ts`) and 9 changed, against the 1 to 3 files and under 500 lines the playbook asks of a task. `contextSignals.ts` is the large one: 1,047 lines, 870 non-blank non-comment, of which 151 are type declarations and 287 the readings and advice; `continuationRouting.ts` has 205 lines (136) and `safeExcerpt.ts` 41 (17). Changed: `continuityPreview` +46, `tabContextOps` +92, `toolActivityLabel` +61 -9, `contextDigest` +4 -23, `contextUsage` +36, `useSummarizeAndContinue` +22 -2, `TabSwitcherModal` +3 -15, and one line each in `cli/index.ts` and `maestro-lib/version.ts`. I did not split finished, tested code (the call 05.1 to 05.3 made); the natural seam, if someone wants one, is the transcript walk (the constants through `readTranscript`, 362 lines) apart from the readings, advice and report. Tests add three new files (1,381 lines) and 659 lines in seven existing ones.
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them). The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

## Supervisor review for phase 06: provider-field reportedness

The existing response reader loses missing-field evidence. A fresh synthetic demonstration using the actual `parseProviderUsage()` shows: Anthropic `{output_tokens:7}` becomes input/cache-read/cache-write zero; `{input_tokens:5}` becomes output zero; OpenAI `{prompt_tokens:20, completion_tokens:3}` without cache details becomes uncached input 20 and cache-read zero. Output: `supervisor-provider-unknown-demo.log`. These are normalized compatibility values, not proof that all four classes were reported. `proxyRequestFromLog()` currently persists them as though all were known.

Phase 06 must preserve field-level evidence before presenting coverage or exact provider classes: distinguish explicitly reported zero, a supported derivation/not-applicable class, and unreported/unknown. In particular, output-only usage must not become known zero input, input-only usage must not become known zero output, and a prompt total without cache detail does not itself prove an uncached split. Preserve required public numeric fields if needed for compatibility using explicit provenance metadata; new stats and UI must honor that metadata rather than count default zeros as measured classes. Historical normalized rows have no reportedness record and must remain marked legacy/unknown instead of retroactively claiming complete reporting. Add executed reader/mapping/aggregation cases, keep raw responses out of analytics, and reuse the same producer/contract throughout. This is within the existing 06.1/06.3/06.4/06.5 missing-provider-class acceptance, not a new feature or a reason to add model calls.

Supervisor 06.1 contract review: reuse the existing canonical `TokenProxyRequestMode` union for `ProxyEffectiveMode` (a type-only alias/import is sufficient), rather than maintaining the same three literals independently. Also align the block-count contract comment with its actual validator: `proxyBlockCountsProblem()` checks the candidate partition and cache/model bounds, but has no independently observed total argument/field with which to validate protected + vetoed + candidates. Document that first sum as the producer's partition/derived recognized total, or validate an actually supplied independent total; do not claim a check the function does not perform. These are bounded reuse/contract corrections inside 06.1, not new tasks.

## 06.1: Shared telemetry contract (row facts, evidence kinds, provider reportedness)

Execution date: 2026-11-12. Status: complete, contract only. There is no migration (06.2), no producer or mapping change (06.3), no aggregation (06.4) and no report or CLI wiring (06.5) in this task. Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`, HEAD `089a94e071`; tracked tree clean before edits, `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` untouched. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin` (the default shell has `v26.12.1`). The supervising session added the "Supervisor review for phase 06" section above while the task ran. I read it before finishing, it belongs to this commit, and both of its 06.1 corrections are applied (below), as is its provider-reportedness requirement, as a contract.

Read the execution contract, the analysis (T20, "Immutable cache and identity design", the O1 layout, the dollar-reduction paragraph), this ledger (01.1 map, 03.4a, 04.4), `CLAUDE.md` with its index, `STATS-ANALYTICS.md` and the fixture rules of `TEST-PATTERNS.md`. Read the source of `stats-types.ts`, `token-proxy.ts`, `request-stats.ts`, `proxy-requests.ts`, both `model-usage.ts`, the server's request log types, `CompressRequestStats` and `compressRequestBody()`, the recovery counters and `readTokenProxyBlock()` in `service.ts`, `response-usage.ts` and `percentiles.ts`. Reused, unchanged: `TokenProxyRequestMode` (now also the type of the effective mode), `isCompressingTokenProxyMode`, `TokenProxyReadOpportunities` (the one opportunity type), `DurationPercentiles` (06.4 will use it for timing). Nothing was duplicated: the server's skip union moved into the shared contract and the server's type now aliases it.

### The contract

| Fact (the task's list)                         | Contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Producer                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Future effective mode                          | `ProxyRequest.effectiveMode?: ProxyEffectiveMode`, an alias of `TokenProxyRequestMode`. Absent is unknown and is never inferred from the agent's setting or from `mode`.                                                                    | `entry.mode` of the request log (06.3).                                                                                                                                                                |
| Eligibility, protected, kept, shortened counts | `ProxyRequest.blockCounts?: ProxyBlockCounts` (`protected`, `vetoed.{failed,recovery,retrieval}`, `candidates`, `belowThreshold`, `kept`, `storeFailed`, `modelFailed`). Shortened is the existing `blocksCompressed`.                      | `CompressRequestStats` today, except `protected`: recognized text spans minus eligible spans per descriptor (`contentSpans` and `eligibleSpans` exist), a new count in `compressRequestBody()` (06.3). |
| Skip and failure categories                    | `TOKEN_PROXY_SKIP_REASONS`, `TOKEN_PROXY_SKIP_LABELS`, `asTokenProxySkipReason()`; `blockCounts.storeFailed` and `modelFailed`. `skipReason` stays open text, so a value from another build reads as none.                                  | The server log and `CompressRequestStats`.                                                                                                                                                             |
| Cache reuse                                    | Existing `cacheHits`, now documented as verdict reuse from Maestro's cache, never provider prompt-cache usage.                                                                                                                              | Unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Processing timing                              | Existing `addedLatencyMs`; absent is unknown and is left out of any mean or percentile.                                                                                                                                                     | Unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Recovery observations                          | `TokenProxyRecoveryObservations`: the shape of the existing snapshot, typed. `getTokenProxyRecoveryObservations()` now returns it.                                                                                                          | The canonical block reader (exists).                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Opportunity data                               | `TokenProxyReadOpportunities` (exists) and the `opportunity` evidence kind. A stored row never holds one.                                                                                                                                   | The observer (exists, process lifetime).                                                                                                                                                               |
| Continuity data                                | No field: no persisted producer exists (the continuity preview and its signals are computed for one tab on request). The unknown reason `on-demand-only` says so.                                                                           | None.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Provider reportedness (supervisor)             | `TokenProxyProviderUsage.reportedness?`, `ProxyRequest.providerUsageReportedness?`, `ProxyProviderClassStatus` (`reported`, `derived`, `not-applicable`, `unreported`) and `proxyProviderClassReading()`, which adds `legacy` and `absent`. | The response reader and the mapping (06.3).                                                                                                                                                            |

Evidence vocabulary (`stats-types.ts`, labels and helpers in `token-proxy.ts`): `ProxyEvidenceKind` is `measured`, `estimate` or `opportunity`; `ProxyUnknownReason` has seven reasons, each with the later task that uses it (`not-recorded` 06.4 historical rows, `not-read` 06.4 passthrough, `not-observed` 06.3 and 06.5 unreported classes and unobserved retrievals, `insufficient-samples` 06.4 percentiles, `on-demand-only` 06.5 and 07 continuity, `identity-unavailable` recovery linkage and context identity, `not-evaluated` 06.5 net dollars and quality); `ProxyCoverage` is `range` (`counted` of `of` requests) or `process-lifetime`; `ProxyFigure<K>` wraps a number with its kind, unit and coverage, or with the reason it is unknown. `proxyFigure()` returns an unknown figure instead of a zero when no request supplied the value. `addProxyFigures()` is the one way to total figures and throws on another kind, another unit or a range added to the process lifetime. A figure's number sits behind `.value`, so `+` does not compile, and the only sanctioned total refuses to mix an estimate with an opportunity or a measured count: that is the acceptance, in the type and in the helper.

Supervisor corrections. (1) `ProxyEffectiveMode` is `TokenProxyRequestMode` through a type-only import in `stats-types.ts`. `token-proxy.ts` already imports types from `stats-types.ts`, so this makes a type-only cycle between the two; both imports are erased and nothing is emitted, and `tsc` for all four configurations accepts it. (2) The `ProxyBlockCounts` comment, the `proxyBlockCountsProblem()` comment and the canonical entry now say what the validator checks: non-negative integers, `candidates = belowThreshold + storeFailed + kept + blocksCompressed`, and `cacheHits` and `modelFailed` not above `kept + blocksCompressed`. `protected + vetoed.* + candidates` is stated as how the producer derives the recognized total; no independent total is stored, so nothing validates it.

Decisions. `blockCounts` is one nested optional object, so a row is either counted or unknown and never half known; flat or JSON storage is 06.2's call. `recoveryScopeVetoes` is left out, because shell hints are never emitted and it could only be zero. The split of recovery reads into model tool calls and human inspection is not in the contract: no producer exists (`callTokenProxyRetrieveTool` and every inspection both reach `readTokenProxyBlock()`), so a field would be invented. `vetoed.retrieval` is documented as retrieved text carried forward in history, apart from the reads the tool served. A class that is `unreported` is stored as 0 and says nothing; the current reader's `{prompt_tokens: 20}` becoming input 20 breaks that rule, and the OpenAI prompt total without cache detail is not kept anywhere by the contract.

The CHECK on `mode` stays.
- **06.3 is over the three-owner bound and must be split before it starts**: (a) `compressRequestBody()` counts `protected`, and `proxyRequestFromLog()` maps `effectiveMode` and `blockCounts` (storing none when `proxyBlockCountsProblem()` finds a problem, and none where the body was not read); (b) the response reader tracks reportedness and the mapping stores it; (c) `service.ts` and `retrieve-tool.ts` split the recovery reads by caller, if that stays in scope. The reader cases it must execute, per the supervisor: Anthropic `{output_tokens}` only (input, cache read and cache write `unreported`, output `reported`), Anthropic `{input_tokens}` only, an Anthropic answer naming a zero cache write (`reported`), OpenAI chat and Responses with cache detail (input `derived`, cache read `reported` even at 0, cache write `not-applicable`), and OpenAI with a prompt total and no cache detail (input and cache read `unreported`). The usage object is null only when no class is reported or derived.
- **06.4** must not count `unreported`, `legacy` or `absent` readings as measured classes, and must report class coverage with the readings counted apart. Its existing `provider_requests` counts any row with an input number and so cannot stand for class coverage. Historical effective mode, counts and reportedness are `not-recorded`; a passthrough row's token fields are `not-read`.
- **06.5** shows `TOKEN_PROXY_EVIDENCE_LABELS` beside every figure, `TOKEN_PROXY_EVIDENCE_SEPARATION_NOTE` where kinds meet, and leaves net dollars and quality as `not-evaluated`.

## 06.2: Stats migration v22 (effective mode, block counts, provider reportedness)

Execution date: 2026-11-12. Status: complete, schema only. There is no writer or mapping change (06.3), no aggregation (06.4) and no report or CLI wiring (06.5). Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`, HEAD `d5d7e4300b` (06.1); `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` untouched. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin`. While the task ran, another process edited four renderer files (`agentGateway.ts`, `LocalModelsGatewaySection.tsx` and their two tests: a Run on check on gateway edits). They are not part of this task and are not in its commit.

Read the execution contract, the 06.1 entry with its supervisor notes, `STATS-ANALYTICS.md`, `migrations.ts`, `schema.ts`, the shared runner `sqlite-migrations.ts`, `proxy-requests.ts`, `request-stats.ts`, `stats-types.ts`, the contract helpers in `token-proxy.ts`, the v20 migration test and the repair test. Reused, unchanged: the runner and its `isApplied` repair, `hasColumn`, the column-list pattern of v17 and v21, the `node:sqlite` test helper and the 06.1 fixtures. Nothing was duplicated.

### The schema

Migration v22 adds fourteen columns to `proxy_requests`. Every one is nullable, has no DEFAULT, no CHECK and no index, and is NULL for every older row. NULL means unknown.

- `effective_mode` (TEXT): `ProxyRequest.effectiveMode`.
- `blocks_protected`, `blocks_vetoed_failed`, `blocks_vetoed_recovery`, `blocks_vetoed_retrieval`, `blocks_candidates`, `blocks_below_threshold`, `blocks_kept`, `blocks_store_failed`, `blocks_model_failed` (INTEGER): `ProxyRequest.blockCounts`, one column per count. The blocks shortened stay the existing `blocks_compressed`.
- `provider_input_reportedness`, `provider_cache_read_reportedness`, `provider_cache_write_reportedness`, `provider_output_reportedness` (TEXT): `ProxyRequest.providerUsageReportedness`, one column per class.

Decisions.

- **Flat columns for the counts, not JSON.** 06.4 sums them in plain SQL. A JSON cell that does not parse makes `json_extract` fail a whole range query; a flat column cannot. A row is counted when `blocks_candidates` is not NULL, because the writer stores the nine together or none (06.1: a row is counted or unknown, never half known).
- **Four columns for reportedness, not one packed value.** 06.4 must count each class by status, and separate columns need no parsing for `GROUP BY` and `COUNT`.
- **No DEFAULT, no CHECK, no index, no backfill.** A DEFAULT turns unknown into a value. A backfill of `effective_mode` from `mode` is wrong: a stored `compress` is `compress` or `compress-early`, and the agent's setting today may differ from the one in force then. A CHECK on a text column cannot be widened without the table rebuild v20 needed (a new proxy mode would need one), so these are open text like `skip_reason`. A read filters on `started_at` and `agent_id`, which are indexed; a column with three or four values does not narrow it.
- **`mode` and its CHECK are untouched.** `compress-early` is stored as `compress` in `mode` and as itself in `effective_mode`. No table rebuild, and `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` rewrites no row.
- **One definition, two paths.** `CREATE_PROXY_REQUESTS_SQL` lists the fourteen columns, so a fresh database (v16 runs with the current definition) has them at once. `ADD_PROXY_REQUEST_TELEMETRY_COLUMNS` (name and type) is what v22 adds to a table an older build created. The body is guarded by `hasColumn`, and v22 declares `isApplied` (all fourteen present), so a database another branch stamped 22 still gets them. One test compares a fresh table with an upgraded one column by column. Another names the expected columns from the contract (the fixture's counts and `PROXY_PROVIDER_USAGE_CLASSES`), so a new contract field fails until the schema has its column.

### Files

- `src/main/stats/schema.ts` (+31 non-comment lines): the fourteen columns in `CREATE_PROXY_REQUESTS_SQL`; `ADD_PROXY_REQUEST_TELEMETRY_COLUMNS`, with the meaning of NULL.
- `src/main/stats/migrations.ts` (+18): the v22 entry with `isApplied`, and `migrateV22()`.
- `src/shared/stats-types.ts` (1 line): `STATS_DB_VERSION` is 22.
- Tests: new `src/__tests__/main/stats/migration-v22.test.ts` (26 cases, 905 lines). The pinned version moved from 21 to 22 in `migration-v20.test.ts` (7 places), `s1-decisions.test.ts` (4), `stats-db.test.ts` (5, and its migration list comment) and `migrations-repair.test.ts` (`RC_TARGET_VERSION`).
- Docs: `STATS-ANALYTICS.md` (a `proxy_requests` schema section, the v21 row the migration table lacked and the v22 row, step 4 of "To add a new migration", and where the telemetry fields are stored); `docs/architecture/local-models.md` (six rows in the `proxy_requests` table and note 6).

### Regression cases (executed)

`migration-v22.test.ts` runs on a real SQLite through `node:sqlite`. The v21 table is written out in the test, not imported from `schema.ts`, which holds the current shape.

- **Fixture.** It really is the v21 shape: version 21, the 25 columns, and the `mode` CHECK refuses `compress-early`. Seven rows: both stored modes, an answer that named no usage, a body forwarded unread, a routed request, zeros, an id with quotes and an agent of NULL, and a timestamp near 2^53.
- **Upgrade of a version 21 database.** It is an ordinary upgrade (version 22, a success record, no warning). The fourteen columns follow the old ones in table order. Every old value is unchanged. Every new fact is NULL in every old row of either stored mode, and no row has an `effective_mode`. The columns are bare (`notnull` 0, no default, no key, no new CHECK, the same two indexes). The `mode` CHECK stays, and each request mode (`passthrough`, `compress`, `compress-early`) round-trips through `effective_mode` paired with its stored mode. The primary key and the NOT NULL columns stay.
- **Fresh database.** It has the v22 shape without a repair. Every column is described exactly like an upgraded one (`PRAGMA table_info`), with the same indexes. There is a column for every contract fact and no other, counts INTEGER and the rest TEXT.
- **Running it again.** A second run changes nothing (rows, table SQL, version, history). The body run against a table that already has the columns (version stamped back to 21) adds nothing, fails nothing and keeps the stored values. A table with some of the columns takes only the rest and keeps their values.
- **A version number that already covers v22.** Stamped 22 without the columns: one warning naming v22, rows kept, version left alone. Stamped 22 and missing two of them. Complete and stamped 22: untouched, no warning. Missing the v21 and the v22 columns together: two warnings, v21 first. Missing the table: v16 recreates it with every column, and one warning names v16.
- **A failure part way.** A failing ALTER on the sixth column leaves the table SQL, columns, rows and indexes as they were, the version at 21 and a `failed` record with the message. The next start applies it with nothing lost.
- **Storing and reading.** A current row stored whole and read back. A counted zero kept apart from an unknown: over 9 requests, `COUNT(blocks_candidates)` is 2, `SUM(blocks_protected)` and `SUM(blocks_kept)` are 4 each, and `SUM` over rows with no counts is NULL, not 0. Each provider class stored with its own reportedness. A request that carries no telemetry, written by the writer that predates v22, stores as unknown and the existing aggregator counts it.

### Unresolved limits and handoff

- **06.3 (writer).** `INSERT_SQL` in `src/main/stats/proxy-requests.ts` names 25 columns and must name these 14 as well. `effective_mode` is `effectiveMode`. The nine counts go in together, only when `proxyBlockCountsProblem()` finds nothing and the body was read; otherwise all nine stay NULL. The four reportedness values go in only with all four provider tokens (the contract writes the tokens together), and a request with no provider usage leaves all eight NULL. `telemetryColumnValues()` in the new test is a reference mapping written for the tests, not a producer: 06.3's real mapping becomes the thing under test. The case "a request that carries no telemetry stores as unknown" stays true after 06.3. `proxy-requests.test.ts` binds parameters "in schema order" against a fake database and will need the new order.
- **06.4 (reader).** Coverage of the counts is `COUNT(blocks_candidates)`. `SUM` over no counted rows is NULL, so test the coverage first and `COALESCE` after. A provider token with a NULL `*_reportedness` is `legacy` and a NULL token is `absent`, as `proxyProviderClassReading()` reads them. A text value a reader does not recognize is unknown. For a row with a NULL `effective_mode`, 06.4 decides what the by-mode breakdown shows. A stored `mode = 'passthrough'` is certain for any row `proxyRequestFromLog()` wrote (it maps `isCompressingTokenProxyMode(entry.mode) ? 'compress' : 'passthrough'`), but the 06.1 contract says an absent mode is never inferred from `mode`, so v22 leaves those rows NULL too, and 06.4 must say which it chose and why. A stored `compress` row can never be resolved.
- **No index.** If 06.4's grouped scans over the `all` range are slow on a real database, add one then, with a measurement.
- **Older builds** (target 21) keep working on a v22 file: their INSERT names its columns and the new ones are nullable. The writer case above exercises exactly that.
- **Engine.** The migration tests run on `node:sqlite` (no native module), as every migration test here does. The DDL is plain `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`. The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched and no packaged build was run.
- **Existing issues, left alone.** `src/__tests__/helpers/nodeSqlite.ts` declares `exec` twice (TS2393; the repository `tsc` configurations do not include tests). `stats-db.test.ts` carries older editor type hints in lines this task did not change.
- **Size.** 50 non-blank non-comment production lines in 3 files, under the 500 the playbook allows. Tests add 905 lines in one new file.
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them).

## Supervisor review during 06.3a: raw fallback must not credit applied compression

The current mapper correctly omits new blockCounts on `compression.skipped=failed`, but still copies old applied-reduction metadata from the completed compressor attempt. Independent actual-mapper probe `fallback-accounting-review.cjs` (only logger/store/DB hosts stubbed) passes its applied-compression control and fails raw fallback: sentBytes=requestBytes and changed=false/skipped=failed still yields blocksCompressed=1, originalTokens=100, sentTokens=50, newBlocks=1 (`supervisor-fallback-accounting-red.log`). This matches the server catch path: it resets the outgoing body/headers and changed=false after a later failure, while attempted stats/blocks can remain on the log.

Resolve this within 06.3a. All applied-reduction fields, including legacy block/token/algorithm/new-block fields used by existing consumers, must agree with what was actually forwarded. A raw fallback cannot credit removed text; use equal original/sent estimates where the original is known, or leave unavailable estimates absent. Cache lookup work and processing time may remain separately observed facts; they are not evidence a shortened block was sent. Add tracked mapper/pipeline regressions, retain the successful positive control, and run the same independent probe to exit 0 before closure. No cache verdict may be deleted or re-decided to repair telemetry.

Comments on the two fields.                                                                                             |

### Supervisor finding: a raw fallback must not credit applied compression

Reproduced as stated: with `changed: false` and `skipped: 'failed'` after a compressor that had returned, the mapper still wrote `blocksCompressed: 1`, `sentTokens` 50 of 100, one new block and the compressor counts. The producer is the server's catch path (it resets the body and headers but left the attempted stats and blocks on the log), and the mapper trusted them. Both are repaired, because the probe feeds the mapper a log entry directly and the server is the real source:

- **Mapper.** `reduction` is the compression log only when no skip reason is set. For a skipped request `blocksCompressed` and `cacheHits` are 0, there are no `newBlocks`, `compressorCounts` or `blockCounts`, and `sentTokens` equals `originalTokens` where the original is known; with no original, no estimate is stored. How long the request was held (`addedLatencyMs`) stays: it is time spent, not a saving.
- **`cacheHits` of a skipped request is 0, a choice.** The supervisor allowed the cache-lookup facts to stay. They described verdicts that decided nothing that was sent, `cacheHits` has no aggregation reader yet, and a zero matches what every other skipped row already stores. 06.4 should not expect a hit count on a skipped row.
- **Server.** The catch path drops the attempt from the log. No cache verdict was deleted or re-decided.
- **Probe.** `fallback-accounting-review.cjs` (Node 24, the real `request-stats.ts` bundled with only the bookkeeping hosts stubbed) exits 0 on the working tree and again after the formatting and comment edits: `applied-positive` ok, `raw-fallback` ok (`shortened` 0, `originalTokens` 100, `sentTokens` 100, `newBlocks` 0).

### Decisions

- **`effectiveMode` is the log entry's `mode`** (the contract's producer), stated for every recorded request, passthrough included. A skipped request, a routed request and a failed compressor keep it.
- **Counts exist only for a request the proxy read and forwarded as the compressor decided.** The counts come from `compression.stats`, which is undefined for passthrough, for any skipped request, and for a request a background target answered (the compressor never ran).
- **A mapping fault cannot reach the server.** `recordTokenProxyRequest()` runs inside the server's `close` handler, and the mapping used to run before its `try`. It now runs inside, after the statistics gate and the readiness check, so nothing is mapped (and nothing can be reported) while statistics are off.
- **Broken counts are reported, not hidden.** `logger.warn` and `captureMessage(..., 'warning')` with the validator's sentence, the route, the kind and the mode; no text of the request. The row is still written, without counts.
- **The writer stores the reportedness columns now.** They have no producer until 06.3b, so they are NULL for every row this build writes. Writing the INSERT once for all fourteen columns avoids a second positional edit and keeps the writer equal to the schema.
- **A required field.** `protected` is required on `CompressRequestStats`; the only constructors are `emptyStats()` and three test fixtures.

### Regression cases (executed)

- **Compressor** (`compress.test.ts`, 173 to 182): `protected` for Anthropic (the fresh result in the last user message), a cache-breakpoint part, a result block carrying the marker (all its parts), Chat Completions (tool messages after the last assistant message), Responses (outputs after the last model item), a vetoed result that also holds a protected part, each with the early-mode count of 0; `protected + vetoes + candidates` equal to the text parts counted off the parsed JSON in both modes; independence from the cache; 0 for a body it does not recognize. `manyResults()` was hoisted to module scope and widened rather than copied.
- **Mapping** (`request-stats.test.ts`, 23 to 62): each of the three request modes with the stored mode it pairs with; the counts with vetoes zero-filled and with every veto, store and model failure carried; a counted zero apart from an unknown; both compress modes against every `TOKEN_PROXY_SKIP_REASONS` entry; the raw fallback (every skip reason with a full attempt attached: no reduction, equal estimates, time kept; the positive control; no estimates when the compressor never returned); a routed request; six broken-count variants (candidates, cache hits, model failures, negative, fractional, NaN), each unstored, warned and captured once, with the rest of the row intact; counts the contract accepts; no shared objects with the compressor's stats; the row's strings are the closed set (`stringPaths`); `recordTokenProxyRequest()` writes mode and counts, contains a faulty entry, maps nothing while statistics are off or the database is not ready, and still writes a row whose counts were rejected.
- **Writer, fake statement** (`proxy-requests.test.ts`, 12 to 15): the 39 positional values in schema order for a compressed request, a passthrough request and a request with provider counts and reportedness; a counted zero apart from NULL; one placeholder per column.
- **Writer, real SQLite** (new `proxy-requests-telemetry.test.ts`, 10): each fact lands in its named column (distinct values per count, so a swap shows), every column of the real table is filled when a row states everything, a legacy row stores all fourteen as NULL, passthrough and skipped rows store their mode and no counts, the two compress modes stay apart in `effective_mode` while `mode` stores one, a counted zero apart from an unknown, and the existing aggregator still reads the table.
- **Live pipeline** (new `request-telemetry-pipeline.test.ts`, 12): a real HTTP request through the real proxy and the real compressor, `recordTokenProxyRequest()`, the real writer and the real v22 table. Passthrough, delayed compress (protected 1, failed veto 1, candidates 2, below threshold 1, one block shortened), early compress (protected 0, candidates 3), the second request's verdict from the cache (`cache_hits` 1, same counts), three fallbacks (unsupported encoding, a body that is not text, a compressor that throws) in both modes with the mode kept, no counts and no tokens, a failure after the compressor returned (nothing credited, equal estimates, the provider receives the original bytes), and a stats write that fails after the response without touching what the provider received.
- **Server log** (`server.test.ts`, 70 to 71): after a compressor that returned and a failure, the log keeps no stats or blocks, equal estimates and the original bytes upstream.

Targeted mutation check, for the named risks only (`06.3a-mutations.py`, log `06.3a-mutations.log`): mapper credits the attempt (M1), server keeps the attempt (M2), both together (M3), the mapping outside the guard (M4), no protected count (M5), the writer swaps two counts (M6), effective mode read off the stored mode (M7), counts stored for a skipped request (M8). **8 of 8 killed**; the four production files were restored byte for byte (the script compares them).

The helper now names `Host` and rejects any answer but the provider's 200, so a rejected request can no longer look like a missing row.

- **06.3b (reader and reportedness).** The writer already stores the four `*_reportedness` columns and `proxyRequestFromLog()` does not set `providerUsageReportedness` yet. It must set it only together with all four provider tokens, and the reader must not store an OpenAI prompt total with no cache detail as uncached input (input and cache read `unreported`). The supervisor's `provider-reportedness-review.cjs` must go from 0/7 to 7/7. The row mapping has no provider test beyond the existing counts case.
- **06.3c (recovery reads).** Untouched: the process-lifetime counters in `service.ts` and `retrieve-tool.ts` are unchanged.
- **06.4 (reader).** A row written by this build always has `effective_mode`; a row from an older build has NULL, passthrough included (v22 does not backfill, and 06.4 decides what its by-mode breakdown shows). Coverage of the counts is `COUNT(blocks_candidates)`. A skipped row has no counts, `blocks_compressed` 0 and `cache_hits` 0.
- **The recognized total is still not stored.** `protected + vetoed.* + candidates` is pinned only against the real compressor in `compress.test.ts`, as 06.1 said.
- **`vetoed.retrieval` per request** counts retrieved text carried forward in history, not retrievals; nothing here counts retrievals.
- **Behavior change to know.** `recordTokenProxyRequest()` now consults the statistics gate and the database before it maps. The only visible differences are a mapping fault (now contained and reported) and no mapping work while statistics are off.

| Docs                                            | `STATS-ANALYTICS.md` (the "no producer yet" bullet now says what the reader records) and `docs/architecture/local-models.md` (note 5: the OpenAI split and the eight columns).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

### The rules the reader now keeps

- **Anthropic:** each of the four classes is `reported` when the answer carries a valid count for it (a named zero included) and `unreported` otherwise (stored 0).
- **OpenAI (Chat Completions and Responses):** input is `derived` only when the answer names both the prompt total and the cached count (total minus cached, held at 0); cache read is `reported` when the cached count is named, even at 0; cache write is `not-applicable` (0 by definition); output is `reported` or `unreported`. A prompt total with no cache detail is therefore stored as neither uncached input nor a cache read, and `prompt_tokens_details` that names no `cached_tokens` counts as no cache detail.
- **Cumulative events:** a field an event carries replaces the earlier value and a field it omits leaves it, so a class an earlier event reported stays `reported`, and a class no event named stays `unreported`. Derivation happens after the last event, so it does not depend on which event carried which fact.
- **Null:** the usage is null only when no class is `reported` or `derived`. `not-applicable` is zero by definition and does not count as a class named.

### Numeric output against the committed reader

`06.3b-numeric-differential.cjs` bundles the committed reader (`git show HEAD:`) and the working-tree reader and compares the four numbers (or null) over 171 usage shapes (every combination of absent, 0 and a count for each Anthropic field; for each OpenAI API, the prompt total, five cache-detail states and the output). 133 are identical. The 38 that differ fall into five groups, each a consequence of a stated requirement, and nothing else changed (log `06.3b-numeric-differential.log`, no unexpected group):

| Group                                                                                          | Shapes | Old                      | New                                            |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------- |
| OpenAI prompt total with no cache detail, and an output named                                  | 8      | input = prompt total     | input 0, cache read 0, both `unreported`       |
| OpenAI prompt total alone (nothing else named)                                                 | 8      | an object, input = total | null (no class reported or derived)            |
| Anthropic answer that names only cache classes                                                 | 8      | null                     | the usage, with those classes `reported`       |
| OpenAI answer that names only a cached count                                                   | 6      | null                     | the usage, cache read `reported`               |
| OpenAI cached count with no prompt total, and an output named (a judgment call, see the notes) | 8      | cache read 0             | cache read = the named count, input unreported |

### Regression cases (executed)

- **Reader** (`response-usage.test.ts`, 13 to 52). The six exact-shape assertions now carry `reportedness`, with every number unchanged. A table of 15 answers is read from both shapes an API sends (a whole JSON body and an event stream), each read three ways (parsed whole, pushed through the real reader in 5-byte pieces that cut anywhere, and gzipped): Anthropic output only, input only, all four named as zero, a zero cache write named beside a cache read left out, a cache read alone; OpenAI Chat Completions and Responses with cache detail (input `derived`, cache read `reported`, cache write `not-applicable`), each with a cache read of zero, a cached count above the prompt total (input held at 0), a prompt total with no cache detail, detail that names no `cached_tokens`, output only, and a cached count with no prompt total. Then: the numbers are what a normalized reader always gave (an unreported or not-applicable class is 0); the result holds five fields and statuses from the closed set and none of the answer's text; across events (earlier classes kept, a stream cut before output leaves it unreported, a later count replaces an earlier one without changing how the class was given); and four shapes that are null (an empty usage, counts that are not counts, a prompt total alone, a grand total alone).
- **Server** (`server.test.ts`, 71 to 72). The existing usage assertion carries `reportedness`. A new case sends an Anthropic output-only answer and an OpenAI answer with a prompt total and no cache detail through the real server: both answers reach the client byte for byte, and the log entries carry the right statuses.
- **Mapping** (`request-stats.test.ts`, 62 to 75). Each class stored with its status beside the four counts, read back through `proxyProviderClassReading()`; a default zero apart from a measured class (`proxyProviderClassHasValue()` false for the three unreported classes, true for output); a reported zero apart from a zero never named; usage without reportedness stays `legacy` for all four classes and carries no `providerUsageReportedness`; no usage is `absent`; a routed request states none; passthrough, delayed compress, early compress and a compress request forwarded as it arrived all keep it (the answer is the provider's, whatever the proxy read); the row shares no object with the reader's; the row's strings are the closed set; `recordTokenProxyRequest()` writes it.
- **Live pipeline** (`request-telemetry-pipeline.test.ts`, 12 to 19). A real HTTP request through the real proxy and compressor, the real mapping, the real writer and the real v22 table, against a provider that answers what the case needs: output only in passthrough, delayed and early compress (stored `0, 0, 0, 7` with three `unreported`); an event stream naming all four classes with a zero cache write (all `reported`); an OpenAI answer with cache detail (`15, 5, 0, 3`: `derived`, `reported`, `not-applicable`, `reported`); an OpenAI prompt total with no cache detail (`0, 0, 0, 3`, not the prompt total); an answer that names no usage (all eight columns NULL). The client receives the provider's bytes unchanged, and no stored column holds the answer text.
- **Writer.** No change: 06.3a already pins the four `*_reportedness` columns against a fake statement and the real table, and both files pass.

### Targeted mutation check

For the named risks only (`06.3b-mutations.py`, log `06.3b-mutations.log`): a class nobody named reported as a zero (M1); an OpenAI prompt total stored as uncached input (M2); an OpenAI cache read nobody named reported as a zero, at the fold (M8) and at the reading (M3); an OpenAI cache write `reported` (M4); `not-applicable` counted as a class named (M5); a later event forgetting an earlier input (M6); derived input not held at zero (M7); the mapping dropping reportedness (M9); the mapping inventing it for usage that carries none (M10); the row sharing the reader's object (M11). **11 of 11 killed.** The two production files were restored byte for byte (the script compares them, and `shasum` before and after matches). While choosing the mutations I saw that no case pinned the clamp when the cached count is larger than the prompt total, so that case was added before the run; M7 is what it kills.

### Unresolved limits and handoff

- **06.3c (recovery reads)** is untouched: the counters in `service.ts` and `retrieve-tool.ts` are unchanged.
- **06.4 (reader).** Read each class through `proxyProviderClassReading()`. `reported` and `derived` are measured; `not-applicable` is zero by definition and is not a measurement; `unreported`, `legacy` and `absent` are unknown and must be counted apart. A stored 0 beside `unreported` is a default, so a plain `SUM(provider_*_tokens)` mixes defaults with measurements: sum only the rows whose class status is `reported` or `derived`, and report how many rows that is. A row this build writes with provider usage has all four `*_reportedness` set, so a provider token with a NULL reportedness is a row from before 06.3b (`legacy`), and a NULL token is `absent`. `provider_requests` (any row with an input number) still cannot stand for class coverage.
- **A behavior change to know.** For an OpenAI answer that names a prompt total and no cached count, `provider_input_tokens` is now 0 (it was the prompt total) and `provider_cache_read_tokens` is 0. The provider sums printed today (the Gateway section and `models proxy status`) add the stored numbers, so they under-count input for such answers until 06.4 and 06.5 print class coverage. I did not measure how often live OpenAI or ChatGPT answers omit the cached count, and no live OpenAI turn ran; the 06.4 coverage figure will show it.
- **A judgment call to review.** An OpenAI answer that names a cached count and no prompt total (the last group above) reports a cache read of that count and leaves input `unreported`. The task's cases fix only the shapes with cache detail and with a prompt total alone; I kept the class status tied to what the answer named, so a named cached count is a reported cache read. No real answer is known to have this shape. If it should stay ignored, it is a two-line change in `readingsOf()` and the one case that pins it.
- **A stream cut short** keeps the output count from `message_start` and calls it `reported` (the answer did name it); it is a partial count, as it was before. Nothing here marks a count as final.
- **No validator** was added for `reportedness` at the mapping: the type fixes the four classes and the reader is the only producer. If a second producer ever appears, a `proxyBlockCountsProblem()`-style check belongs in the shared contract.
- **Size.** 77 added non-blank non-comment production lines in two files (`response-usage.ts` 74 against 37 removed, `request-stats.ts` 3) plus a comment-only edit in `token-proxy.ts`, under the 500 the playbook allows. Tests add 805 lines across four existing files (199, 168, 379 and 59).
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them). The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

Reused, unchanged: the one canonical reader and its eight counters (no second reader, no second counting site), `estimateTokenCount()`, `validateTokenProxyOriginalLineRange()`.

### What changed

| File                                           | Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `src/shared/local-models/token-proxy.ts`       | `TokenProxyRecoveryCaller` (`'mcpRetrieveTool' \| 'otherReads'`) and `TokenProxyRecoveryReadCounts` (the eight counters, named once). `TokenProxyRecoveryObservations` now extends the counts, so every existing top-level field keeps its name and meaning, and adds `byCaller: Record<TokenProxyRecoveryCaller, TokenProxyRecoveryReadCounts>`. `modelExposure`, `unobservedRetrievals` and `cliInvocations` keep their values. The comments say what each part claims. |
| `src/main/local-models/proxy/service.ts`       | `readTokenProxyBlock(hash, range?, options?)` takes `{ caller?: 'mcpRetrieveTool' }`. One step bumps the total tally and the caller's tally, so they cannot disagree. A read that names no caller, or any other value, is `otherReads`. The snapshot copies both parts, `saturated` looks at all three tallies, and the test reset zeroes all three.                                                                                                                      |
| `src/main/local-models/proxy/retrieve-tool.ts` | The tool's one read passes `{ caller: 'mcpRetrieveTool' }`. A comment at the success return says the reader has already counted the call as served, so a refusal added after the read must count itself.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Docs                                           | `CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md` (the "How sure a token proxy figure is" entry), `STATS-ANALYTICS.md` (recovery reads are not row facts) and `docs/architecture/local-models.md` (the tool passes its caller).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

### The rules the counters now keep

- **Attribution is by caller, at the one reader.** The IPC and WebSocket inspection routes pass exactly `(hash, range)` and nothing they receive can set the option. Their existing tests assert two arguments (`toHaveBeenCalledExactlyOnceWith(hash, range)`), so they are the guard that those routes stay `otherReads`.
- **Totals are unchanged.** The top-level counters are their own tally, bumped by the same amounts as before. They are not computed as a sum, because a sum of two saturated parts is not a safe integer. Until a counter saturates, each total equals the sum of its two parts for all eight counters (tested).
- **Served is what the tool answered.** For the tool, `recoveredOriginals` is the number of calls answered with original text (a range past the end counts as zero bytes), and the bytes and token estimate are those of the original text; the header line the tool adds is not counted. The tool always sends a range (default lines 1 to 200), so its `rangedInvocations` equals its `invocations`.
- **Nothing here is receipt or a person.** `modelExposure` stays `unknown`: a served response is not proof the model read it. The second group is named `otherReads`, not a human group: an agent can run the CLI. `cliInvocations` stays null.
- **History retransmission is not a read.** Forwarding retrieved text in a later request never calls the reader, so neither group moves; that text stays `blockCounts.vetoed.retrieval`. A call no counter saw stays `unobservedRetrievals: 'unknown'`.

### Regression cases (executed)

`service.test.ts` (73 to 77 tests) and `retrieve-tool.test.ts` (13 to 14):

- A tool call and a non-MCP read of the same hash, whole and ranged, through the real `callTokenProxyRetrieveTool()` and the real reader over a mocked archive: each group's eight counters are exact (multibyte text, CRLF and tabs), the header is not counted, the totals are unchanged, and each total equals the sum of the parts.
- A missing verdict, a kept block, a missing original, an archive failure and a byte-limit refusal under the tool, and a missing verdict, a missing original and an archive failure under another caller. The tool's `recoveredOriginals` equals the number of `ok` responses.
- The same five-read script gives identical totals whether every read is unattributed or every read names the tool, and the other group is then zero. Only the tool's own name moves a read out of `otherReads` (`{}`, `undefined`, `'otherReads'` and an unknown string all stay there).
- Rejected inputs (a bad hash, a bad range, four malformed tool calls) and compression/history replays leave both groups at zero.
- Saturation: a tool call saturates the byte counter, the total and the tool part sit at the largest safe integer, the other part is not moved and keeps counting, and `saturated` is true. Snapshots are independent, including the nested parts. The test reset zeroes both parts, and a listener stop keeps them.
- The tool names itself on every read (success, unknown hash, missing original, failure). The WebSocket bridge test (`messageHandlers.test.ts`) now asserts the read behind a verified tool call carries the caller.
- Supervisor fixture repair: the launch-binding test mutated `runtimePath` on the merged `LaunchCapability` union. It now narrows with a `local-posix-cli` guard that throws on any other kind, with the snapshot-isolation assertions kept.

### Unresolved limits and handoff

- **06.5 must carry the snapshot into the report.** `getTokenProxyReport()` and `TokenProxyReport` do not hold `getTokenProxyRecoveryObservations()`, and no IPC or CLI route serves it. It should show the tool part as served by the retrieval tool and the other part as other reads with the actor unknown, both as process-lifetime figures labelled as character estimates for tokens, never inside a range total, and with model receipt and unobserved retrievals stated as unknown, so no net causal savings follow from it.
- **Process lifetime, desktop process only.** The counters reset on restart. A retrieval answered by another process (a headless host, a federation peer) or before this build started is not seen. That is `unobservedRetrievals: 'unknown'`.
- **A tool call refused before any read** (a malformed hash or range) is in neither part, like a rejected read, so a report cannot count refused calls.
- **For the tool, `rangedInvocations` equals `invocations`** (it always sends a range). It is kept so both parts share one shape.
- **After saturation** a total no longer equals the sum of its parts (documented in the contract). `saturated` reports it.
- **No mutation sweep.** No named test gap remained: every rule above has an executed case. The one equivalence I know of is `saturated` over the parts, which can only be true when the total is already full.
- **Left alone, pre-existing:** the untyped diagnostics in `messageHandlers.test.ts`.
- **Size.** 57 added and 31 removed non-blank non-comment production lines in 3 files (`service.ts` 51/29, `token-proxy.ts` 5/1, `retrieve-tool.ts` 1/1), under the 500 the playbook allows. Tests add 313 lines and remove 24 across three existing files.
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them). The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

## Supervisor 06.4 group timing coverage finding

Independent actual-SQLite probe `group-timing-review.cjs` creates two agents: one read with added_latency_ms NULL and one measured at 0 ms. Current aggregation returns the same group shape/mean for both, and the existing canonical describeAddedLatency() renders both as `0 ms`; only the global effectiveness.latency can distinguish them (`supervisor-group-timing-red.log`, exit 1). Global coverage cannot explain individual agent or mode means. Within 06.4, expose the existing timed_requests sum as optional backwards-compatible ProxyRequestTotals.timedRequests for totals, byAgent and byMode (no new query/schema). Preserve the legacy numeric mean, but include a real 0 vs 1 sample count on new reports. Add tracked cases and rerun aggregation-review.cjs to rebuild its real-module bundle, then group-timing-review.cjs to green before closing 06.4. Phase 06.5 must consume this coverage in describeAddedLatency/CLI; zero timed requests must never be presented as a measured 0 ms, and absent metadata remains legacy coverage unknown. No extra task or production owner is needed.

### What the answer says, and the decisions the earlier handoffs left to 06.4

- **A historical mode stays unknown (06.2 handoff).** A row with no `effective_mode` is `effectiveMode: null` in `byMode`, a stored `passthrough` row included. Reasons: the 06.1 contract says a mode is never inferred from `mode`; the mapper makes a stored passthrough certain only for rows it wrote; a stored `compress` can never be resolved; and one group for every old row is simpler to explain than two kinds of unknown. A mode text this build does not know reads the same way. Nothing reads an agent's setting.
- **Counts have coverage, apart from estimates (06.1, supervisor).** `blocks.coverage` is `COUNT(blocks_candidates)` out of the requests that carry token estimates (`traffic.read`, which is the legacy `compressRequests`). `blocks.counts` is null when none is counted: unknown, not zero. Its sums, `shortened` and `verdictReuse` cover the counted requests only, so `candidates = belowThreshold + storeFailed + kept + shortened` holds for the sums and a reuse rate is read against its own blocks. A late fallback keeps equal estimates and has no counts, so an estimate is not evidence that shortening was applied. `compressRequests` now says what it counts (rows with `originalTokens`), as the supervisor asked.
- **`read` and `skipped` can overlap**, because a request that failed after the compressor returned is both. The type says so; the reasons in `skips` are one partition of `skipped`.
- **Provider classes (06.3b handoff).** A class's `tokens` is a `ProxyFigure<'measured'>` summed over the requests where the class is `reported` or `derived`, unknown (`not-recorded` when legacy rows dominate, else `not-observed`) when none is, and `rows` counts all six readings, which add up to the requests. A status this build does not know reads as `legacy`. `totals.provider` still adds the stored numbers, for compatibility: it includes default zeros and legacy numbers and is not a measured total.
- **Verdict reuse is not the provider's cache.** `blocks.counts.verdictReuse` (Maestro's cache) and `providerClasses.cacheRead` (the provider's) are separate fields with separate coverage.
- **Algorithms overlap.** `algorithms[].blocks` add up to more than `uniqueBlocks` whenever a block had two; `uniqueBlocks` covers the requests that name their algorithms.
- **Latency.** The distribution is `percentilesFromSorted()` over the timed requests and is withheld below 30 (`insufficient-samples`), a display policy that says nothing about how stable a tail is. A read request with no time is `untimed`. The mean now divides by the requests that carry a time; the mapper writes the estimates and the time together, so the number is unchanged for every row it wrote, and a read row without a time can no longer lower it. `timedRequests` is the sample of each mean (total, each agent, each mode), so 0 means "no time" and not "0 ms" (supervisor finding). The legacy numeric mean is kept.
- **Days.** Local calendar days by SQLite `localtime` (as `queryByDay`), sparse, oldest first; the first day of a rolling range is partial. A timestamp SQLite cannot place on a day is left out of the series and not of the totals.
- **Not added.** A provider-route breakdown: `token-optimization-analysis.md` O1 lists it, and the text of 06.4, 06.5 and 07.2 does not. A second consumer of the same call would need one column and one field.

### Regression cases (executed)

- **New `proxy-effectiveness.test.ts` (52), real SQLite and the real v22 table.** An empty range (every block present, every count 0, every unknown stated; no NaN anywhere; the same for every range). Traffic (a read-and-skipped request, the range start). Block counts (sums over counted requests only with `counted` of `of`; null counts when none is counted; a counted zero apart from an unknown; requests forwarded unread are not asked for counts; every count on its own field; verdict reuse over the counted requests). Verdict reuse against the provider cache (separate fields). Skips (by reason, an unrecognized reason, the partition). Processing time (below the minimum, at the minimum, unordered input, an untimed request, `not-recorded` against `not-read`, the mean over timed requests, the mean at 0 with no sample, a group with no time against a group measured at 0 ms for each agent and each mode, groups whose samples add up to the total). Algorithms (overlap against unique blocks, ordering, an algorithm another build added, malformed cells). Provider classes (five request shapes across all six readings, default zeros and legacy numbers not summed, a plain stored sum shown to differ, the six readings adding up to the requests, an unknown status, both unknown reasons, agreement with `proxyProviderClassReading()`). The days (local days either side of midnight, sums equal to the totals, a partial first day). The cuts (agents, modes and total add up on 10 fields; an old mode stays unknown; an unknown mode text; ordering; a removed agent). A differential case: five seeds of 240 generated requests, the SQL held to a plain JavaScript reading of the same rows for traffic, counts, skips, latency, algorithms, provider classes, days, modes and agents.
- **`proxy-requests.test.ts` (fake database, 15 to 18).** The grouped scan rolled up by agent, mode and total; groups with an unknown mode text; the divisor of the mean; no divide by zero; the empty range; every scan of one answer bound to the same start.
- **`model-usage.test.ts` (17 to 21).** The empty record carries `byMode` and `effectiveness`; the Models `proxy` block is the very answer `getProxyRequestUsage()` gives; the modes add up to the total; a removed agent is nameless and counted; days follow the range.
- **`token-proxy-telemetry.test.ts` (35 to 38).** `asTokenProxyRequestMode()` against `asStoredTokenProxyMode()`, and the latency minimum.

Targeted mutation check, for the named risks only (`06.4-mutations.py`, log `06.4-mutations.log`): a mean divided by the requests read (M1), an old mode filed as passthrough (M2), unique blocks as the sum of the algorithms (M3), no counted request answering a zero record (M4), legacy numbers summed as measured (M5), default zeros in a class's coverage (M6), verdict reuse over every request (M7), a distribution one sample late (M8), a never-read request counted as untimed (M9), an unrecognized skip reason dropped (M10), UTC days (M11), a roll-up that keeps the last group (M12), count coverage over all requests (M13), `timedRequests` left out (M14), identical algorithm cells counted once (M15). **15 of 15 killed**; both production files restored byte for byte (`shasum` before and after identical). M11 is killed only off UTC (this machine is CST); on a UTC machine the two kinds of day agree, so that mutation would survive there by construction.

### Unresolved limits and handoff

- **06.5 must consume `timedRequests`.** `describeAddedLatency()` still prints `0 ms` for a group with no timed request, as the supervisor's probe shows (`description` is `0 ms` for both agents). It must print the mean only against `timedRequests`, treat 0 as "no time" and an absent field as unknown coverage. The CLI formatter has the same gap (`proxyUsageLine()` prints `avgAddedLatencyMs` whenever `compressRequests` is not 0).
- **06.5 must print class coverage.** The Gateway section and `models proxy status` still add the stored provider numbers (`totals.provider`), which under-count OpenAI input for an answer with a prompt total and no cache detail (06.3b). Use `effectiveness.providerClasses` with its `rows`, and label `byMode` null as `TOKEN_PROXY_UNKNOWN_LABELS['not-recorded']`.
- **06.5 also owns the recovery observations** (06.3c handoff): the process-lifetime counters are still in no report, and must not enter a range total or the `ProxyFigure` coverage of a range.
- **Cost.** The aggregation is five scans of a wide table. On 300,000 rows it costs 103 ms for a week, 456 ms for a month (the Gateway default) and 1.35 s for all time, against 20, 67 and 180 ms before, on the main thread. A leaner formulation (the provider readings as group keys) was measured at about 15% faster, so the cost is row decoding and not expression count; a covering index does not apply to a full-range scan. The remaining lever is folding the totals scan into the summary scan (about 20%). Not done: no measured need yet, and it would join two modules. The 06.2 note about a possible index is therefore answered: not needed, and not useful.
- **The reader needs the v22 columns**, as the writer does. A database whose v22 migration failed cannot be read until the next start applies it.
- **`blocks.coverage` rests on a writer invariant:** counts exist only on a request that carries estimates. The mapper enforces it (a skipped request carries none); the reader does not re-check it.
- **A provider-route breakdown** is not in the answer (see above); the O1 mock lists it.
- **Rows written before 06.3a** can carry `blocks_compressed` above 0 on a request that was forwarded as it arrived (the bug 06.3a repaired). History cannot be corrected, so those rows count in `shortened` and `blocksShortened`.
- **Size.** 457 added and 25 removed non-blank non-comment production lines in 6 files (`proxy-effectiveness.ts` 295 new, `proxy-requests.ts` 72/23, `stats-types.ts` 73/1 (types), `model-usage` pair 11/1, `token-proxy.ts` 6), under the 500 the playbook allows. Tests add 1,340 lines in the new file and about 320 in four existing ones.
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them). The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

Do not concurrently edit those owners; 06.5 should consume the resulting optional byRoute just like byMode, with existing route labels and unknown stored route text preserved. No new endpoint/schema/query or worktree is needed. This is completion of the selected O1 requirement, not a separate feature.

### The rules the report now keeps

- **A mean is read against its sample (06.4 handoff, supervisor).** `describeAddedLatency()` prints `-` when the proxy read nothing, `unknown` when it read requests and none carries a time, `14 ms` for one or more timed requests (a measured zero stays `0 ms`), and `14 ms (sample unknown)` for an older desktop that sent a mean and no sample. An older desktop's mean of 0 is `unknown`. `0 ms` is printed for exactly one state (checked over all 16 combinations of read, sample and mean).
- **Every cut is worded once.** The CLI's total, agent, mode and route rows all go through `proxyUsageLine()`. A mode a row never stated is `not recorded (the request predates this measure)`, never a mode inferred from a setting. A stored route keeps its stored name when this build does not know it (`Object.prototype` names included), and a route the Gateway names is shown by its label.
- **The provider's numbers keep their provenance (06.3b and 06.4 handoffs, supervisor copy review).** A current desktop's classes read `1.2M tokens on 100 of 120 requests. Other requests: 12 not reported in the answer (unknown, not zero); 8 recorded before reporting was tracked (unknown)`; a measured zero keeps its coverage (`0 tokens on 50 of 120 requests`), and a class nobody reported is `unknown: <reason>`. The class note allows derived values (OpenAI's uncached input is the prompt total minus the cached tokens) and says the estimates are not subtracted. The sums of an older desktop (`totals.provider` without `effectiveness`) are `Stored sums` with unknown provenance and coverage, under their own note, and are never called reported or derived. The line and the note follow one test, so a sum cannot sit under the class note.
- **The algorithm subtotal is a cohort (supervisor copy review).** `200 unique blocks in the requests that recorded their algorithms, out of 210 shortened in the range`; the lists overlap and do not add up.
- **Recovery is not a range (06.3c handoff, task text).** `TokenProxyReport.recovery` is printed in its own section: the retrieve tool apart from other reads (actor unknown, never called human), original bytes and a character-estimate token figure for each, receipt by a model and retrievals outside this app stated as not known, and `Net saving: not evaluated` with the reason that a return counts text once while the saved-token figure counts a block every time a later request resends it. Nothing is subtracted. `stats models` does not print it: that record is a range.
- **No money, no quality.** `TOKEN_PROXY_RESULTS_NOT_EVALUATED_NOTE` is said by the CLI (both commands, even for an older desktop), the Settings card and the dashboard section. The CLI prints no dollar figure.
- **Days are the series behind the totals.** The text report lists the latest 7 local days that have a request, each through `describeTokensSaved()` (a day with nothing read says `none read`, not a saving of 0), and says how many earlier days the JSON record holds.

### Regression cases (executed)

- **New `token-proxy-report.test.ts` (39).** Latency: the 16-combination table, the saved-token text unchanged, mode, route (known, unknown, empty, inherited names), the distribution with its untimed requests, below the minimum, and the reasons. Effectiveness: a busy range line by line, an empty range (unknown, no row of zeros), requests read before counts were recorded against a counted zero, an unrecognized skip reason, the algorithm cohort (3 unique of 6 shortened). Provider usage: classes with coverage, the stored sums ignored when classes exist, a class nobody reported, the all-absent collapse, an empty range, the older desktop's stored sums, the note under each, and a measured zero beside legacy requests. Trend: order, the latest 7, a day with nothing read, a late fallback and a zero denominator. Recovery: the two callers apart, no human actor and no net saving, one caller alone, the saturation limit. A sweep that no sentence contains `NaN`, `undefined`, `null`, an em dash, an en dash or a dollar figure.
- **`models-proxy.test.ts` (61 to 78).** The pinned totals line changed to the shared wording. New: every effectiveness line with its coverage; the time against each group's sample (0 ms, unknown, `(sample unknown)`); modes apart from agents with the historical request `not recorded`; routes with an unknown route kept as stored, and no route cut for a desktop that sent none; the provider classes apart from the estimates; the not-evaluated note for any desktop; the older shape as stored sums under the stored-sums note and never the class note; the latest 7 days with the earlier-days line; the whole record in `--json` (effectiveness, modes, routes with `null` and stored text kept, `recovery` as its own field); and the recovery section (apart from the range, the range prints identically with and without it, one line for nothing read, nothing for an older desktop, the limit, `--json`).
