### Unresolved limits and handoff

- **07.2 and 07.3 own the displays.** Settings now shows the provider rows (their layout is provisional, per the supervisor's note) and the dashboard shows one note. The effectiveness rows, modes, routes, days and recovery are in the CLI and in `describeProxyEffectiveness()`, `describeProxyTrend()` and `describeProxyRecovery()` for those tasks to draw. They should call these functions and not word a number again.
- **The text report lists 7 days.** `effectiveness.trend` holds every day and `--json` prints it; the dashboard draws its own series.
- **`stats models` has no recovery.** The Models record is a range and the counters are the life of the app, so they stay on `models proxy status` and `TokenProxyReport.recovery`. A dashboard that wants them reads the report.
- **Behavior change to know.** The CLI totals line is now `12 requests, est. tokens saved 10,000 (25%), average added latency 14 ms` (it was `10000 tokens saved (25%), 14 ms average added latency`), and a group with no timed request says `unknown`. A script should use `--json`.
- **`formatCountNoun()` is new on purpose.** Five private `plural()` copies exist (CLI formatter, dashboard sections, two other CLI commands, one renderer service). Theirs print the raw number, and the CLI's feeds many existing outputs, so I did not swap them; this one groups digits like `formatCount()`. They are a later dedup, not a blocker.
- **Open text is printed as stored.** Algorithm ids and unknown route names come from the local database, as they were written; the CLI does not strip them.
- **A group with no recorded time is `unknown` even when its mean field holds a number** (an older desktop's), and a mean with no sample is labelled. I did not try to recover a sample for history.
- **Size.** 424 added and 50 removed non-blank non-comment production lines in 8 files (the new module 266, `formatter.ts` 109/32, Settings 30/15, `token-proxy.ts` 10/2, the dashboard 4, `formatters.ts` 3, `service.ts` 1, `index.ts` 1/1), under the 500 the playbook allows. That is more than 1 to 3 files because the other five are consumers that held a copy of the rule or one line. Tests add 729 lines in the new file and about 940 across eight existing ones.
- No live provider turn, packaged build or full suite was run (the final phase owns them). The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

## Supervisor 07.4a: Stored-agent context audit API

Implemented `auditStoredAgentContextFootprint()` in `src/main/context-footprint-service.ts`, `context:getFootprint` in the existing live registrar, typed preload/Window access and web bridge parity. The sole input is an exact stored live agent ID. Reuses the bounded collector, canonical account and memory resolvers, and CLI prompt assembly with bounded asynchronous Git facts. Both SSH markers veto local inventory and prompt assembly. Unknown memory and missing prompt evidence remain explicit; configured text does not establish delivery or window membership. The CLI audit had still rebuilt remote prompts using local Git facts; it now honors both SSH markers and omits that preview. No live settings, provider call, timer or write was added.

Executed Node 24 Vitest on the collector/service, context handlers/preload, prompt builder, CLI audit, memory resolver, web bridge and audit reachability suites: **259 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped in 11 files** (`supervisor-footprint-api-tests.json` and `.log`). Covers account isolation, existing project/account/memory files, unchanged inventory contents/mtime, exact identity rejection, remote collisions, unsupported memory, relative cwd, actual subdirectory cwd, error propagation and web availability. Initial new assertions used the wrong audit field names; corrected to the existing `subject`, `inventory` and `unknowns` contract before the green run. Updated reachability guards to admit the explicit app boundary while retaining counter/write/schedule exclusions.

`npm run lint` passed all four configurations; after the final CLI repair, `tsc -p tsconfig.cli.json --noEmit` passed again. ESLint passed for the changed production files and the changed-test dash pass passed; Prettier, `docs:verify` and `git diff --check` passed. Fresh LSP resolved the new service definition/references and returned 14 successful empty syntax/semantic diagnostic responses across seven production files (`supervisor-footprint-api-lsp.log`). Detailed logs are in the completion scratch folder. UI consumption is still 07.4b; final integrated full tests/build/push remain phase 08. The small integration touches seven production owners (service, prompt assembler, CLI audit, handler, preload, ambient type and bridge), about 120 implementation lines rather than a duplicate collector.

No new endpoint, query, schema, store, timer or setting.

### What changed

| File                                                                       | Change                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

| `.../ModelUsage/modelUsageView.ts`                                         | Pure builders: `proxyTrendView()` (one slot per local day, a `null` gap for a day nothing was read), `proxyModeSlices()`, `proxyRouteSlices()`, and the scope notes and bounds.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `.../ModelUsage/ModelUsageStats.tsx`                                       | Passes `since`, `loadedAt`, `refreshing={loading}`, `onRefresh={reload}` and `colorBlindMode`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `src/shared/local-models/token-proxy-report.ts`                            | `describeProxyHandling()` (the first lines of the report) and `describeProxyAlgorithmCohort()` split out of the existing functions so the dashboard lays the report out beside widgets in the same words. The CLI output is byte-identical (the full report is those lines plus the other two; a shared case pins it).                                                                                                                                                               |

### Decisions

- **One fetch owner, no timer.** Refresh is the hook's `reload`; "Loaded" is `loadedAt` shown with `formatTimestamp()` and never recomputed. `usage.since` is used only to bound the start of the trend axis and is never shown as a freshness (a case asserts the label carries the read time and not the range start). An hour of fake time with no stats event makes no read and changes no label.
- **Every figure keeps its denominator and its certainty.** The reduction is `describeTokensSaved()` with "of 100.0K estimated tokens in 100 read requests"; coverage is read of carried; the time is the mean through `describeAddedLatency()` (0 timed requests is `unknown`) with the sentence of `describeProxyLatency()` under it (below 30 samples it says why there is no distribution). Nothing read is `-` with `not read (...)`. Handling lines come from `describeProxyHandling()`, provider classes from `describeProviderUsage()` under `describeProviderUsageNote()`, the algorithm cohort from `describeProxyAlgorithmCohort()`.
- **Trend.** One slot per local calendar day from the range start to the day the record was read, stepped with `addLocalDays()`; a day with no request, or none read, is `null` in both lines (never 0), a read day with no text is a real 0, and a day that holds data is never dropped. Past `PROXY_TREND_MAX_AXIS_DAYS` (1100) the axis uses only the days that hold a request, so a stray row cannot make it thousands of slots wide. The two estimates overlap, so they are two lines (the sent one dashed, and a note says so because the legend cannot), never stacked.
- **Partitions are donuts, overlaps are bars.** Mode and route split the requests carried, so they are donuts; the `not recorded` group stays its own slice and a route this build does not know keeps its stored name. Algorithm counts overlap, so they are bars with `topN` set to their number (the widget's `+N more` row would add overlapping counts) and the cohort note, never a share.
- **Verdict reuse, the provider's cache and the estimates stay apart.** Verdict reuse is in the handling lines (Maestro's cache, said not to be the provider's), the provider's cache read is its own class, and the separation and not-evaluated notes are shown. No dollar figure and no grade.
- **Older desktop.** Without `effectiveness`, the three figures come from the totals, the trend, splits and handling lines are left out, provider sums are `Stored sums` of unknown provenance, and one line says what needs a newer desktop.
- **Found by rendering the real component** (Pedurple, 1000 and 520 px, `07.2-render-*.png`): six whole-date axis labels collided at phone width, so the axis shows four (`PROXY_TREND_AXIS_LABELS`); and the palette's violet beside the accent made two slices of a donut look alike, so splits take the series colors in an order that keeps the violet and the indigo for last (the colorblind palette keeps its own).
- Mode configuration stays in Gateway: the toolbar only links there. The mock's read-only inspection row (opportunities, footprint audit, continuity) belongs to 07.4b and is not drawn.

### Regression cases (executed)

- **`useModelUsage.test.ts` (new, 5):** `loadedAt` is null until a read answers and then the answer time (not `since`); a reload keeps the record and its time with `loading` true, then moves both; a background refresh moves the time and a failed one keeps the last good one; a failed foreground read clears record and time; a new range starts from nothing and the answer of the range it left sets neither.
- **`ModelUsageStats.test.tsx` (30 to 61, +31):** the three figures with denominators; not read, unknown and below-minimum states; freshness from the owner; Refresh in flight (record stays, button disabled, one more read of the same range), after a failure (error plus Retry), no read or label change after an hour; a stats update moves the time; the trend (axis labels, one polyline per series with the gap, single days as marks, dashed sent, colorblind colors, notes, no chart for no day or only unread days, a hidden legend survives a refresh); mode, route and algorithm splits (partition, unknown slice, unknown route, no `+N more` for 11 algorithms, colorblind arcs); provider classes with coverage, stored sums under their own note, verdict reuse apart from the provider cache, the handling lines equal the shared ones, block counts unknown for requests read before counts; the table and notes, no dollar or grade, Gateway navigation closing the dashboard, an older desktop, a missing split, and the empty line.
- **`modelUsageView.test.ts` (19 to 33, +14):** the axis (empty, range start to read day with gaps and totals equal to the range's, trailing gap, measured zero against unread, all time, never dropping a day, clock changes, the bound and past it, a malformed day) and the splits (modes, colors, colorblind, routes, empty). **`token-proxy-report.test.ts` (39 to 42, +3):** handling lines, the cohort sentence, and that the full report is the handling lines plus the other two.
- **Targeted mutation check** for the named risks only (`07.2-mutations.py`, log `07.2-mutations.log`): an unread day drawn as 0, the axis ignoring the read day or the range start, the bound off by one, an unknown mode colored as a series, known route names lost, algorithm bars collapsed into `+N more`, freshness from `usage.since`, Refresh not wired, a failed read or a new range keeping its time, a refresh that does not move the time, the wrong provider note, the cohort against the wrong total, the wrong percent denominator, a mean without its sample, handling lines without skips, a legend hide lost on refresh, the sent line not dashed, six axis labels, and splits in plain palette order. **21 of 21 killed**; the production files were restored byte for byte (`sha256` before and after).

## Supervisor integration repair: defer Run on metadata discovery until validation

The 07.2 broad regression report had four failures in `update-agent.test.ts` after the concurrent `cfc5998086` Run on change: unsupported/unknown source edits and conflicting gateway flags contacted the app before refusal, and the valid combined-edit test still assumed no metadata read. `updateAgent()` now uses the existing `readRunOnFlags()` for preflight validation, checks the remaining edit constraints, then asks `reportedRunOnFlagContext()` and reapplies the canonical parser only when metadata supplies a context. Refused edits do not query the source. Valid edits still perform one configuration write and may first read metadata. Updated that test and its patch selector, and added a positive source-reported-context case.

### Decisions

- **Ranges.** "Provenance and completeness for ranges" is read as the coverage of each count inside the 30 days, plus the span of the two process-lifetime sections. Diagnostics are the CLI's lines (`90 of 100 read requests counted`, `none of 100 read requests carries counts` where nothing was counted, `not read` where nothing was read), the latest 7 days with the partial first day said, and the number of earlier days not listed. Each disclosure title carries its scope. The card never requests a line range: `block(hash)` keeps asking for the whole block as before, and line ranges stay in the CLI, where their completeness is reported.
- **Closed means nothing.** A closed disclosure draws no body and makes no call; its state is local and survives Refresh (the previews are keyed by their own facts, so an open preview stays open when the same previews come back with a new tally). Nothing polls.
- **A diff here is a read.** `block(hash)` is counted under `otherReads`. The card says so and that Refresh shows the count; opening a diff does not re-read the report.
- **Zero coverage is said, not drawn as 0.** Nothing examined: `No request has been examined since the app started`. No preview: `No preview has been built yet`. No counts: unknown with its reason. A proxy never set up shows none of it.
- **CLI behavior change, to know.** `models proxy status` opportunity lines now group digits from 1,000, carry a `Work bound:` label on the work-bound line, and name a skip reason a newer build recorded instead of printing `undefined`. Every pinned string of `models-proxy.test.ts` is unchanged.

### Regression cases (executed)

- **`LocalModelsProxyInspection.test.tsx` (26).** Diagnostics: closed draws nothing and opening makes no read; the handling lines with the share each count covers (a partial range: 90 of 100); zero coverage (no read request carries counts) as unknown, never `0 shortened`; a passthrough-only range as `not read`; the latest days with the partial note and the earlier-day count; an older desktop (totals-only note); an empty range offers none. Cuts: modes and routes apart from the agents with the same figures, a dash for an unread group; an unsupported route (`zeta`, empty, `constructor`) kept as stored and a null mode as `not recorded`; no cut for an older desktop. Compression row: cache key, Copy, the exact-original command and `--lines`; a missing original shows the replacement as text (and escapes markup); a block that is gone offers no key. Observations: closed by default; recovery by caller with no net saving; opportunity lines with unsupported reasons by name; a preview opens to its facts and text; text shown as text; cut and redacted said; an unknown reason named as stored; nothing examined; older desktop and never-set-up proxy; only the part sent. Reads: one read on mount, none on opening, one on Refresh with disclosures still open, none after an hour of fake time; a diff does not re-read the report; a failed read keeps what was shown.
- **`TokenProxyObservations.test.tsx` (9).** Both parts; either; neither; default and supplied agent names; the preview toggle with `aria-controls` only while open; a kind this build does not know; an open preview kept across a refresh; no use of the app bridge; `ProxyReportLines` terms and definitions.
- **`token-proxy-report.test.ts` (+12).** The opportunity lines (labels, exact texts, grouping, the work bound, an unknown skip reason and `constructor`, nothing examined), the identity sentence, the preview facts (unchanged, changed, outline, cut and redacted, unknown route and kind), the three delivery sentences, partial and zero block coverage, and the new sentences in the no-`NaN`/no-dash sweep.

## 08.1: Integrated review against T01 to T22

Execution date: 2026-11-12. Status: complete. Images analyzed: 0 (neither the task nor the document references any). Branch `acappella`; HEAD `a289b117e5` at the start. Node `v24.22.1` via `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin`. The supervisor edited this checkout while the task ran and extended the header guard (see "Supervisor 08.1 header-variant repair"); the merged state is what was checked, and one commit carries both. LSP was exposed as a deferred tool and used (`documentSymbol` over `compress.ts`); it has no diagnostics operation, so type errors rest on `npm run lint`. Scratch evidence is in `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/08.1/`.

Read the analysis (T01 to T22, the cache and identity design), the ledger sections each contract names, `CLAUDE.md`, and the source of `compress.ts`, `compression-cache.ts`, `server.ts`, `service.ts`, `request-stats.ts`, `retrieve-tool.ts`, `opportunity-observer.ts`, the stats migration and the report vocabulary. Result: one real defect, fixed (next section). The other named contracts held on the code and on tests that already ran (table below). No mutation sweep was run.

### Defect: failed Codex calls were not recognized (T02)

Failure protection read `is_error` and a JSON `metadata.exit_code` envelope. A Responses `function_call_output` has no error field, and current Codex does not send the envelope. Counts from this machine's newest 276 Codex rollouts (0.162.1 to 0.167.2; counts only, no content was read out; `real-format-counts.json`): of 6,239 tool outputs, 1 was an envelope. 5,404 were `exec` results, a list of text parts whose first part reads `Script completed` (5,392) or `Script failed` (12). 435 were `exec_command` results: 389 finished with a `Process exited with code N` line (20 non-zero) and 46 were still running. No output item carried a `status`, `success` or `is_error` field. So a failed Codex call was eligible for compression once it aged, and in `compress-early` even while fresh. Claude Code was already protected: of 9,546 `tool_result` blocks in the newest 250 transcripts, all 200 with a non-zero `Exit code` header had `is_error: true`.

Fix, in `src/main/local-models/proxy/compress.ts`: `hasFailedResultHeader()` with `leadingText()` and three patterns, `SCRIPT_FAILED_HEADER`, `PROCESS_EXIT_HEADER` and `SHELL_EXIT_HEADER` (`shell_command`'s `Exit code: N`, from the recorded Codex fixture in `codex-session-storage-messages.test.ts`). They read the raw JSON literal, so a large result is not decoded. The header must open the first text part of the result; the same words later, or in a later part, are output the call printed. The check only vetoes: a zero code, `Script completed`, a running call and any other spelling stay unknown. It runs in `responsesToolResults()` beside the envelope check, so the veto still comes before `resolveBlock()` reads the cache. The supervisor's repair makes the colon after `Wall time` optional (the code-mode failure prints `Wall time: 0.0 seconds` and then `Script error:`) and allows ten digits (signed `-1073741819`, unsigned `3221225477`). `docs/local-models.md` now says what counts as an explicit failure.

Regression cases, `src/__tests__/main/local-models/proxy/compress.test.ts`, describe "result headers of Codex tools" (27 cases; the file went from 182 to 209): 16 classification rows (failed script, completed, running, string form, header in a later part, non-text first part, exit 2, -1 and 0, running process, header after text or after a space, a wall time that is not a number, shell exit 1 and 0, a failure the output only printed); a failed header outvotes `is_error=false` and never yields a success; 6 frozen-verdict rows in both modes (the cache is seeded with the same text as a success, then the Responses request must go out byte for byte with no cache read or write, no model call and the verdict unchanged); 4 rows that prove successes still shorten. Red check: with the new call disabled, the 9 header-dependent cases then present failed (5 classification, the `is_error` case, 3 frozen-verdict) and the rest passed; the supervisor recorded the red run for its three added rows (`supervisor-header-red.log`).

### Contract review

| Changed API regions                       | Held              | `findToolResultDescriptors` selects only tool-result text and `spliceSpans` rewrites only those literals. | Legacy cache reads                        | Held              | `COMPRESSION_RECORD_VERSION` is still 1 and a `full` key is the plain SHA-256. `readFromDisk()` accepts records with no `recoveryScope` or `modelFailure` and keeps retired compressor IDs. Tests: "preserves a legacy %s verdict through upgrades, guard vetoes and repeat requests", "preserves a verdict with unreadable recovery scope".                                                                                        |
| Error and recovery veto order             | Held and extended | The veto is computed from the descriptors before `resolveBlock()`, so the cache is never read for a failed, recovery or retrieval result, and the count reaches `blockCounts.vetoed`. Tests: "$kind vetoes a failed copy before reading a frozen successful verdict in either mode", "protects a structured exit code %i before a frozen verdict in both modes", the header rows above.                                             |
| Passthrough streaming                     | Held              | With no compress or background route, `createTokenProxyServer` pipes the request body and the response (`upstreamResponse.pipe(res)` after `flushHeaders()`); the usage reader and the end marker only count chunks. | Route capabilities                        | Held              | `localRecoveryRoute()` accepts only a `local-posix-cli` capability with absolute, bounded paths. `recoveryFor()` and `agentHasTokenProxyRetrieval()` refuse an SSH remote and a mismatched agent or route. Tests in `service.test.ts`: "does not start for an agent on an SSH remote", "is true only for a local agent with retrieval on that the proxy compresses for".                                                            |
| Mode history and isolation                | Held              | Migration v22 adds `effective_mode` bare, with no backfill from `mode` or from today's setting; `proxyRequestFromLog()` stores the mode in force. Early mode without the tool uses the `complete` profile under its own key (`requestProfile`, `verdictKey`). Tests: "keeps early and full verdicts apart in the shared cache", "chooses the profile from the launch capability, never from early alone", `migration-v22.test.ts`.  |

The tool answers only the agent a run proof names, re-checks `agentHasTokenProxyRetrieval()` at call time, looks up one exact hash with no search or list, caps a range at 1,000 lines and 256 KiB, and counts its reads apart (`caller: 'mcpRetrieveTool'`). See the limit on exact hashes below.                                                      |
| Unsupported identity                      | Held              | Context-dependent previews refuse by named reason (`ReadUnsupportedReason`, `ReadPreviewUnsupportedReason`). Only `service.ts` imports the observer; it runs after the body is queued, and a hung observer changes nothing that is sent or answered (`server.test.ts`, `opportunity-observer.test.ts`).                                                                                                                             |
| Stats failure isolation                   | Held              | `recordTokenProxyRequest()` wraps the mapping and the write in try/catch with `captureException`; block counts that break the contract are reported and not stored. Test: "swallows a failed write and reports it, so the request is not failed by it".                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Dashboard and CLI agreement               | Held              | One aggregator (`getProxyEffectiveness()`), one reader (`readModelUsage()` serves the IPC handler and the WS handler behind `maestro-cli stats models`) and one vocabulary (`token-proxy-report.ts`, used by the CLI formatter, the Models section and the Gateway card). Each surface test derives its expected text from the same describers and `proxyTelemetryFixtures.ts`.                                                     |

Unsupported (U) and excluded (X) behavior, by name:

- **U, active replacement from read history** (deltas, skeletons, duplicate suppression). The proxy cannot prove tab, native session, subagent or window identity, so it refuses by reason: `incremental-history`, `managed-context`, `ambiguous-tool-id`, `baseline-unproven`, `baseline-replaced`.
- **U, recovery for an agent on an SSH remote**, and the retrieve tool for OpenCode (`agentHasTokenProxyRetrieval`, `localRecoveryRoute`; "Which launches get it" in `docs/local-models.md`).
- **U, failure evidence where the client states none**: Chat Completions tool messages, and a Codex result whose first lines are another spelling. The status is unknown, so the result stays eligible.
- **U, dollar savings and provider-cache effect** without matching token classes (`ProxyFigure`, provider reportedness).
- **X**: the shell recovery hint (03.4b3b and 03.4b3c, superseded by the retrieve tool and never emitted), a CSV reducer, custom filters, keep-warm requests, automatic model switching, edits to instructions or memory, a quality grade.

### Unresolved limits

- **Header evidence is for Responses requests and these spellings.** The `Exit code: N` form was not seen in the local rollouts (0 of 6,239); it rests on the recorded fixture. A new Codex spelling reads as unknown (the result is compressed with its diagnostic lines kept) until a pattern is added, and nothing probes for drift.
- **Exact hashes are capabilities.** By design (architecture doc, "Retrieval: the model asks for an original") any agent with retrieval on and a valid run proof can read the original of a block whose hash it holds. Hashes cannot be guessed but do appear in markers and transcripts. No per-agent ownership table was added; the design chose not to keep that state.
- **One unguarded hook.** `createTokenProxyServer` calls `options.onRequest` without a guard in its close handler. Isolation rests on `recordTokenProxyRequest()` catching its own failures (tested) and `logRequest()` only formatting and logging. Not changed here.
- **Evidence, not outcomes.** Every number above is a test, fixture or local-rollout count. None is a measured token saving, a provider cost or a task-quality result; the earlier real-provider smokes prove protocol behavior only.
- **Still open in phase 08.** The stable full suite and `npm run build` (08.2), the final documentation and count reconciliation, and the push (08.3). The `sqlite-vec-linux-arm64` clean-install limitation is unchanged. The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

The gate ran from a clean environment (`env -i` with HOME, USER, LANG and a short PATH; no `MAESTRO_*` or `CLAUDE_*` variables) and a fresh 0700 `/tmp/x.XXXX` TMPDIR. The driver, every log and the JSON of both runs are in `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/08.2/` (`run-gate.sh`, `run1/`). LSP was a deferred tool: `documentSymbol` answered for `agentQualification.ts`, but for the new loopback test it returned a snapshot from before its last edits, and the tool has no diagnostics operation. No repository tsconfig includes `src/__tests__`, so the two touched test files were type-checked with a scratch `tsc` project (`tsconfig.tests.json`, exit 0).

### Added in this task

- **End-to-end loopback test**, `src/__tests__/main/local-models/proxy/token-proxy-loopback-e2e.test.ts` (`7327455bda`, 5 cases). Real: the product service (`configureTokenProxy`, `ensureTokenProxyPort`, `registerTokenProxyRecoveryLaunch`) listening on a loopback port, `createTokenProxyServer`, the default compressor and `getCompressionCache()` on a temporary data directory, `recordTokenProxyRequest()` into an in-memory SQLite built by the real migrations, and the app's own `handlePluginsListTools` and `handlePluginsCallTool` with `pluginToolRunIdentity` proofs. Stand-in: only the provider, a local HTTP server that records what reaches it. The compression cache is a process-wide singleton with a memory layer, so the file shares one data directory and each case names its own log. Cases:
  1. Only the tool result changes: putting the original back in place of the shortened text reproduces the bytes the client sent. The stored row names the mode in force (`compress`, effective `compress-early`), the block counts, the provider's usage (120 in, 4 out) and a smaller sent size.
  2. A repeated block goes out with the same bytes and `cache_hits: 1`, and the stored cache files keep their content hashes. The same text as a failed result goes out byte for byte (`blocks_vetoed_failed: 1`, `cache_hits: 0`), before any cache read.
  3. The tool returns the exact lines of the original with its header (`more lines follow`, then `this is the end of the original`). An unknown hash is a tool error. The service counts three `mcpRetrieveTool` reads (two recovered, one unavailable, returned bytes equal to the two ranges) and none as `otherReads`.
  4. The tool is offered and answered to nobody else: a valid proof of an agent with retrieval off, no proof, a proof nobody issued and a revoked proof are refused (`PLUGIN_DENIED` or `Unknown tool`), and turning the stored switch off removes the tool from the next list and refuses the next call. Only the one allowed call counted as a read.
  5. What the tool returned, sent back under an `mcp__maestro__` prefix, is not shortened (`blocks_vetoed_retrieval: 1`), while the log before it is still shortened from the stored verdict (`cache_hits: 1`).
- **Signing fixture repair**, `src/__tests__/helpers/agentQualification.ts` (`ba13591544`). The throwaway repo's commit used the user's global git config, so a user who signs every commit (`commit.gpgsign=true`, gpg at `/usr/local/bin`) failed it on a PATH without gpg. `git()` now passes `-c commit.gpgsign=false`, the convention `remote-worktree-paths.test.ts` already uses. New case in `agent-qualification.test.ts`, "commits its scratch repo whatever commit signing the user has configured", points `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` at a config that signs with `/nonexistent/gpg`. Red before the change (`fatal: cannot exec '/nonexistent/gpg'`, `qualification-red.log`); green after, on the clean PATH, with 16 of 16 passing (`qualification-green.json`).

### Unresolved limits

- **Opt-in provider and model suites did not run here.** The real-provider retrieval, all-mode and Codex smokes recorded earlier are unchanged and were not repeated (no new paid workload). This gate proves the protocol path against a fake provider. It is not a measured saving, a provider cost or a task-quality result.
- **The CI legs have not run.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs its Ubuntu/Windows matrix on Node 22 for pushes to `main`/`rc` or pull requests to its listed targets. Pushing `acappella` does not trigger it. The verified gate here is macOS with Node 24; Ubuntu/Windows behavior remains unproven by this run. The loopback test and signing regression use Node-only APIs and temporary directories.
- **Build notices, all non-fatal and none from this feature.** Vite's CSS minifier notice (`Expected identifier but found "-"` in the generated `style.css:4367`, the malformed-CSS case the comment beside `cssMinify: 'esbuild'` in `vite.config.mts` describes; no CSS or Tailwind file changed in the feature commits), the externalized `fs` and `path` notices for `src/shared/maestro-lib/store/*` in the browser builds, four classic-script notices for `index.html`, and chunk-size notices. The build proves compilation and bundling, not a packaged-app start.
- **Environment for 08.3.** The gate used a short 0700 TMPDIR because the system temp directory is known to fail local proof-file and socket checks on this machine (not re-observed in this task). The pre-push hook (`.husky/pre-push`, `validate:push` through bun) repeats Prettier, lint, ESLint and this full suite, so the push needs the Node 24 PATH and a fresh short 0700 TMPDIR. It keeps the normal Git credential environment: the `env -i` gate environment is for the checks and must not be reused for a network push. The `sqlite-vec-linux-arm64` clean-install limitation is unchanged (`npm ci` was not part of this gate). The Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` was not touched.

- **Not implemented:** 03.4b3b (proof of the launch's tool shell) and 03.4b3c (end-to-end fixtures and emission of a shell recovery hint). The retrieval playbook (R02.1 to R02.7, merged in `4efc78e413`) replaced both on 2026-11-11. Neither is to be resumed.

### What the numbers mean

| Measure                     | What it is                                                                                                                                                                                          | What it is not                                                                                                                                              |
| --------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Estimated payload reduction | Characters divided by 4 on the request text the proxy read, marker included, before and after (`ProxyFigure` kind `estimate`). A shortened block counts again each time a later request resends it. | Not a provider token count and not a bill. The error runs both ways: the estimate matched the provider's count on one log and ran 1.9 times low on another. |
| Provider-reported usage     | Four classes (input, cache read, cache write, output) that the answer named or that Maestro derived, each with its own reportedness. Passthrough requests carry it too.                             | Not reduced by the estimate. An unreported class is unknown, never 0.                                                                                       |
| Cost                        | Not computed. No dollar figure is stored or shown. Verdict reuse (`cacheHits`) is Maestro's own cache and is never a provider cache read.                                                           | No net saving is claimed. The dated samples show cost moving either way with provider caching, retrieval and session history.                               |
| Task quality                | Not evaluated. `TOKEN_PROXY_RESULTS_NOT_EVALUATED_NOTE` says so wherever a saving is shown.                                                                                                         | Nothing here shows that answers stay as good. A paired trial needs separate authorization (analysis, "Evaluation protocol").                                |
| Opportunities and signals   | Previews of what a read could shrink to, repeated-call signals and the context inventory (kind `opportunity`).                                                                                      | Nothing was removed. They are never added to a saving, and `countsAsSavings` is the literal `false`.                                                        |

Comparing two date ranges, for example this week with last week, is not a causal saving: workload, model, cache state and session length differ between them.

### Remaining limits

- **No measured net saving or quality result.** The smokes prove protocol behavior with one session each. The dated cost samples are one sample per mode. Paired provider trials were not run and need separate authorization.
- **CI has not run, and will not on this push.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on pull requests to its listed branches and on pushes to `main` and `rc`. The Ubuntu and Windows legs therefore have not run for this work. By the rule in `CLAUDE.md` the branch is not mergeable until a pull request runs both. No pull request was opened.
- **Clean-install preflight.** `npm ci --dry-run --ignore-scripts` fails on a lock entry for the optional `sqlite-vec-linux-arm64` package (registry E404 for `@0.1.6`). The preflight fails the same way on the baseline and changed manifests, so it is not attributed to the new parser dependencies. A destructive clean install was not run in this checkout; no lock entry or upgrade was invented.
- **No active replacement from read history.** Deltas, skeletons and duplicate suppression stay previews, because the proxy cannot prove tab, session, subagent or window identity.
- **Codex failure headers.** Three spellings are recognized. A new spelling reads as unknown and is compressed with its diagnostic lines kept, until a pattern is added. Nothing probes for drift.
- **Exact hashes are capabilities.** An agent with Retrieval on and a valid run proof can read the original of any block whose hash it holds. No per-agent ownership table exists.
- **One unguarded hook.** `createTokenProxyServer` calls `options.onRequest` without a guard. Isolation rests on `recordTokenProxyRequest()` catching its own failures.
- **Retrieval reach.** Not available to OpenCode, to an agent on an SSH remote, or to an Auto Run task, Cue run, Group Chat, consult or `maestro-cli send` turn.
- **Not exercised.** A packaged-app start, the Electron ABI of `better-sqlite3` (untouched), and live account traffic beyond the three smokes. Component renders use synthetic fixtures.
- **Build notices.** Non-fatal, none from this feature (see 08.2).
- **Dormant code.** The shell-hint pieces listed above stay in the tree, tested and unused.

### Publication

`origin/acappella` was at `4efc78e413` (the retrieval merge) before publication. The push publishes the work already on this branch and the publication and documentation-repair commits recorded here. Phases 04 to 08 had not been pushed before. The branch also includes documentation audits and other work that already sat on it. Only the files named under "What this task changed" were staged. The untracked `.interceptor-research/` and `output/` were not touched.

Normal hooks, no bypass and no force. The commit ran the pre-commit hook (`lint-staged`) and has no co-author trailer. The push runs `.husky/pre-push`, which runs `validate:push` (`bun run format:check:all && bun run lint && bun run lint:eslint && bun run test`), with `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin:$PATH`, a fresh 0700 `TMPDIR` under `/tmp` and the normal Git credential environment. The push logs and exit codes are saved in `.maestro/scratch/token-completion-20261111/08.3/`: `push1.*` for the first attempt, which the hook refused (next section), and `push2.*` for the second. The result is accepted only when `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/acappella` equals `git rev-parse HEAD`. That hash is recorded on the 08.3 line of the playbook. A hook rejection is investigated and fixed, never bypassed.

### First publication attempt and documentation repair

The first normal pre-push hook refused publication: 84,068 tests passed, one documentation-surface guard failed and 212 were skipped. The 08.2 temporary-directory rationale named an internal-only feature. Removed that label from the rationale, keeping the guard unchanged with no exception. This is a documentation repair; executable code and test expectations are unchanged. The original failed push artifacts are preserved as `08.3/push1.log`, `push1.exit` and `push1.status` under the scratch evidence directory. Publication requires a fresh normal-hook push and an exact remote hash match.

After repair, `npm test -- src/__tests__/main/*/leak-guard.test.ts` exited 0: 15 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (`supervisor-doc-guard-green.log`). No guard expectation or exception changed.
